# Host tests

The host tests compile kernel and user sources as ordinary programs for the build machine and run them under AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer. A test drives the real code against models of what surrounds it (a device's registers, a disk, a scheduler, a peer on the network), so a bounds fault, an overflow or a wrong branch shows in a few seconds on the host rather than as a fault inside QEMU. What needs the machine itself, a system call through the trap path, a privilege change, a real device or a whole subsystem at once, is tested by the guest suites of [`tests/system/`](../system/Readme.md). This directory has the `*_hosttest.c` programs, the headers, models and fixture builders they share, and the scripts that check kernel functions no host test can compile whole.

## Running

`itco_hosttest.c` drives the production TCO register and file operations against a register model. It checks the reserved bits, start and halt write order, both expiry stages and reload, magic and ordinary close, the Linux ioctls, invalid arguments and user pointers, absent registers, a locked NO_REBOOT refusing the device, and refused timer and halt writes. Other rows run the kernel's feed of a timer it starts at boot, `rach2.itco.arm`, on a tick of 5 a second: no reset in a minute of feeds every half heartbeat, a reset two expiries after the tick stops, as after a panic, the feed ended by an open and by `watchdog.open_timeout`, and none with `watchdog.handle_boot_enabled` off; with watchdogd, the tick leaving each feed to `itco_feed_work`, a reset two expiries after the thread stops though the tick goes on, as after a hang with interrupts on, and no feed left due across an open; `iTCO_wdt.nowayout` refusing DISABLECARD with `EBUSY` and leaving a magic close running; a heartbeat out of range taking 30 s; and a timer that firmware left counting kept running and fed, with NO_REBOOT clear and with it set, which the probe clears. The last rows link `cmdline.c` and read the options from a command line: a bare `iTCO_wdt.heartbeat` and an `iTCO_wdt.nowayout` whose value is no boolean are each refused with a `refused:` record and leave the option as it was, as Linux's `parse_args` refuses and logs them, and the parser's `kcmdline_bare_count` and `kcmdline_parse_bool` count only whole bare tokens and read a boolean as Linux's `kstrtobool` does. Hardware time is advanced in ticks, without a host clock.

`i915_pci_hosttest.c` binds the Intel graphics driver to the host model of the Lenovo's HD 4400 through the driver's hardware boundary, and probes a bus that holds the chip. It checks Linux 6.12's Haswell GT2 ids, the boot option that gates binding, `nomodeset` and `i915.modeset=0`, which keep the driver off, and `i915.modeset=1`, which binds it under `nomodeset`, BAR 0's registers mapped uncached and the GTT's entries write-combining, every interrupt source masked and cleared before the message is enabled and before the driver's base comes up, the configuration words read, the OpRegion and the boot display's ROM sought and, with neither mapped, Linux's default outputs taken, the DRM device's identity and features, the refusals, a failed registration, a bind whose GTT gets no scratch page, which lets the display power well's request go, leaves the command word as found and maps no window of the aperture, the unplug, and the boundary's bounded waits, timed by the model's clock.

`i915_base_hosttest.c` brings up the Intel driver's base on the same model, whose forcewake, GT FIFO and power well blocks a row slows or stops. It checks forcewake's handshake and reference count, a slow acknowledgement waited for and a stuck one failing by name at 50 ms, the GT FIFO's reserve; the display power well taken over from the BIOS, turned off, and kept on while the firmware's pipe needs it; stolen memory reserved from a boot map that offers it, found with the Lenovo's values and refused when the allocator owns a frame of it or it lies outside the GPU's RMRR; and the global GTT's sizes, its entry format, the firmware's 1032 framebuffer entries left as they were, every other entry on the scratch page, and insert and clear with their refusals; and a clear of 1,537 entries giving way after each 512 but the last, three times, with the GEM lock held and `may_yield` set, flushing the GTT once, and giving no way for a notifier's clear or without the lock (`row_ggtt_clear_yield`).

`i915_ring_hosttest.c` starts the Intel driver's four rings on the same model, whose rings run the driver's commands from memory the test hands out, a page at a time with physical addresses spread apart, and whose interrupt registers raise the device's message, which the test takes by calling the driver's handler while its processor's interrupts are on. It checks the rings' status pages and buffers mapped at the GTT's top, Linux's stop and start order, `RING_VALID`, and every interrupt source masked after the bind but the master enable and the rings' user interrupts; a ring that will not stop, a stop forgiven on an empty ring, a dead ring failing by name at 5 ms, and a TLB flush that never ends; the handler table's refusals and each bank's sources acknowledged under the master enable, the south display's behind the PCH event, spurious and unclaimed bits; breadcrumbs, waits woken by the user interrupt and ended by polling, a stuck ring's timeouts, the ring's wrap, a full ring and a command that crosses its end; and first light end to end and in each of its failures.

`i915_ctx_hosttest.c` runs the Intel driver's contexts, requests and parameters on the same model, whose render ring also takes `MI_SET_CONTEXT`, saving a few registers and a marker into the image it leaves and restoring them from the one it loads, and faulting on an image with nothing to restore. It checks the 17-page image mapped at the LLC's level, the default state recorded with its restore inhibited and Linux's context workarounds in it, the switch's flags and the commands around it, a context's registers carried across a switch and back, and an image kept until the switch away retires; `CONTEXT_CREATE` with its extensions, `DESTROY`, `GETPARAM` and `SETPARAM` with each refusal Linux 6.18 gives on Haswell, and engine maps with their extensions, and an engine map whose copy sleeps under the file's context lock, for which another thread's destroy, first use or map of the context waits; requests' sequence numbers and breadcrumbs on both rings, fences signalled from the interrupt, retirement and the GT parked, kept awake by a callback asked for once the breadcrumb is written and parked by a retirement before the interrupt of a request listened for while it ran, its breadcrumb between the retirement's two passes among them, waits ended by their timeouts, a lost interrupt and polling, refused commands taken back, a wedged GPU and the unplug; `GETPARAM` against Linux's answers row by row, each `I915_QUERY` item, `GET_RESET_STATS`, and `REG_READ` of the render ring's timestamp, which the model moves on at each read, under forcewake, whole across a carry with `I915_REG_READ_8B_WA`, with each of Linux's refusals; THROTTLE, which waits for the file's requests older than 20 ms and not for younger ones or another file's, and EIO on a wedged GPU.

`i915_ppgtt_hosttest.c` makes the Intel driver's aliasing PPGTT on the same model, whose rings walk it from the directory `PP_DIR_BASE` names while `RING_MODE_GEN7`'s PPGTT bit is set, and run a batch's stores through it when the batch's start asks for it. It checks the directory's 2 MiB in the GT's space at a 64 KiB boundary with every entry naming the scratch table, each ring's `PP_DIR_DCLV`, `PP_DIR_BASE` and PPGTT bit written between its start and `RING_VALID`, and the PPGTT's caching; a binding mirrored across a 4 MiB boundary, its two tables made and named, its entries the global GTT's, cleared to the scratch page with the tables kept, the refusals and a table with no memory; a batch's stores on the render and blitter rings reaching the pages the global GTT maps, while the status page and a range cleared from the PPGTT alone are out of reach; and after a reset of both rings, the registers written again and a batch run through the PPGTT.

`i915_reset_hosttest.c` hangs the Intel driver's rings on the same model, whose `GEN6_GDRST` puts the rings of each domain it is written for back to their power-on state, which ends a ring the row holds stuck, and reads clear, unless the row holds the domain stuck too; the render domain's reset also puts the registers of the render engine's workarounds back to their power-on values, and the whole GPU's those of the GT's. The heartbeat runs after each 10 ms of the model's clock, as the driver's worker would run it. It checks a stuck request found between Linux's 5 and 7.5 s, by the heartbeat and by a wait for it, and a busy engine that completes its work never found; the render domain reset twice, the guilty request's payload skipped and its fence failed with `EIO`, the ring started again at it with its interrupt unmasked, the request behind it run, the blitter untouched, and the engine's reset counted but not the GPU's; the error state with its message, the guilty program, the registers read before they are cleared, the requests on the chip and the ring's dwords, the first state kept until it is cleared; a domain that sticks reset with the whole GPU, the fences restored, the other ring's request run again innocent with `EAGAIN` and not reset again for its stale beat; the GT's and the render engine's workarounds standing after the bind, with the line that counts them, the engine's after its reset and both after the whole GPU's, and one the chip does not keep logged and counted; a GPU that does not reset wedged, every request failed and no context made, a later reset that ends the wedge and the unplug's that none ends, and a wedge with a request that waits for another driver's fence, whose commands and switch are taken back so that the reset runs none of them; a request held back by another driver's fence that never signals, which runs after 10 s with ETIMEDOUT; and the bans: an unbannable context never banned, the third hang within 120 s banning a context, its later requests failed, a context that is not recoverable banned at its first, a closed one banned with its file not scored, a file refused new contexts at a score of 9, and `GET_RESET_STATS` counting each; the heartbeat with `i915.enable_hangcheck` off: a beat that resets nothing and stops every engine's heartbeat, an engine kept busy all the while left without one once hangcheck is on again, and another engine's heartbeat back as it goes busy, armed by the beat after and its stuck request reset between Linux's 5 and 7.5 s with the render engine untouched; turning hangcheck on disarming a busy engine and the beat after arming it again without a reset, and a beat that finds hangcheck off still resetting an engine that missed its heartbeat before it stops them all; and an engine that goes busy while another keeps the GT awake, armed by no beat within an interval of going busy and its stuck request reset between Linux's 5 and 7.5 s.
`i915_power_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's RC6 and frequency control on the same model, whose RC6 block counts residency while the GT is idle and answers the pcode mailbox, and whose frequency block runs the GT at the request within the chip's limits. It checks Linux's RC6 thresholds and the park and unpark, 10 s of idle read as 10000 ms of residency, the counter's wrap and rounding; the mailbox's answers, errors, a busy box and a stuck one failing by name at 20.5 ms; RPn, RP1, RP0 and RPe from the Lenovo's values, the interrupt thresholds and the ring's frequency table; the up and down interrupts' steps, park and unpark, and 400 random events with every request inside the soft limits; and the `/sys` files, their values and their refusals.

`i915_display_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's display on the same model, whose pipe block holds the firmware's picture on pipe A as the Lenovo boots, latches the planes' registers at each vblank, counts frames, raises the vblank and flip done events and computes a CRC of the planes a source sees; it includes `drm_kms_model.c` and the display's files and runs them through the DRM core's ioctls, taking each message by calling the driver's handler, with a wait that runs frames as a sleep would. It checks the readout of pipe A against Linux's `i915_display_info`; the adoption, which writes no register of the pipe, its link or the backlight, and the eDP connector's probe; the console's takeover, a fastset that pins its buffer at 256 KiB and lets the firmware's framebuffer go after the next flip, with a refresh rate the panel lacks refused, and its watermarks; the planes' formats and their checks of stride, tiling, format and coverage, an X-tiled corner split between surface and offset, and the sprite's registers and watermarks; a flip's event at the vblank that latches it with its count and a later stamp, and an async flip's at flip done; the vblanks the frame counter kept while the interrupt was off; the cursor's registers; the backlight taken over from the boot's driver, set, switched and off with the pipe; the pipe CRC's sources, the fitter forced through while one runs, its first frame dropped, and its lines; a smaller mode set through the fitter in Linux's register order, for each scaling mode; and a link that fails to train, with its status and hotplug event.

`i915_edp_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's eDP output on the same model, whose sink block, `i915_model_sink.c`, is the Lenovo's panel behind DDI A: its AUX channel with the panel's DPCD, its EDID over I2C and its link training, which needs the swing and pre-emphasis the sink asks for, and the panel power sequencer, with faults a row asks for: timeouts, receive errors, native and I2C DEFER replies and empty replies. It checks the AUX channel's messages and retries, VDD forced on and let go, the EDID's read, the link's training and fallback in Linux's order, the output's init, a mode's link and M/N as Linux computes them, the fitter's windows and limits, and the sequencer's delays and power cycle.

`i915_kms_trial_hosttest.c` runs the path of the display trial on the Lenovo on the same model, with the straps reporting DDI B and C and no monitor: the adoption of pipe A without a write to the pipe, its link or the backlight, and the outputs probed with no hotplug event; the modes the trial's CRC tool sets, the panel's own and 1280x720, 1024x768, 800x600 and 640x480 under each scaling mode, set by SETPROPERTY and SETCRTC with the panel's timings, each fitter window against Linux's `pch_panel_fitting` written out in the test and three CRCs of `auto` read through `crc/data`; pipe A's FIFO underruns unreported after the adoption and reported after the first mode set, one underrun logged once and then masked, and a mode set reporting them again; the backlight's levels 1, 468, 937 and 0 at Linux's duty cycles from `scale_user_to_hw`, 0 turning the enable off; IGT's kms_hdmi_inject as it runs there, the panel's pipe off and HDMI-A-1 forced on with a 4K EDID set on pipe A through DDI B, with limited range by the matrix after the gamma and no second M/N on transcoder A; and ten seconds of frames with no hotplug pulse or event. Each row also finds no wait at its bound in the log.

`i915_hdmi_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's outputs on DDI B to D on the same model with its straps reporting DDI B and C, whose DDI block, `i915_model_ddi.c`, holds a monitor's DDC on each GMBUS pin, answered by the GMBUS controller, which a row may hang, and by an I2C target that follows the GPIO pins' lines; a DisplayPort sink behind each port's AUX channel that trains on the patterns the port sends; and the PCH's hotplug detection, which a row pulses. It checks the ports the VBT and the straps give; an EDID of two blocks read over GMBUS, and again by bit-banging after a GMBUS transfer timed out; with the scheduler running, the hung transfer's wait sleeping after its spin with the GMBUS mutex held, ending at its deadline on the monotonic clock though each sleep of a millisecond lasts two ticks, and leaving the mutex free; the WRPLL's dividers and frequency for a table of clocks against Linux's hsw_ddi_calculate_wrpll, its special reference on a ULT part and on another with FUSE_STRAP3's 24 MHz, and the SPLL's clocks; an HDMI monitor found by its long pulse and set at 1920x1080 on pipe B with Linux's WRPLL, the VBT's buffer translation, the AVI, SPD and GCP infoframes, limited range, kept by the plane off and on, the power well held for the pipe and its plane's watermark, and its dot clock read back at 6 and 10 bits a colour; hotplug's connect and disconnect events, a pulse that changed nothing probed again 1 s later, and a storm that masks the pin, which is polled until it comes back 2 minutes later; a DisplayPort monitor on C trained on four lanes at 1.62 Gb/s and shown on pipe C; and IGT's kms_hdmi_inject, a connector with no monitor forced on with a 4K EDID override and set at 3840x2160@30 from the second WRPLL.

`i915_trial_hosttest.c` runs the path of the driver's first trial on the Lenovo whole: the bind under `rach2.i915`, without and with `rach2.i915.firstlight`, and with `rach2.i915.disable_display`, which must neither make the display nor remove the firmware's framebuffer device, the processor's interrupts on and off, through a boundary of the test's that notes each register and GTT entry the driver writes, on the same model as the Lenovo's firmware leaves the chip, from gfxtool's dump. It checks that no wait reached its bound; that nothing was written outside the GT but the interrupt registers, the GTT's flush, the power well's takeover and the display arbiter's tile swizzle, and in the GT nothing but forcewake, the render and blitter rings, the fence registers, which the bind clears, the swizzle of tile control and ARB_MODE, and the GT's and the render engine's workarounds with the message channel's register; that the firmware's 1032 framebuffer entries, the plane, the pipe and the eDP transcoder are as the firmware left them; that the power well was never released; that the chip reached no memory but the driver's pages; that after the bind every interrupt source is masked and the unclaimed access the firmware leaves latched is cleared; and that the bind decodes the VBT of the Lenovo's OpRegion, which stands at ASLS from its fixture, and the release unmaps it.

`i915_handoff_hosttest.c` runs the path of the Lenovo's GPU assignment whole: the driver's release of the HD 4400 through `/sys/bus/pci/drivers/i915/unbind` and its bring-up again through `bind`, on the same model. The whole driver stands on the model, the bind of `i915_pci.c` and the display's files together, and the test supplies the kernel around it: the entries of /sys, the PCI function's owner, command word, message and reset, the DRM core's devices, the console's client and the boot's backlight driver. The function's reset is the test's, and clears what the reference run on the Lenovo showed cleared or what a reset clears by rule: the display engine's and the PCH's registers but for the straps, the raw clock and the PWM's granularity, the interrupts, the message, bus mastering, the power well, the link and the panel's power with the sequencer's count of its power cycle; the GTT's entries stay. The rows check the release: the backlight, the transcoder, the DDI and the panel's power off in that order, then the GPU reset before the rings' pages go back, every interrupt masked, the message and bus mastering off, no owner, the console told that the loader's framebuffer has gone before the device leaves, `intel_backlight` on a stand-in that writes no register, and a record that a program still holds reading all ones with no access reaching the chip. They check the refusals: a release while a program holds a window of the aperture, or while the GPU will not reset, which leaves the driver bound and the GPU wedged; a bring-up while `vfio-pci` owns the function, while its `driver_override` names another driver, or when the function's reset fails. And they check the bring-up from a function left as a guest's driver leaves it: one reset before any access, no access until the reset's 100 ms and the panel's power cycle have passed, nothing adopted, none of the guest's GTT entries left, the PWM's period from the VBT, and a full mode set of the panel by SETCRTC with the link trained and ten frames counted, the whole of it twice, with no wait at its bound and every allocation, page and vector given back.

`i915_gem_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's buffers over the real GEM, PRIME, dma-buf and fence code, on two devices of the chip of `i915_gem_model.c`, whose GTT entries and fence registers are arrays and whose aperture is memory that holds what a program's GTT map or the driver's window wrote until the driver syncs it, and that refills from memory when an entry or a fence changes, so that a sync the driver leaves out loses bytes. It checks GEM_CREATE's rounding and refusals, GEM_CREATE_EXT's flags and each of its extensions with Linux's refusals, PREAD and PWRITE through the processor's view and an import's through the aperture, the cache domains of SET_DOMAIN and SW_FINISH, SET_CACHING and GET_CACHING as on an LLC part; MMAP_OFFSET's kinds and refusals and GEM_MMAP_GTT on its number, the legacy GEM_MMAP's refusals and a map from the object's second page, write-back and write-combining, write-back and GTT maps faulted, written through one and read through the other, a GTT map revoked when its binding goes, an object past the aperture faulted through 1 MiB views, an object busy above the aperture faulted through a partial view of the whole of it without a wait, failed binds running the access again, ptrace's peeks and pokes through a GTT and a write-back map, and six imports of 2 MiB written through four windows; the driver's tiled_offset against Linux's selftest's for X and Y, every swizzle and seven strides, the swizzle read from the DIMMs of the channels, SET_TILING's refusals and GET_TILING, a tiled object written through a GTT map found in memory where X tiling and swizzle 9/10 put each dword, a change of tiling revoking the map and rewriting the fence, and the 33rd tiled map taking the least recently used fence; BUSY's engine bits and WAIT's timeouts on the driver's fences, and WAIT on a timeline in the reads whose middle point completes at any of the driver's looks at it, which still times out on the oldest; PRIME's self-import and another device's import, a dma-buf mapped write-back and its CPU access sync; the ioctl table; a fault whose binds fail; and the unplug's revocation of every kind of map.

`i915_exec_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's EXECBUFFER2 with its contexts, requests, syncobjs and sync files on the chip of `i915_gem_model.c`, whose rings run what the driver gives them as each tail moves, the breadcrumbs and the batches' starts, and of a batch its `MI_STORE_DATA_IMM`, so that a row sees where a relocated address points; the user interrupt is taken when a row asks. It checks an LRI of 128 register writes followed by an interrupt, so that the model must read all eight length bits and hold the whole command; each refusal of the call as Linux 6.18 gives it; relocations written with the target's offset and the signed delta when the presumed offset is wrong and left when it is right, the offsets given back, `NO_RELOC` skipping them until an object moves, `HANDLE_LUT` and `BATCH_FIRST`, the batch with relocations above 256 KiB; objects pinned at an offset, taking it from an idle binding, padded and aligned; the batch started unprivileged on the render and blitter rings at its binding plus its start, the request's fence on the objects until it retires, and the stream output offsets zeroed; the in-fence holding the batch, its error skipping it, the submit fence holding it behind the other ring's request, the out-fence's sync file and the fence array's syncobjs; the timeline fences, a point signalled added as the request's fence and a point waited for holding the batch, with Linux's refusals; each of the driver's ioctls that Mesa's Crocus and hasvk call, with Linux 6.12's number and a row a render node may call, and the sync file an image's dma-buf exports for the batch that wrote it and the one it imports to hold the next; a batch with a length run from the parser's shadow, privileged, and then its trampoline, unprivileged, whoever calls and on both rings, a refused one chaining from the trampoline to the original, which runs, a command of no known client refused with EINVAL, and `SECURE`; the shadow's pages and a batch closed while it runs held until the request retires; a batch and its target closed while the request runs, which keep their bindings, refuse to be unbound, and go once it retires; a batch whose breadcrumb is written before its interrupt is taken, which WAIT, BUSY and a query find done without asking for the signal, the GT left awake; a ring that refuses the commands; and the heap's blocks a call shaped as Mesa's Crocus makes it takes, at most 4 on an idle ring and 7 on a busy one, whose target and buffer then hold one fence of the ring each, the newest.

`i915_exec_hosttest.c` also holds a request incomplete through the next call's creation and completes it at that call's ring-tail write, requiring retirement before the call returns. Its timeout row leaves a busy GEM mutex untouched with a next-tick retry, then attempts a submitter's mutex take after expiry makes a request runnable and requires it to remain blocked while the scan reads its wait record. After the scan, the next submission runs and retires the expired request. These interleavings use the model's single-thread scheduling hooks.

`i915_parser_hosttest.c` runs the Intel driver's command parser on batches in pages of the test's, each its own allocation, and reads each verdict from the shadow: copied whole with the trampoline's end, so it runs privileged, or ended at once with the trampoline's start of the original, so the original runs unprivileged, or the call's error. It checks Linux 6.12's tables sorted, every entry of the render and blitter rings' tables judged by its action, every register of the allowlists written and read on its ring and refused on the other, the masked registers, each masked check of a command, the length fields of commands no table holds by client, a client the ring does not run, a command past the batch and a batch without its end refused with EINVAL, the copy across pages and its flush, a refused mapping, and every case of IGT's gen7_exec_parse that Linux's parser at version 10 runs, each with Linux's verdict.

`i915_video_hosttest.c` starts the video ring, vcs0, and the video enhancement ring, vecs0, on the same model and checks each started as Linux's xcs_resume starts it, with Linux's name, class, engine id, reset domain and fault register; the video ring's user interrupt in the GT's bank and the other's in power management's, a breadcrumb's wait on each woken by its own interrupt with the ring's mask opened before the bank's, and a reset of each domain reaching its ring alone. It then runs the command parser on both rings as `i915_parser_hosttest.c` does: Linux 6.12's gen7_video and hsw_vebox tables sorted and every entry judged by its action, every register refused since neither ring has an allowlist, the masked checks of MI_FLUSH_DW, MI_STORE_DWORD_IMM and MI_CONDITIONAL_BATCH_BUFFER_END, MFX_WAIT taken as one dword on the video ring and by its length field on the other, the lengths of Linux's gen7_bsd_get_cmd_length_mask, and gen7_exec_parse's basic-rejected on both rings.

`i915_evict_hosttest.c` drives the Intel driver's eviction, aperture accounting, purgeable buffers and userptr objects on the same chip, whose memory has a limit a row sets and whose reclaim calls the shrinkers the driver registers. It checks 48 objects of 1 MiB written through GTT maps of the 16 MiB aperture, the 17th taking the place of the least recently used, and every page read back through rebound maps and PREAD; a pinned binding and a busy one never evicted by a bind that may not wait, which ENOSPC refuses, a GTT fault whose wait for the GPU a signal ends, and one that may wait waiting for the GPU and evicting the busy one once its request retires; 80 MiB bound in turn in the 64 MiB GTT around a reserved page, each eviction's clear allowed to give way, as its caller may sleep; GET_APERTURE's sizes with pins, reservations, kept surfaces and the GT's own objects; MADVISE's refusals, its states and an object without pages purged at once; a DONTNEED object, bound and mapped, purged by reclaim for an allocation, its maps revoked, while a WILLNEED, a busy and a pinned one stay, and an allocation past memory refused with ENOMEM; a second pin of an object's pages while the first's reclaim sleeps counting one more on the first's pages, and an object purged during that sleep given none; and GEM_USERPTR's refusals as Linux 6.12's, a size past what an object holds among them, the calls it refuses on a userptr object, and its pages pinned by SET_DOMAIN and a bind and let go, the bindings revoked first, on an unmap, whose clear from the notifier gives no way, an mprotect under a pin, which holds them until the last pin, a fork, a change during the pin, a second pin while the first sleeps, a change while the GPU uses the object, which keeps its binding and frames until the next use waits for the GPU, a copy-on-write break and the end of the address space, and refused to another address space; and an object's free giving way wherever the GEM lock's holder may: a GEM_CLOSE with the lock free clearing and freeing its pages giving way, a last put under a held lock left on the device's list until the holder that took the lock lets it go and frees it giving way, one under the shrinker freed at once with no give-way and its frames counted, the shrinker, under its caller's lock and in a wait's drop of it, freeing the list the same way and counting its frames, and a bound object freed with another device's fence unsignalled on its reservation taking its binding out of the GTT; and an import closed under a held lock, its last reference gone and another device's fence unsignalled on its reservation, evicted at a fixed offset and by scan with no wait for the fence or the GPU; and a bound object whose handle, its last reference but eviction's, is closed while eviction at a fixed offset waits for its fence with the GEM lock let go, kept whole through that close and a free of the deferred list, and left on that list by eviction's put once the lock is retaken.

`intel_bios_hosttest.c` decodes the Lenovo's OpRegion and video BIOS table, `tests/fixtures/lenovo-gpu/i915_opregion.bin` and `i915_vbt.bin`, which stand at the Lenovo's ASLS behind a `mmio_map` of the test's. It checks the OpRegion's version, mailboxes and the driver's writes to them, the VBT of mailbox 4, a bad signature, RVDA on version 2.0 and 2.1, and mailbox 2 on version 3; the outputs Linux made of the table, eDP on DDI A, HDMI on DDI B and DP with HDMI on DDI C; the panel's index, 18-bit link, power sequence, which gives the timings of Linux's `eDP-1-i915_panel_timings`, mode, DRRS and backlight; each block padded to the size of Linux's structure for it; the panel's index from the EDID's product id and from the fallback, and refused when out of range; malformed and missing LFP data pointers; Linux's defaults without a table; and the VBT of an option ROM.

`etude prepare collection host` builds the programs and immutable input inventory of [`tests/cases/host.host.json`](../cases/host.host.json). `etude run host` executes that prepared suite through the native C driver [`tests/cases/host/host.c`](../cases/host/host.c). The collection holds the tree while its checks run. No compiler, interpreter or Linux tooling machine runs on the execution host. [The driver's Readme](../cases/host/Readme.md) defines preparation, execution and unsupported adapters.

The cases keep their assertion identifiers:

1. `tests`: the host tests outside etude, with the vendored sources of each selected architecture.
2. `etude`: the shared checks of Etude's complete inventory, once per collection.
3. `etude_quiet`: the platform, remote and repetition checks of that same inventory, with no other collection case running beside them.
4. `boots`: tests with `host-arch:`, over each selected architecture's kernel and images.
5. `checkers`: the five checker selftests under the sanitizers.
6. `fuzz`: each prepared fuzz harness replaying its complete committed corpus. An empty corpus fails preparation.
7. `scripts_native`, `scripts_images`, `scripts_build`, `scripts` and `scripts_tools`: disjoint partitions of all 46 behavioral assertions of `host.list`, preserving each assertion and its position relative to the other rows of its partition. A row awaiting an adapter fails explicitly; it is never a preparation-time pass.

The build-only inventory builder reads source metadata, compiles the checks and hashes compiler dependencies, including locally included `.c` files. Its `--print-commands` option prints commands without compiling or executing. `tools/dev/gen_compile_commands.sh` and `tools/verify/coverage.sh` read the commands it prints.

The `icmp_hosttest.c` model sends pings with timer interrupts still pending at each attempt. It checks the deadline before and at its expiry, a count past 2^32 ticks, release of the deadline, and a reply, carrying the call's own echo identifier, received before the timer handler catches up. Its fragmentation-needed rows quote a TCP segment: a sequence past what was sent, one acknowledged, another connection's and a quote too short for the sequence leave the path MTU as it was, and only a sequence in flight lowers it and reaches TCP.

The `genetlink_hosttest.c` model registers a family against the real generic dispatcher and netlink queue, resolves it through `nlctrl`, checks its operation and group nests, reads a dump in three datagrams, joins and leaves groups including group 40, and fills one reader's queue while another keeps receiving. It also checks privilege refusal, capped and extended errors, malformed and unaligned attributes, the dormant/up link policy and route notifications, and a link's counts past 2^32, whole in `IFLA_STATS64` and cut to 32 bits in `IFLA_STATS`. The model supplies one Ethernet card and empty address, route and socket tables.

`kvm_irq_hosttest.c` checks the full interrupt controller through port and MMIO exits, GSI and state ioctls, and the real eventfd callbacks. Its full-controller rows cover allocation rollback, PIC priority and cascade, level EOI, RTC acknowledgement after state restore, irqfd resampling with independent line sources, PIT latches and reinjection, HPET handoff, halted wakeups and routing replacement. `--irqchip-only` runs these rows alone. Its other rows give the halted vCPU's wait a model of `wq_wait_event_sigflag` that ends on the caller's signal-work flag alone and counts a wait nothing would end, and check the EOI exit of a split controller's level route after each vCPU took the routes' KVM_REQ_SCAN_IOAPIC, for the vCPU the route targets, another, and one with the vector in service at the scan.

`synthfs_hosttest.c --thp-only` and `procfs_hosttest.c --thp-only` run their transparent-hugepage rows alone, `sched_hosttest.c --affinity-only` its remote affinity rows, and `kvm_slots_hosttest.c --grace-only` its rows of two replacements of the KVM read side at once; each set also runs with its program's other rows.

## Results

`drm_vblank_hosttest.c` and `drm_vblank64_hosttest.c` also queue two planes with independent engine fences. They check that only the current plane enables signalling and reaches the driver's wait hook, both in the worker and outside the signaller's modeled interrupt locks; a second engine that finishes first is left unlistened until its completion is read; a pending second fence gets a full timeout of its own, including when the worker resumes late after a timely first completion; completion during callback setup leaves no wait; and a timeout prevents later listening or boosting, whether the first fence signals before or after the worker runs.

`vdso32_hosttest.c` checks the fast i386 entry with a modeled user copy, with the cycle account off and on. Both sysenter and syscall32 must read the clock, when the account is on, before the copy of the saved EBP and copy before the call's entry; a failed copy answers EFAULT, pushes a frame record of no lock and counts the call once, as a successful entry does. `vm_hosttest.c` checks a fallback mapping across the mmap base and the 256-page gap below a growing stack.

`drm_prime_hosttest.c` and `drm_prime64_hosttest.c` check PRIME on both kernel ABIs over the real GEM, dma-buf, fence and udmabuf code. They model frames, sealed memfds, descriptor tables and mappings. The cases cover descriptor modes, self-import and repeated import identity, cross-device and udmabuf pages, shared reservations, reexport after handle close, exporter exit, mappings retained after every descriptor closes, allocation failures and all reference counts returned to their baseline. They also include vgem: its fences on a buffer another device imports, held until signalled, Linux's refusals, the timeout, which a model of the commit worker's queue runs, and the close of a file with fences pending.

`rc_hosttest.c` checks the wireless rate controller with recorded retry completions and modeled clean, lossy and recovered links. It also checks per-series ACK attribution, capability intersections and reset on a peer change, without files or child processes.

The driver prints one result, assertion and progress record per completed check, and the assertion `listed` for agreement between the manifest, source tree and prepared inventory. A source check missing from the manifest is not executed and fails `listed`; a declared check missing from the inventory fails its assertion and `listed`.

A check passes only after exit status zero, confirmed process cleanup and unchanged binary and input hashes. Every sanitizer is fatal. Each check has private scratch under the driver's `rach2-host.XXXXXX` directory in `TMPDIR`; complete output goes to its `NAME.log`. Failed evidence is kept and its directory printed. Successful runs remove their scratch. A stopped driver cancels and reaps its children before removing scratch; unconfirmed cleanup retains it. The case has no time limit of its own. Each completed check writes `progress checks N` for etude's watchdog.

`sched_hosttest.c` also checks slice events with only lower-priority work, a single selection request at expiry and a competitor becoming ineligible before selection.

The wireless station and ath9k fixtures whose literal clocks count milliseconds, and `procfs_hosttest.c` and `unix_hosttest.c`, whose times and time limits count whole hundredths of a second and milliseconds in ticks, declare `TICK_HZ=1000`; production uses 250 Hz. `hda_hosttest.c` and `ps2kbd_hosttest.c` declare it too, and `hda250_hosttest.c` and `ps2kbd250_hosttest.c` run their rows at 250 Hz. `i915_base_hosttest.c` selects its device's register lock in the shared time stub, which traps if a service deadline is published while that lock is held. The forcewake row also checks the published timestamp and its independent two-millisecond deadline.

## Design

### The compile

Each test is one compile and one program:

    cc -std=c99 -D_DEFAULT_SOURCE -D_FORTIFY_SOURCE=0 -DRACH2_HOSTTEST \
       -fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all -g -O1 \
       -Wno-empty-body -ffreestanding INCLUDES CFLAGS -o OUT TEST DEPS

`CC` in the environment replaces `cc`. Every group has `-I` paths for `utils`, `krffs` and `tests/host/compat`, in that order. The `tests` group also has `-iquote` paths for `rach2/sys`, `rach2/sys/arch/x86`, `rach2/sys/arch/i686`, `rach2/sys/lib` and `rach2/sys/proc`, before those shared paths. These kernel directories apply only to quoted includes, so a system header such as `<pthread.h>` reads the host's `<sched.h>`. When the source list includes libtsm's `tsm-unicode.c`, `mk/host-check.mk` puts that library's `external/wcwidth` directory first on the quoted include path, so the kernel's `rach2/sys/wcwidth.h` cannot replace libtsm's header. A source list that includes `term/pty.c` also gets `-D_GNU_SOURCE` on the compiler command line: a forced header can include the host's `features.h` before the source's `_XOPEN_SOURCE` definition. The `etude` and `boots` groups have no kernel include path. The command printer uses the same group flags as native preparation. `-ffreestanding` keeps the compiler from turning the loops of `k_memset`, `k_memcpy` and `k_memmove` into calls to the routines they implement, which `rach2/sys/lib/kstring.c` defines as wrappers around them. `-D_DEFAULT_SOURCE` restores the POSIX declarations glibc hides under a strict `-std`, and `-D_FORTIFY_SOURCE=0` turns off the checked string calls that Ubuntu's compiler adds by default and that make `snprintf` a macro on macOS. `RACH2_HOSTTEST` lets a kernel source leave out what a host build cannot compile, and gives it i686's `cpu.h`; a test that also defines `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` on its `host-cflags:` line gets x86-64's.

A test reaches the code under test in one of two ways. It names separately compiled sources on `host-deps:` lines, or it includes a `.c` file whole, which reaches the file's `static` functions and needs no `host-deps:` line for that file. Most tests do both.

`libtsm_width_hosttest.c` compiles the patched libtsm sources, its replacement hash table and its wcwidth. It checks CJK and emoji at the right margin, exact fit, scrolling and scrollback, a scroll region, disabled autowrap and a one-column screen; decomposed accents on narrow and wide bases, pending wrap, attributes, redraw ages and the ten-code-point symbol limit; byte-at-a-time UTF-8 input through the VTE and alternate-screen isolation. Allocation faults cover symbol creation, hash insertion and index growth, with the base retained and a successful retry on the same table. It makes no files and starts no processes.

`fbdev_hosttest.c` also checks nested screen owners, event reads and polling, separate opens and inherited handles, a dead suspended owner and a dead bottom owner, allocation failure, and mode and keyboard restoration at each return. Its keyboard model is checked against the real save and restore operations by `kd_hosttest.c`. `vga_gfx_hosttest.c` requires the console to be redrawn with interrupts masked when graphics ends.

`term_unicode_hosttest.c` checks the actual terminal screen layer with the CJK and rocket from the recorded agent output and explicit accent, Cyrillic, variation-selector, emoji-modifier, flag and ZWJ vectors. Every UTF-8 split point preserves each vector's cells and following sentinel. A byte-at-a-time replay observes the recording's check mark, rocket and CJK with their two-cell widths; its final screen, colors and cursor agree with a whole-input replay. The fixture is declared with `host-inputs:` so preparation fingerprints the recording as well as the compiled sources. These checks have no accepted-failure list. [`term/Readme.md`](../../term/Readme.md#esctest2-and-the-unicode-tests) states the limits they preserve.

### The header lines

A test's opening comment may have five kinds of line, which the preparation tool reads:

* `host-deps: PATH ...`: sources, by path from the repository root, compiled and linked with the test. The line repeats, one list per line. A list wrapped onto a following comment line without its own `host-deps:` prefix fails the test, since the rest of the list would be lost, and so does a named file that does not exist.
* `host-cflags: FLAGS`: more compiler flags, also repeatable: the include paths and warnings of a source outside the kernel tree, or a `-D` the test needs. 134 tests have one.
* `host-first: REASON`: the test starts before the tests of its case without the line, for the reason given, so that a test that runs for minutes does not run alone at the end. The interruption matrices of `ext2_hosttest.c`, `fat32_hosttest.c` and `exfat_hosttest.c`, which run external checkers on copies of the volumes, have one, and so do eight of etude's tests.
* `host-alone: REASON`: the test runs when no other check runs, after the others, for its declared platform isolation or a window of a fraction of a second that a busy machine could miss. `etude_platform_hosttest.c`, `etude_qemu_hosttest.c`, `etude_remote_hosttest.c`, `etude_repeat_hosttest.c` and `etude_watchdog_hosttest.c` have one.
* `host-arch: REASON`: the test boots the kernel of the architecture `ARCH` names, for the reason given, and so is the `boots` case's, which runs once for each architecture. `etude_qemu_hosttest.c`, `etude_watchdog_hosttest.c`, `etude_scenario_hosttest.c` and `etude_legacyset_hosttest.c` have one.

A path of a check's metadata in a vendored port's extracted tree, `vendor/NAME/NAME-VERSION/` as i686 has it, such as `vendor/musl/musl-1.2.5/`, is read for a run with `ARCH=x86_64` from `build/x86_64/vendor-work/` instead, where an x86-64 build extracts the same sources, so a tree that has built only x86-64 runs the test. Another path under `vendor/`, such as a GNU recipe in `vendor/gnu/ports/`, is the repository's file on both architectures.

`tools/verify/coverage.sh` reads the same lines to measure which lines of the named sources a test runs.

`etude_platform_hosttest.c --collection-only` checks the native collection adapter without booting a machine. The real C entry stages local operands, preserves mtools offsets and names, returns failed partial output and the original status, translates debugfs requests, selects native goals and builds an isolated style job. Its preparation row uses real Git and tar with small source and product files; it checks the source index, history bundle, supplied WAD and recovery key, retained workspace and ordinary Make commands. On both architectures a native Make consumes returned musl inputs without rebuilding guest products; an archive missing an input fails even when an older copy remains in the tree. The preparation job must ask for the tooling machine's network, so that a fresh workspace can download the pinned GNU sources; a tool command or a style job that asks for one fails its row. Only the tooling transport is substituted by the fixture. The prepared image-helper controller retains its separate real image and bulk-transfer assertions.

`etude_platform_hosttest.c --retention-only` checks native retention and selected-site reference commands in temporary owned directories. It covers unchanged old bundles, active and unknown evidence, failed or incomplete histories, changed and missing proof closure, accepted baselines, open issues, stale snapshots, logical byte limits, reader use, fractional timestamps, native Windows sequence receipts, concurrent allocation, site mismatch and create-once forecast outputs. The same compiled fixture supplies the read-only GitHub response and estimator roles. No real evidence root or GitHub state is changed.

`etude_scenario_hosttest --real-forward-only` runs only the existing real command-forwarding row, including its exchange, relay closure, restricted-network assertions and cleanup checks. It uses the prepared kernel and images for `ARCH` and the QEMU programs on `PATH`.

### Shared code

* `compat/pathmax_host.h` includes the host's pathname limit for POSIX tests. On macOS it includes `sys/syslimits.h` explicitly, since Clang's `limits.h` omits the system headers under `-ffreestanding`.
* `compat/crypt.h`, `compat/shadow.h` and `compat/sys/random.h` declare the three musl interfaces `utils/pwauth.c` uses, for hosts that lack the headers (macOS has no `crypt.h` and no `getrandom`). They are musl's declarations, and the tests define the symbols.
* `compat/netutil/` has the `net/if.h`, `net/route.h` and `netpacket/packet.h` structures the network utilities use, for the tests that compile those utilities.
* `compat/e2fsprogs/` configures e2fsprogs' `unix_io.c` for the two alignment tests and gives them an in-memory sector device.
* `compat/kheap_host.c` is the kernel heap over the C library's allocator, with an optional cap on the largest allocation and on the page allocations it grants before it fails; `compat/spinlock_host.c` is a one-processor spinlock that stops the test when a held lock is taken again, `compat/kirq_none.c` the interrupt flag of a test with no interrupts, `compat/kirq_on.c` the answer of `kirq_enabled` for a test whose calls run with interrupts on, as a system call does, `compat/bkl_none.c` the big lock of a test of one thread, `compat/bkl_held.c` the big lock of one that stands for callers holding it until a row reports it free (`bkl_host_free`), when it is held only between the code's own takes, which it counts in `bkl_host_takes` and stops the test at a give it never took, and whose try fails while a row has another processor hold it (`bkl_host_elsewhere`), `compat/i915_gem_lock_host.c` the i915 GEM lock's helpers for a test that links the display, PCI or reset files without `i915_gem.c`, `compat/mutex_none.c` the kernel's mutex for one thread, which stops the test when a held mutex is taken again, `compat/resv_host.c` a reservation's write fence for a display test that links no `dmabuf.c`, `compat/atomic_host.c` the kernel's atomic exchanges through the compiler's builtins, and `compat/mmap_lock_host.c` an address space's two locks for one thread, counting its holds as `mm.c` does, where a row may name a lock another thread holds (`host_busy`), whose write trylock then fails and whose write lock runs the row's `host_wait` before it takes the hold, as that thread would run while the walker sleeps, and may have another thread run once just before the next take of a page-table lock (`host_ptl_hook`); `compat/pressure_host.c` answers the buffer cache's memory target.
* `compat/fd_flat.c` is `fd_slot`, `fd_install`, `fd_peek`, `fd_entry_open`, the table's lock and `file_ops_lock` for a test that keeps a descriptor table's entries in one array, `chunks[0]`, and stubs the rest of `fs/fd.c`.
* `compat/devmodel.c` is a disk with pending and durable writes, for the FAT tests. Reads see every write; `block_flush` makes the pending writes durable, or fails on the Nth call; a stop after the Nth write refuses every later write; and a snapshot at the stop is the medium with none, the newest one to six, or all of the writes since the last barrier, each a state a drive that reorders its cache can leave. `compat/blockmax_host.c` answers the same two block-layer calls for tests that need no model. `devmodel.h` states the model and what lies outside it.
* `compat/vm_iter_host.c` supplies ordered region readers for the address-space, paging, procfs and core-file models, which keep their own regions and do not link the VM tree.
* `compat/park_host.c` runs a second thread's call inside a sleep of the first without threads: `park_run` starts the call on a stack of its own, a model wait calls `park` to switch back, and `park_resume` continues the call where it waited. It uses the C library's ucontext calls.
* `compat/host_threads.c` runs a function on several host threads at once for a test whose kernel headers stand in the way of `<pthread.h>`, which it includes alone, so each handle has the host's own `pthread_t`, pointer-sized under MinGW-w64's winpthreads: `host_threads_run` starts no call until every thread is made, and fails when one is not made or a join fails, after joining those made.
* `compat/fixdir_host.c` gives a test that writes files a directory of its own, a new one under `$TMPDIR`, when that is an absolute path of at most 100 characters drawn from letters, digits and `/ . _ + -`, or else under `/tmp`. `fix_path` names a file in it and `fix_format` formats a command or path, ending the test when the text does not fit. The directory is removed at exit only when the test called `fix_passed` and LeakSanitizer found no leak. `bootctl`, `exfat`, `exfatcheck`, `ext2`, `fat32`, `fatcheck`, `krffs_fuse` and `netcfg` use it, and `fixdir_hosttest.c` tests it.
* `ath9k_model.c` is a software chip of the AR9003 family, a row of the ath9k driver's table, for that driver's tests, which list it in `host-deps:` and point the driver's register access at it with `ath9k_model_attach`. It keeps the chip's register file and an ordered log of the driver's writes and waits, and of its reads on request; answers the revision, the RTC's power states and resets, EEPROM and OTP reads, the interrupt status and the receive and transmit FIFOs as ath9k expects them; fills receive buffers and the transmit status ring through a DMA hook the test sets; and takes hooks on reads, writes and resets. `ath9k_model.h` states each behaviour and the faults a test can inject.
* `ath9k_ant_hosttest.c` checks antenna switches, LNA combining, PCIe ASPM, RF-silence GPIOs and suspend to idle against that model, PCIe and station models and the actual process freezer. [The driver's Tests section](../../rach2/sys/network/driver/ath9k/Readme.md#tests) lists the rows.
* `ath9k_hw_hosttest.c` checks the driver's probe, attach and resets against that model made the Lenovo's AR9565 as Linux traced it: its accesses up to the end of the OTP's image match the trace's one by one, and the probe prints the card's boot line. [The driver's Tests section](../../rach2/sys/network/driver/ath9k/Readme.md#tests) names the trace and lists the other rows.
* `ath9k_phy_hosttest.c` checks the radio, the calibrations and noise immunity against that model by the order and values of their register writes, with the wireless worker's queue driven by hand. The driver's Tests section lists what it covers.
* `ath9k_trial_hosttest.c` runs the whole driver against that model made the Lenovo's card, through the path a trial boot takes: the probe, the start, interrupts by message and the tick, a scan, a transmit hang and its reset, the join to a WPA2 AP with the station's data path and Minstrel linked in, encrypted traffic both ways with aggregation, a reset under that load, the AP's restart and the join again, and the stop, once with messages and once polled, as `rach2.ath9k_nomsi` leaves the card. It fails on an access past the window `pci.c` maps, on a cause that asks again at once, and on an access to the MAC, baseband or radio while the chip sleeps. `ath9k_lenovo.h` holds the recorded card it shares with `ath9k_hw_hosttest.c`.
* `ath9k_mac_hosttest.c` checks the driver's receive and transmit rings, interrupts, key cache, hang checks and resets against that model, with the station's calls, the worker and the clock recorded. [The driver's Tests section](../../rach2/sys/network/driver/ath9k/Readme.md#tests) lists the rows.
* `ath9k_ampdu_hosttest.c` checks transmit aggregation against that model, from the station's ADDBA request through the real data path to the driver's aggregates and the BlockAck's bitmap, with the test as the AP. The driver's Tests section lists the rows.
* `i915_model.c` is a software HD 4400 for the Intel graphics driver's tests, which list it in `host-deps:` and hand the driver its `hw`, so that every access goes through the model: BAR 0 as a register file, the configuration space of `i915_lenovo.h`, a clock that moves when the driver waits, and a log of every access. Each part of the chip with behaviour is a block in a file of its own, which i915_model.c includes and lists in its table ([`i915_model.h`](i915_model.h)).
* `i915_gem_model.c` includes the Intel driver's buffer code whole, with the GEM, PRIME, dma-buf, fence and address-space notifier code, for `i915_gem_hosttest.c`, `i915_evict_hosttest.c` and `i915_exec_hosttest.c`, which include it in turn, the last with `MODEL_HAS_REQUESTS` and `MODEL_HAS_SYNC_FILE` to bring the driver's requests and the real sync files in place of the model's stand-ins: a chip of two devices whose GTT entries and fence registers are arrays, whose register writes a test may also act on (`chip_write_hook`), and whose aperture holds what was written through it until the driver syncs it; eviction's wait for the GPU, which signals the fence a row names; memory whose frames are host pages, with a limit a test may set, reclaim that calls the registered shrinkers and a balance that runs a row's hook once, as another thread would run while it sleeps; a program's memory of regions the test lists, whose pins it counts; and descriptors, clocks, waits and programs' mappings.
* `drm_kms_model.c` includes the display code of `rach2/sys/drm/`, `drm_mode.c`, `drm_atomic.c`, `drm_vblank.c`, `drm_legacy.c` and `drm_edid.c`, and stands for the kernel around it, for the tests that include it in turn: allocation with a live count and a chosen failure, user copies from test addresses with those below 4096 faulting, a monotonic clock of 5.25 s that a test may move, waits whose condition holds since one thread runs or once a test's hook has run as a sleep would, events reserved against a file's space and recorded as sent, buffer objects behind a table of handles, device holds as a count, and descriptor 5 as a sync file whose fence a wait passes at once. A test that defines `KMS_MODEL_FENCES` brings the real fences, sync files and descriptors instead.
* `ns_first.h` answers the namespace calls of `proc/ns.c` as they answer for a process in the first namespaces. `acpi_qemu_tables.h`, `exfat_upcase.inc`, `krffs_hash_vectors.h`, `ext2_batch_cases.h`, `ext2_node_rows.h`, `ext2_lock_rows.h`, `ext2_fsync_rows.h`, `ext2_order_rows.h`, `ext2_pair_rows.h`, `ext2_write_rows.h`, `ext2_fence_rows.h` and `ext2_batch_lifecycle.h` are data and rows included by one or two tests; each begins with a comment saying where it comes from.
* `keytable.c` reads [`keytable.txt`](keytable.txt), the bytes each key of a 105-key PC keyboard sends as xterm sends them, into cases: the modes, the key events with Linux's key numbers, and the bytes, signal or local action they give. A line of the table with a modifier class stands for each choice of the left key, the right key and both. The table's header gives the xterm and resources it is read from, its form and its modes.
* `fuse/fuse.h` declares the FUSE types and calls the KRFFS mount tool uses, so `krffs_fuse_hosttest.c` needs no FUSE installed.

### Fixture builders

[`mkfatfixture.c`](mkfatfixture.c) writes FAT32 volumes from the specification, valid ones and ones with each fault a reader must refuse, and with `--check IMAGE` walks a volume and names every fault it finds. [`mkexfatfixture.c`](mkexfatfixture.c) is its pair for exFAT: a builder of the volumes the exFAT tests and the images use, and a checker that walks a volume as `fsck_exfat` does, with `--list`, `--dump` and `--read PATH`. File export preserves every byte through stdout as well as `--out FILE`, including on Windows, where stdout otherwise translates line feeds. Both are written apart from the kernel's drivers, so that the drivers are checked against a second implementation of each format; the options are listed at the head of each source. The tests that use them compile their own copies into their fixture directories; the build compiles both into `build/tests/host/` for the image rules and the guest suites. The host tier does not run them as tests; `fatcheck_hosttest.c` and `exfatcheck_hosttest.c` test their checkers.

`mkexfat_external.sh DIR` formats and fills an exFAT volume with another implementation for `exfat_hosttest.c`'s rows over a volume it wrote: `hdiutil` and `newfs_exfat` on macOS, and elsewhere exfatprogs' `mkfs.exfat` and Linux's driver in etude's [tooling machine](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#the-tooling-machine), since a mount on the host needs root. Inside an existing root Linux tooling job, it uses that job's formatter and driver directly; its private loop mount and scratch are removed on every exit. Preparation first runs `mkexfat_external.sh -p FILE` to ready that writer, then creates the volume, expected source files and manifest. The rule `build/tests/host/exfat_writer` records their digests and the producer's source digest. The host checks consume those immutable files; no writer or tooling machine runs during a check. Missing or changed files require preparation again.

### Fixtures for extracted functions

Some kernel functions are in files no host test can compile whole. The files in `fixtures/` are test programs over such a function: a rule of [`mk/host-fixtures.mk`](../../mk/host-fixtures.mk) extracts it unchanged from its source into an `.inc` file with [`extract.c`](fixtures/extract.c), which the rules compile first, and compiles the fixture over it. `kstack.c` checks that a new kernel stack is cleared and has a guard page; `endothers.c` that an init under the debug shell waits for the other processes to end, and that execve's wait for the other threads ends with `-EAGAIN` and clears `exec_task` when SIGKILL comes for its caller or its process; `chown_plan.c` the set-id rules of a chown on a mounted file system; `krffs_open.c` and `krffs_root_lock.c`, with the thread glue of `krffs_open_threads.c`, an open during the flush that publishes a moved KRFFS node, the cached walk without the big lock over a model type whose names the name cache keeps (its links whose targets the node holds, its crossings of covering mounts by namespace, its entry into a nameless mount and its `..` from the directories it came down through, the mounts over a nameless mount's root and over the root a `..` from one reaches, which it crosses as the full walk does, the name record it drops at a file bind, and its root at an absolute link), and the KRFFS root's data operations under the mount's lock, while `krffs_open.c` also changes a model ext2 file through the extracted `vfs_write`, `vfs_truncate` and `vfs_fallocate` by an open handle that is not its node, with the real `loop_backs` refusing either of two independent opens of a loop's backing file; and `krffs_stat.c` the inode number the KRFFS root gives to the stat calls. The fixture `pagecache` is `pagecache_hosttest.c` compiled with `sys_sync`. Host preparation builds the seven, and the case `scripts_native` runs each as a check of its own.

`etude_guest_hosttest --backpressure-only` fills the serial frame queue, witnesses the native child exit, and withholds acknowledgements past the original pipe-drain allowance. It then requires every final byte, both full stream digests and confirmed cleanup. Two further rows cancel or expire an exited attempt with the queue still full and require stopped, unconfirmed records without another acknowledgement. On Linux a fourth row reads the console through a pipe of one page at a frame every 40 ms, so that sending what the child left after its exit takes longer than the allowance, and requires every byte and confirmed cleanup. The ordinary entry includes these rows.

`etude_guest_hosttest --detached-only` runs its three unreported-daemon cleanup paths on Rach2 or Linux: normal exit, cancellation and responder loss. Its ordinary entry still runs every protocol check. A failed check retains and names its private evidence directory. macOS refuses this focused entry because its guest adapter has no child subreaper.

`etude_platform_hosttest.c --evidence-only` checks native evidence staging, restoration, routine proof packing and installed-reader verification. It retains original locators across transport, including foreign native paths, follows late history and load proofs, excludes executable artifact objects and refuses changed, ambiguous, missing, duplicated or escaping members. Restoration verifies every member before creating any destination file. All files are private fixture data.

## Adding tests

Name the file `NAME_hosttest.c` and put the sources it links on `host-deps:` lines in its opening comment:

```c
/**
 * Host test for foo.
 *
 * host-deps: rach2/sys/lib/foo.c rach2/sys/lib/bar.c
 */

#include "foo.h"

int main(void)
{
    return check_foo() ? 0 : 1;
}
```

Repeat the `host-deps:` prefix rather than wrap a long list. A test may use the host's C library, but one that includes kernel sources whole must avoid host headers that define the kernel's types again; the compat files above declare what they need for that reason. Return 0 only when every check passed, and a status from 1 to 255 otherwise: the exit status keeps the low eight bits of what `main` returns, so a count of 256 failures returned whole reads as a pass. Keep the files a test writes in the directory `compat/fixdir_host.c` gives it. Add the test's assertion, `NAME`, to its case in [`tests/cases/host.host.json`](../cases/host.host.json): `tests`, or `etude` for an `etude_` test, or `boots` for one with a `host-arch:` line.

## Limits

* The models are written from the datasheets and the code under test, not from hardware: a device that behaves differently from its model passes here and fails in the guest suites or on the machine.
* No test enters the kernel through a system call or changes privilege. Where a test needs a second thread, it switches at fixed points, by `park_host.c` or by host threads, so an interleaving it does not name is not tested.
* The suite builds with the host's compiler and C library, not the cross compiler, so a fault that only `i686-elf-gcc` or the x86-64 build produces is not seen here.

## Tests

* `keymap_hosttest.c` links the shared translator and loads keymap entries from text held in memory. It checks US, Dvorak, Colemak, a Russian layer switched by ShiftL locks, Cyrillic and Armenian Caps Lock, German and French dead keys, Compose cancellation, sticky locks, modifier releases after map changes, Alt numeric input, UTF-8 bounds, and xterm cursor, editing, function and keypad bytes.

Each entry names a test, the code it compiles, and its cases. The reasoning of a case is in the test's source.

### Etude

* `etude_artifact_hosttest.c` tests etude's artifact store and preparation graph, `tools/etude/posix/store.c` and `prepare.c`, with the POSIX executor: objects published whole under their digests, digests kept in a digests file and read again after a write, a sparse file published and copied with its holes, a large file published as a read-only link of itself in place of its copy and left as it was by a rebuild, with `--link-only` that row alone, qcow2 overlays made over objects, writers killed while they copy, graphs reused by key, recipes that fail each way, damaged objects and records, six processes that prepare one node at once, and a controller killed while its recipe runs. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests). Its bound-refusal row advances a host-test preparation clock after the hanging recipe writes its partial product and ready witness. The 300 ms comparison, real cancellation, sanitizer settings and partial-product assertions stay unchanged; process startup is not judged against that fixture clock.
* `etude_budget_hosttest.c` tests etude's budget, `tools/etude/budget.c`, `digest.c` and `etude estimate`, over streams it writes through the history store: policies whose limits do not nest, the digests of a case, the schedule a forecast assumes, forecasts from recorded costs with unknown costs and nodes and without a case whose hosts leave out the site's system, and checks of runs that pass, pass the ceiling, are not observed, leave out an item, reuse an exempt first run's attempt, hide preparation or set up a machine once. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_legacy_hosttest.c` tests etude's preparation adapter, `tools/etude/posix/legacy.c`, with the POSIX executor, on fixture trees of a Makefile: the steps of the preparation in order, the goals built and recorded, the requests refused before anything is built, a failed step, a missing artifact, a step past its bound and one under a stop signal, the check of a record by status and by digest, and the record published by a preparation graph. It also runs the built etude's host preparation and import on fixture trees, from `compat/prepare_host_rows.c` and `compat/prepare_import_rows.c` with the fixtures of `compat/prepare_fixture.c`: the partition of the etude and scripts checks, the reuse of a predecessor's programs and tools, prepared external tools and inputs named in hex, the Windows companion programs under a stand-in compiler, a preparation of one job that builds only while it holds a slot of `--slots`, and an import's request, record, receipt, inventories, roles, native builds and its local, SSH and WSL transports, whose `ssh` and `rsync` are copies of the test program. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_legacyset_hosttest.c` tests etude's set driver, `etude_legacy_set` in `tools/etude/posix/legacy_set.c`, on the suites of a fixture tree, with a private ledger and private locks: suites run by the host, guest and machine cases of fixture manifests, among them a set with a failed case and its history, two suites under a width of two, with and without locks, and of one, a serial member, a stop at the first failure and at a member that stops the set, a stop signal and the status record, a failed preparation, a case larger than the ledger's limit, and the requests refused before anything runs, the cases judged by the built etude's `check`, with the manifests it refuses, the artifacts the machine cases share copied to the run's store once, and again after a change or a failed copy, a machine case of the lazy boundary profile with and without the site's permission, and a manifest read without its case whose hosts leave out this host. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests). The native stop sender waits for its own Ctrl-Break ignore callback before detaching from the target console, preserving the handler through asynchronous delivery. A Windows child whose Job still has processes records the original controller status and a PID, creation identity, exact native image, wait result and role census. Only verified system console hosts permit owned Job teardown without changing that status; any other or unknown member fails the row, and every Job must become empty. A private ready/release handshake holds each driver at entry until the parent records its console's native image and creation identity. A denied teardown image query can use only that exact earlier observation after current Job membership and creation still match. Generated measurement documents use binary output so the observer header hash binds the same newline bytes on Windows and POSIX hosts. A regular file in place of the first case's working directory exercises startup refusal: first-failure mode cancels the next case before it writes its marker, while ordinary batch mode runs it. `--stop-only` runs these native rows and the completed-failure cancellation row without guest artifacts or QEMU. On Windows, a row whose driver leaves a suspended copy of itself in its Job and exits 0 must end with 121 once the Job is empty. `--children-only` runs the rows that judge a controller's exit status without guest artifacts or QEMU: the mixed-result row, the three scheduler-width rows and, on Windows, that row. Files whose literal bytes supply artifact digests are written in binary mode on every host, including the changed and restored disk rows. Its default path and `--capture-only` mode also check the real watchdog result writer without QEMU: complete and missing replies, exact stored bytes after supervisor data is released, original capture errors and refused evidence writes; and the order in which the pool makes members ready: the first member that does not fit beside the running members, or that is larger than the pool, is given the time at which it will fit, and a member after it starts only when it is expected to end by then or fits beside it then.
* `etude_compare_hosttest.c` runs `etude compare`, the comparison of a run's cases with their timing history in `tools/etude/`, over streams it writes through the history store: a candidate within the noise of five samples, a slowdown with the commits in between, its changed step and the commands that profile it, an improvement, three samples that are too few, a changed workload, failed, disrupted, unrecorded and retried samples, other architectures and accelerators, an accepted baseline that five slow runs do not move until `-n` asks for a new one, and damaged streams. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_failures_hosttest.c` runs `etude show failures`, the listing of a run's attempts that did not pass in `tools/etude/`, over streams it writes through the history store, with results documents in a store: a failure that a passing retry follows, a failure that the run record calls passed, a run without its run record, attempts that did not finish or were disrupted, damaged and missing results, a listing of forty items cut at 80 lines, and the streams and records it refuses. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_history_hosttest.c` tests the C99 history store and collectors in `tools/etude/`: validated durable records, concurrent exclusive writers, interrupted and damaged streams, missing data, clock intervals and resource aggregation. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_ledger_hosttest.c` tests etude's ledger of resource claims, `tools/etude/ledger.c`, with the POSIX executor: claims and their limits, registration before a case runs, competing processes, damaged ledger files, recovery judged from the identities of real processes, and workers killed while their cases run. Its authority fixture has private protected files, including the program, and checks that group-write permission on that program prevents admission. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_ledgercmd_hosttest.c` runs `etude status` and `etude release`, `tools/etude/posix/claims.c`, on a ledger of real processes: the listing of owned, live and uncertain claims and of a request, a drop refused for a claim whose owner runs, for one whose process runs and for one the ledger lacks, an uncertain claim dropped, and the paths it must refuse. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_lines_hosttest.c` runs `etude show lines`, etude's compatible formatter, `tools/etude/lines.c`, on histories it writes through the history store, and requires its lines to keep a suite's failure apart from the runner's: failures, a retry, a stalled case, a disrupted attempt, a run cut short or unfinished, maps that leave a case or a suite without the other, a cancelled run and run records that disagree with their attempts. It then writes a status record with `tools/etude/posix/status.c` from real watchdogs, and a real ledger, and requires `tools/flow/test_status.py` to tell work within its bounds from waits for admission, stalled attempts and uncertain claims. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_remote_hosttest.c` tests etude's remote execution in `tools/etude/`: the responder's ledger and checked transfers, and the controller's side through a relay that damages, repeats and cuts frames. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_native_hosttest.c` runs `etude run native`, the controller of one case on a machine that is already up, `tools/etude/posix/run_native.c`, on the TinyCC build of `tests/native/tccbuild.sh` with the host's cc, through `etude-guest --stdio` and a transport that cuts the connection, holds it open on the machine's side, or kills `etude-guest`: a pass, an intended failure, a cancellation, a cut during the build and after its end, a held session taken over, an end lost with its `etude-guest`, and a script given fixtures, an input and a variable left out by `-F`, `-i` and `-U`, each with no process left. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_nativeprep_hosttest.c` tests the private KRFFS roots of `etude prepare native`, `tools/etude/native_prepare_image.c`, as their own `mkfs.krffs` and `stageimage.krffs`, which it is when started under those names: a free extent brought to a block boundary for files of five lengths, the staged nodes kept unchanged, the padding a file of zeros under a block owned by root with mode 0444, the same bytes when a root is made again, the refusal of a manifest that names the padding's path, and a root made again on a larger volume when its files leave less free space than asked.
* `etude_sched_hosttest.c` tests etude's admission and schedule, `tools/etude/sched.c`, `cost.c` and the requests of `ledger.c`: processor reservations and costs from history, the order of both architectures' jobs, a blocked request protected from claims that would take what it needs, its fair choice between accounts, requests of workers that died, and six workers that compete for one machine. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_bridge_hosttest.c` tests etude's bridge from ledger claims to the old runner's host and port locks, `tools/etude/posix/bridge.c` over `portlock.c`, and its external wrapper `etude admit`, `posix/admit.c`, with lock files and a ledger of its own: old shared runs and new shared claims together on distinct port blocks, exclusive runs that keep out the other kind in both orders, ten port blocks all taken, the range of native ports, a claim the ledger does not have, the wrapper's offset, registration and exit status around a stand-in for an old checkout, and its wait for admission and its result file, which tells a command's exit from a refusal, a stop and an incomplete end; on Linux also a killed controller whose locks are released once its executor confirms the cleanup, and a dead worker whose uncertain claim keeps the locks until an operator drops it. It also checks a CI orphan sweep against a job whose marker was inherited through exec: the case ends and its claim and locks are released. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_ports_hosttest.c` runs `etude lock ports`, `tools/etude/lock_ports.c` over `portlock.c`, in children of itself on lock files of its own: the lowest free block, kept while its command runs, and the offset given to the command; the host taken alone and shared and the order of the two; a stop during either wait, which starts no command; the record of a wait; refusals; and a stand-in for an older checkout's claim, `compat/old_ports_host.c`, beside the command.
* `etude_route_hosttest.c` runs the fixed route actions of `tools/etude/routine_route.c` through `tools/ci/fixtures/routine_route_actions_test.c`, which replaces the route's path and remote transport functions: the remote operation, transport admission and environment of a production, with an environment value that is not a string refused; the result files fetched for each import mode and action, and a fetch that stops at its first failure; an installed actor sent only while its digest matches; and a direct request refused for another output root, selection or preparation.
