Through `etude prepare route direct-setup` on a small committed tree, the frozen policy is projected for the Linux endpoint and kept, and only a request that matches the invocation packs the routine proofs. The owner of `tools/etude/authority_run.c` gives its child's exit status, and 128 plus the signal once SIGTERM has stopped it and its child's cleanup is confirmed. It uses no endpoint or authority.
* `etude_producer_hosttest.c` runs `etude prepare producer`, `tools/etude/routine_producer.c`, through the built etude on small committed trees, with itself as the producer's installed etude: it fakes the collection preparation, the export and the ledger's status, and passes every other command to the real etude. A production stages the candidate bundle's commit and prepares once into a fresh output of its run, with the clean declaration of its purpose and no inherited phase selection, and releases the tree's run lock; the same run again is refused, and a second run has an output of its own. A clean declaration that its purpose does not allow, a changed commit argument, a clean value that is not a boolean and a transport admission of another mode are refused before any output. Transport cleanup passes only when the ledger has no claim or request of the producer's run, the actor only while its digest matches, and the site projection is made once. On macOS a jobs snapshot without a Linux boot proof is refused.
* `etude_repeat_hosttest.c` tests etude's shuffled and loaded repetitions, `tools/etude/repeat.c`, `posix/repeat.c` and `posix/load.c`: the plan a seed gives and its record, a race between two cases that a seed exposes and reproduces, retries whose history keeps the failures they repeat, cases run at once, loads reserved beside their cases, and the budget a run records, which the check of the budget passes. Its FIFO pair enters cleanup only after its real completed-exchange output; the blocked single-width pair retains its short execution gap. Both forms still require actual process ends and confirmed cleanup. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_runtime_hosttest.c` runs etude's parts together on Linux: workers of three accounts that run plans through the repetition driver on one ledger, workers killed while their cases run, a disk that fills under the store and the history, protected requests and one whose worker died, a case blocked forever, and a remote case whose transport is lost for good or once. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_step_hosttest.c` runs `etude build`, `tools/etude/step/step.c`, in children of itself, in two copies of a tree and a store of its own: the key and what changes it, a copy-mode step stored by one copy and taken by the other, a link-mode step built in the store and the step that uses it, the builds that stay in the copy, a failed and a partial build, ccache's names on the command's path, two builds of one key at once, and the refusals. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_volume_hosttest.c` tests `etude-volume`'s code, `tools/etude/volume/volume.c`: the tooling machine's FAT16 seed volume, read back by a FAT reader of the test's own, and the tar archive of its job volume, extracted by the host's tar(1), and archives of the host's tar in the GNU and pax formats read back by the pairs that place their entries, with damaged, truncated and hostile archives refused. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_winfmt_hosttest.c` runs `tools/etude/win/winfmt.c`, the plain C of etude's Windows adapter. Rows quote command lines and parse them back with a model of the UCRT's rules: arguments with spaces, tabs, quotes, runs of backslashes before a quote and at the end, an empty argument, bytes beyond ASCII, and 20,000 lines drawn from those bytes by a fixed seed; a program's name with a quote is refused. Rows sort an environment by name without regard to case, and refuse an entry with no name, one with no `=`, and two names that differ only in case. Rows convert the performance counter's ticks at 10 MHz and 3 GHz, the largest count included, and FILETIMEs at, after and before 1970. The fixtures of `tools/etude/wincheck.sh` run the same code on Windows, where the UCRT itself parses the line.
* `etude_guest_hosttest.c` runs `etude-guest` of `tools/etude/guest/` in a child process, over standard I/O and as the serial transport: commands repeated and refused, a pass and an intended failure, cancellation, deadlines and a lost connection, a reported child that calls setsid(2) in the test program, damaged, repeated and out-of-order frames, retransmission, and `exec` in place. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_serial_hosttest.c` tests the controller of `etude-guest` on a serial console, `tools/etude/serial.c`, with frames it writes itself and with `etude-guest` in a child process on pipes, directly and through a relay that drops, repeats, damages and reorders frames both ways: acknowledgement and resending, frames not acted on, staged and sent files, output in order, a pass, an intended failure, a program killed by a signal, cancellation, a closed console and an `etude-guest` started again. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests).
* `etude_exec_hosttest.c` tests the executor of host processes in `tools/etude/posix/`: registration before the exec, a keeper killed during registration whose held child must end on EOF without running its program, output, cancellation, the descendants a case leaves, and the processor time and memory of its tree. A sequence of 300 reported children followed by a live descendant checks that cleanup retains every identity, with native process handling and with POSIX interfaces alone. Every registration requires the held-child witness. A GNU build identifies the running Rach2 kernel through its uname release and version before choosing the process-state witness; Linux retains its syscall-file witness. On macOS it uses the task's runnable-thread count, since BSD process status SRUN also describes a sleeping task. An unavailable or terminal state fails the witness; transitional states are checked again after a short sleep.
* `etude_platform_hosttest.c` checks the native cache mutex and complete output files. Its output fixture compares bounded captures with complete separate and merged files, checks truncation and file replacement, and refuses a missing output parent. A failure reports its stage, executor result and captured bytes, and keeps the output files at the printed evidence paths. Instrumented platform and executor children receive explicit fatal sanitizer settings and the inventory-bound native symbolizer, without inheriting the rest of the parent's environment.
* `etude_qemu_hosttest.c` tests the QEMU launcher of `tools/etude/`: the command lines of profiles, disks on IDE, AHCI and NVMe, a CD, disks that boot first and qcow2 overlays among them, and those of the q35 machine, an SMBIOS vendor and an ACPI table with commas doubled, the audio cards recorded to WAV files, the user networks with the forwards of the case's ports and their guest forwards, cards on hubs of their own, and the end of a machine told from QMP's events and QEMU's exit, against a stand-in for QEMU and against the real one with the tree's kernels, with disks on IDE and AHCI, and with OVMF's code image, which must name a CD and an empty disk of 4 TiB on NVMe. The private NVMe file is created exclusively through the sparse-file adapter and its full 4 TiB length is checked before firmware starts. Its `--argv-only` mode checks profile arguments and accelerator refusal messages without starting a machine. `--memory-only` checks the existing boundary arguments and allocation refusals without booting QEMU. The boundary backend omits `dump=off` on macOS and Windows, where disabling guest-memory dumps is unsupported; its 72 GiB size, lazy allocation and reservations are unchanged. Its `--host-only` mode checks the launcher without a machine, serving early output events until the host child completes. Its separate `--boundary` mode on x86-64 runs the lazy 72 GiB accounting boot and checks the original mapping boundary and its resident peak. The stand-in reports the WHPX accelerator name separately from its command-line options, retaining the exact interrupt-controller option assertion and real accelerator confirmation.
* `etude_scenario_hosttest.c` tests `libetude-case`, the library of etude's machine scenarios, `tools/etude/posix/scenario.c`: private disk copies kept across dependent boots, waits that a lost QEMU, console or QMP ends as lost and never as the event awaited, step bounds, stops, refused requests and damaged artifacts, a failure's detail without the spaces around it or the space where its record is cut, which a report refuses, services and several machines at once, and failures at each stage that end only the failing case's processes, an empty sparse disk on NVMe kept across two boots, a CD and an empty disk on AHCI, the recordings of two boots' audio, and the forwards of the case's ports, against a stand-in for QEMU, and a reboot on a disk on AHCI, a reboot through a qcow2 overlay that `qemu-img` maps and converts, a guest run of `etude-guest`, two machines that reach a service, the empty disk found by the kernel, a forward of the case's ports, and a guest forward to a service on a restricted network on the real one. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests). On native Windows the same rows use Winsock socket transfer, direct child reexecution and Job Objects; a requested stop enters the scenario's C signal handler through a private native event, while a hard kill bypasses it. The killed suspension row observes the actual process exit status rather than a POSIX signal handler's file. The stand-in serial peer uses the production frame codec and executes the native report and killer programs; the real guest row still runs the Rach2 responder and `/hello` under QEMU. Its generated invocation paths use the JSON string writer, including native Windows executable paths; decoded path bytes and all stand-in and real-machine assertions remain exact. The media expectation reserves each of its three complete paths and its fixed drive options. Forward expectations use the native executable suffix. Its Windows ownership census compares canonical backslashes and repeated path separators without changing the exact private fixture and row names, and prints each matched PID and argument vector before checking counts and cleanup. It also passes ended QMP shutdown results with absent or unconfirmed executor cleanup through the real scenario wait, disk binding and restart operations, preserving the shutdown cause while refusing disk reuse. These and the raw SSH bulk rows run by default as well as through `--net-bulk-only`.
* `etude_watchdog_hosttest.c` tests the per-case watchdog of `tools/etude/` and its supervisor: bounds fixed from samples, progress told apart from heartbeats and other output, quiet work, and the end of a busy loop, a stream of heartbeats, a stand-in QEMU whose QMP hangs, the real QEMU idle at its shell and a remote case whose transport is lost. Its cases are described in [the component Readme](../../tools/etude/Readme.md#tests). On Windows, its quiet child starts from the exact prepared executable path, including a suffixless PE, and remains in the supervisor's Job. Launch errors, child exit statuses and retained standard error are printed without changing the watchdog bounds.

### Kernel libraries, memory and processes

The mapped-file rows also keep a failed last release's dirty page, and let it go after a retry by each of fsync, sync(2), msync, the flusher and a later release, reporting the error once; release with the heap refusing allocations; write a queued writer's stores without waiting for its release; keep a later store when a release or a sync succeeds before a later release fails, in either order; free a dirty page at a truncation to zero; drop a failed page on a gone device; and zero a partial page's EOF tail before regrowth. `tmpfs_hosttest.c` truncates mapped pages with a stale remembered size. `open_hosttest.c` creates a mapping during a writer's buffer fault and checks descriptor and core writes against its resident page, and checks that a descriptor write puts into a dirty page the bytes the file system accepted, from the kernel's copy of the caller's buffer, while every read of the file fails and the buffer changes after the write, and that the page's write-back keeps them (`mapped_snapshot`). `i915_evict_hosttest.c` keeps a shared file through a busy GPU's revoked pin, marks its page dirty at the release while the file and frame are still held, and drops the file only after the last device store.

`filemap_hosttest.c` models page-cache misses with file reads and a hit with distinct cached bytes. The hit returns a resident page without a file read or another cache store and adopts the frame under the file's lock. A delayed write mark after truncation refuses both an absent frame and a replacement at the same offset; writeback leaves the regrown clean hole unallocated. Its sync row grows the file and stores into a new page while the pass sleeps, and checks that the pass is bounded by the pages it began with and leaks no description reference. Its writer rows model the record's own handle (`vfs_open_node`, `vfs_release`) and the node hold beside it, and check that a failed final write leaves the page dirty with the handle open and the mapping's description let go, that each retry writes through the handle and lets it go with the record, that sync(2) writes inside the base mount (`vfs_enter`), that a gone device drops the page, that the base's unmount writes or drops the page and lets the handle go while an unmount of a bind or of another mount leaves it, so a store through a bind whose write failed with ENOSPC is written once space is freed, that a handle that cannot be opened fails the mapping, that `filemap_disk_written` puts the bytes it is given into a dirty page with reads failing, and that two passes failing on one file system each record their failure for syncfs, which the fsync of a third file does not hear. Its error rows keep a model of the file system's errors for fsync and for syncfs, each with its cursor, and of the files' own error sequences (`vfs_ino_err_set`), each description with its cursor into its file's, and check that a failed release records for syncfs and in the file's sequence, so that after a retry by sync(2), fsync, msync, the flusher or a later release that writes every store, the next fsync or MS_SYNC through each description opened before the first report, one opened between the failure and the report among them, reports the failure once, while a description opened after the report, a third file's and another file system's hear nothing, a failed MS_ASYNC is not told again to its own description, and that pages dropped on a gone device are told to fsync. Its remount rows check that a remount read-only writes the base's pages and none of another mount's, and answers a failed write with the page kept (`filemap_sync_mount`); its evict rows, that the put of a nameless file that leaves only its record's writer drops the page unwritten and lets the writer go, and does nothing while a mapping lives or for another node (`filemap_evict_unlinked`). Its model counts a write-back by the pages written, one at the first piece of each. Its clean rows model the shared mapping's entry of a page and the walk that takes its write access (`paging_wrprotect_where`), and check that a pass while the writable mapping lives writes only the pages stored since the last, that a store after a pass faults and marks the page, that a pass between a fault's mark and its entry makes the store fault again, that a failed write keeps the page, that a page a device holds (`frame_pinned`) keeps its bit while the pin lasts and when the pin goes during the write, after the device's last store, and that the release of a write pin (`filemap_pin_dirty`) marks the page once, during a write or after the last pass, and marks nothing for a frame no longer the file's or through a private mapping, and that a release that comes while a write fault grows the directory of dirty bits, at the heap's free of the old directory, marks its page in the directory that replaced it, beside the fault's page, with nothing freed or logged under the record's bitmap lock (`pin_grow_rows`).

* `kstring_hosttest.c` runs the string and memory routines of `rach2/sys/lib/kstring.c` with `rach2/sys/lib/kctypes.c`. Rows check `k_strlen`, `k_memset`, `k_memcpy`, `k_strncpy`, `k_strreverse` on strings of zero to five characters, `k_toupper`, `k_tolower`, `k_strtoupper`, and `k_memmove` over ranges that overlap in either direction and between two separate objects. A sweep runs the copy, the fill and both directions of the move for every length from 0 to 67 at four source and four destination offsets, and checks the bytes copied and the bytes beside them.
* `kstd_hosttest.c` runs `k_itoa` of `rach2/sys/lib/kstd.c`, the integer formatter behind kernel and utility numbers, with `kstring.c` and `kctypes.c`. Rows check signed decimal to both limits of `int`, the most negative value included; hexadecimal, octal and binary, which print the bit pattern unsigned, and a radix of 36; the returned count, which includes the sign and the terminating NUL; a buffer too small and a radix out of range, each answered with 0; and the plus `force_sign` writes before a positive decimal and not before zero or a negative one. It also runs `k_strtoul` against Linux's kstrtoul with base 0: the bases, the plus sign, one newline, leading zeros, the end at a NUL or at the length, junk, and a number past 64 bits, which is `ERANGE` whatever follows it, and past 32 bits, `EINVAL` with junk after it and, where an unsigned long has 32 bits as on i686, `ERANGE` without; and `k_strtoll` against Linux's kstrtos64, at both ends of 64 signed bits and one past each.
* `kfmt_hosttest.c` runs `k_vprintf` of `rach2/sys/lib/kfmt.c`, the formatter of every kernel message, into a buffer and compares each row's whole output. Rows check the conversions `%d`, `%u`, `%lu`, `%x`, `%X`, `%o`, `%b`, `%s` with a null pointer, `%c` and `%%`; unsigned values past the largest `int`; the `0` flag and the width, and a value wider than its width; a PCI address message of five fields; a width before another argument; a format that ends on its percent; and an unknown conversion, which prints its letter.
* `klog_hosttest.c` includes the kernel log, `rach2/sys/klog.c`, with recorders for the screen, the serial port and netconsole, and counts the interrupt brackets it takes. Rows check a record's priority, uptime, subsystem and single newline; truncation and its mark; the clamped levels and the levels each destination takes; the order of the destinations, with a serial port that never drains; the eviction of whole records when the ring wraps; console output, which reaches the screen and the port but not the ring or the wire; records raised during the delivery of another, one at a time and in a burst one past the queue of sixty-four, which is counted, and a burst of sixty-four while a drain writes the queue's last record, which the queue holds beside it; a record raised during a program's console write or a line of the debug shell, which follows the line; and the records kept for later, built with `RACH2_HOST_CPU` so that a record on processor 1 is one made without the big lock: kept records reach the ring and the port ahead of the holder's next record, in the order they were made, inside another record's delivery too; a part-built slot holds back the records after it until the panic skips it; a full queue keeps 128 and counts the rest; and the processor that writes them out for a locked-up holder (`klog_flush_stuck`) stands back while the holder is in a record or a console write, while a holder back meanwhile keeps its own records for its next one and its console byte or write waits for it to finish, a second such processor leaves the first's number in place, and it takes only the records kept when it began, over the second pass that one more than the queue holds takes; a nested record of the holder's stays in after such a processor's exchange; a record made inside a console write while a kept record waits behind the full queue is kept after it; a panic that stops a ring append part way, after the eviction or inside the bytes of a full ring, writes after the last whole record, and one that stops a collector after the ring and the queue took a kept record sends that record once; and the panic's first record, made with the queue full, which goes out after the queue and is not dropped, a record kept behind that full queue, which goes out between the two, and a record a drain the panic stopped was part way through, at the port or at netconsole, which goes out again whole to that destination and those after it, but not to those that had it whole, and the rest once, from its own slot also when a burst of sixty-four was raised during its write, which a queue of sixty-four slots would let the burst's last record take.
* `krandom_hosttest.c` includes the ChaCha20 generator of `rach2/sys/lib/krandom.c` with a model of the processor's RDSEED and RDRAND that gives scripted words and failures, and a cycle count, a boot time and a tick the rows set. Rows check `chacha_block` against the block of RFC 8439 section 2.3.2 and its serialized bytes; the stream of one seed against OpenSSL's ChaCha20 of the key it expands to, across two block ends and the carry of the block counter into its high word; that `krandom_u32` and `krandom_bytes` draw from one cursor and a reseed drops the rest of a block; that another seed gives another stream, the all-zero seed no zeros, a fill of 37 bytes stays within its bounds, and 4096 bits have between 1844 and 2252 set. Further rows check `krandom_seed`'s choice of source: RDSEED's eight words as the key when it gives them, a word allowed 32 attempts; eight new words of RDRAND's when RDSEED stops part way; the boot's timing, untrusted, when neither gives; and the source `krandom_source` and `krandom_is_trusted` report before seeding, after each choice and after a reseed.
* `latency_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/latency.c`, the kernel's record of its longest delays, with a clock the test sets. Rows check that a delay is the difference of its two readings and only a longer one replaces the maximum; that a pair whose later reading is the smaller is dropped, not kept as a delay of nearly 2^32 microseconds; that the gap between timer ticks follows the same rule and measures the next gap from the stepped reading; and that a kind out of range reads zero and a note for one changes nothing.
* `mbinfo_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/arch/x86/mbinfo.c`, the reader of the boot loader's information, over multiboot2 tag lists the test writes and a Multiboot structure whose pointers a model of physical memory answers. Rows check the boot record: the command line, the loader's name and the memory above 1 MiB; multiboot2 memory maps and the entry sizes refused; an EFI map of 48-byte descriptors, with each kind of memory classed, which takes the place of a multiboot2 map and is cut and counted past 256 entries, and whose runtime ranges keep their attributes, the runtime code and data joined and the memory-mapped I/O apart; the EFI system tables; each kind of framebuffer and its palette; the two RSDP revisions; ten modules, of which eight are kept; and lists cut short, read up to the fault. The Multiboot rows check the same fields. A last row passes the record's command line to `kcmdline_capture` of `rach2/sys/cmdline.c`, which keeps a line of 510 bytes whole and cuts one the record cut at 511 after its last whole token.
* `modlayout_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/modlayout.c`, the check that the boot modules leave the kernel image and each other alone before the kernel reserves their memory. Rows accept modules back to back, out of order, ending at the limit or starting at the kernel's end, and none at all; and refuse an empty, backwards or wrapping range, a module past the limit or over the kernel image, and two that overlap in any way, with the later module's index. Further rows check the panic text of `modlayout_describe`, at the width of the host's addresses and cut to a short buffer or to none, and the reason given for each code.
* `tables_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/i686/gdt.c`, `tss.c` and `idt.c` with the assembly they call stubbed, and reads the tables they build for processor 0. Rows check the double fault's task: vector 8 is a present task gate of DPL 0 whose GDT entry is an available 32-bit TSS over `double_fault_tss`, and that TSS starts `isr_double_fault` with interrupts off in the kernel's segments, on a stack of its own, with the page directory it is given. Other rows check that every other exception gate is an interrupt gate to its own stub, that each message vector, 48 to 239 but 0x80, has a gate to its own stub of the run of 16-byte stubs, that the kernel's TSS keeps its ring 0 stack, and that GDT entry 8 is the processor's data segment, whose first word is its index.
* `frame_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/mm/frame.c`, which builds the map of physical memory, over memory maps in the boot record's form, and checks the usable, unreachable, reserved and managed bytes and which frames are usable. With i686's reach of 4 GiB the maps are QEMU's from 64 MiB to 4 GiB; the Lenovo G50-70's as its boot printed it, with NVS and ACPI ranges and most of its memory above 4 GiB; and synthetic maps with holes, overlaps, a wrapping length, a cut entry, no entries or too many ranges. With x86-64's reach of 64 TiB they are QEMU's at 6 and 8 GiB, the Lenovo's, a range past 64 TiB, and a UEFI-shaped map of 256 entries; with the 64 GiB reach of a processor without 1 GiB pages, QEMU's at 72 GiB, whose usable bytes and 513 KiB of holes make 63 GiB and whose 9 GiB past 64 GiB are unreachable. Further rows check `FRAME_MAP_RANGES` and where `frame_map_place` puts the frame table.
* `paging_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/paging.c` and `rach2/sys/arch/i686/mmu.c` with the processor primitives and page directory of the test's own and a heap that counts the page tables it gives; its `host-cflags:` lines make `uintptr_t` and `size_t` 32 bits wide, as on an i686. Rows check that `map_mmio` maps a register page uncached and refuses a range past the top of the address space, and that `premap_kernel_window` gives a window its tables, at address 0 and ending at the top, and refuses one past the top. Other rows check the 1024 slots of `kmap_temp`: a released slot stays mapped until a sweep, made only when no slot is free, which a held slot survives. Seven rows, built with `RACH2_HOST_CPU`, split the slots between processors: two processors mapping at once take the first slot of each one's pool and drop their own translations of it, a slot released on another processor goes back to its owner's pool, a sweep covers one pool, a processor whose pool is all held borrows another's free slot, one processor may still hold all 1024, and three and 64 processors split the slots in whole words; every pool's lock is free after each call. Two rows fault in and above a kernel stack's guard page, in a forked child, and check the panic. Two more check that faults the handler recovers from, a page-cache window fault that the fill serves and a user write that becomes `SIGSEGV`, note no fault for a panic's report, which a later plain panic would show. Four rows (`cow_race_rows`) give `cow_fault` the entry a second thread's write fault finds after another fault changed it: made writable, made absent or its table gone, each answered -1 so the resolution runs again, and a present read-only page that is not copy-on-write, answered 0. Three rows (`fault_race_rows`) run the real write-protecting walk (`paging_wrprotect_where`) from a hook of the page-table lock, between a shared store's mark and its entry: `shared_fault`'s `install_entry` installs nothing and `shared_mkwrite` leaves the page read-only, each answering -1 because the walk moved the space's count of zaps, and a walk over two regions of one space takes write access from both with one flush. The give-way rows (`give_way_rows`) free an address space of 1,000 page tables: `free_user_pages` gives way after each table, 1,000 times, and `free_page_directory` once, after the 512th table.
`devmap_hosttest.c` and its x86-64 wrapper also copy through model kernel mappings of a shared file page. A process-memory write marks the held frame before copying, a second write marks it again with the PTE already writable, a refused mark copies nothing, and every hold comes back.

* `devmap_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/paging.c` with `rach2/sys/mm/vm.c` and a page directory of the test's own, a run of leaf tables over a window of user space, so that `devmap64_hosttest.c` runs the same rows with x86-64's `cpu.h`. Two objects stand for a driver's: a range of a device's frames, mapped uncached, and frames the allocator counts, mapped write-combining. Rows check that a fault maps each object's page with its cache bits and its region's rights, taking a reference on a counted frame and none on a device's; that a private mapping is refused; that fork shares the pages and the region's file and leaves out a region marked MADV_DONTFORK; that munmap and exit drop the pages' and the regions' references and the object's reference once, with the last region; and that `paging_zap_device` unmaps a range of an object's pages in every address space and no other object's, after which a moved page faults in at its new frame and a revoked one raises SIGBUS with BUS_ADRERR from the page fault handler, while an object that cannot give the page yet sends no signal and leaves the access to run again; and that `paging_access` reaches a device region through its object's `access`, at the object's byte, a page at a time, its refusal EIO.
* `pin_hosttest.c` and `mmnotify_hosttest.c` include `tests/host/mm_model.h`, which includes `rach2/sys/mm/paging.c` over a model of the page tables: a user directory of leaf tables over a window of user space, the MMIO region's tables made whole as the boot makes them, each with a directory entry that a 2 MiB page replaces on x86-64, and 2048 frames of host memory, which faults and copies reach through the direct map on x86-64 and the temporary slots on i686. `pin64_hosttest.c` and `mmnotify64_hosttest.c` run the same rows with x86-64's `cpu.h`. `pin_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/mm/pin.c` and `rach2/sys/pci.c` with it. Rows check that a pin of a user range brings its pages in and pins their frames, charged to VmPin; that a pin past RLIMIT_MEMLOCK answers ENOMEM and one of an empty, wrapping or unmapped range or a write pin of a read-only one is refused, all pinning nothing, while a privileged process has no limit; that a pin of an object's frames refuses a frame the allocator does not count; that the frame KVM shares with another program (`paging_user_ram_frame`) is given for an ordinary writable page and refused for a memfd_secret page and once the address space has ended, and that a run of them read in one walk (`paging_user_ram_frames`) crosses a leaf table's end and a region's and stops at the first page refused, a present, writable page of a memfd_secret region, which only the walk's look at the region it enters refuses, and a page past the last region; that a fork copies a pinned private page for the child and a pin of a page shared copy-on-write takes a copy first; that an unmap of a pinned page and the end of the address space tell the holder once and drop the frames and the charge, and leave a pin charged to no one; that the end of the address space leaves a pin whose holder keeps it (`PIN_KEEP_PAST_END`) live and untold, with its frame pinned after the mapping goes until `unpin`, and drops only its charge, a kept pin of a BAR as well, while a BAR pin that is not kept goes; that such a pin holds a shared mapping of a disk file its range meets once, over two regions, which a pin that does not keep never holds and an unmap lets go, so that the file's pages stay the file's and pinned once the regions go, until `unpin` releases the mapping; that mlock's regions split, merge and count in VmLck, lose their lock in a fork's copy, and that a locked stack grows only within RLIMIT_MEMLOCK. Its MMIO rows size a BAR with memory decoding off and map it in windows that are freed and used again: on i686 std-vga's 16 MiB BAR at 0xFD000000 in 4 KiB pages, on x86-64 a 4 GiB BAR at 14 TiB in windows of 2 MiB pages and one, 4 KiB into the BAR, of both sizes. `mmnotify_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/mm/notify.c` and `rach2/sys/mm/pressure.c`, over caches that give nothing back. Rows check that a range is called for each change that overlaps it with the part they share, and not for one outside it or in another address space; that a range may unregister itself in its call; that the end of the address space calls each range with MMN_RELEASE; that a fork, a copy-on-write copy and a device's taking back of its pages are told, the copy and the taking back counted under way across the notice (`mmn_change_begin`) and the fork, under the write lock, not; and that reclaim below the low mark asks the shrinkers, the last registered first, for what the caches did not give, until the high mark, saying whether they may sleep. Its lock rows, which `--lock-only` runs alone, check that two faults of one page interleaved across a file's fill, which lets the regions' lock go, map the page once and free the frame of the fault that lost, and that a device's page taken back during the device's call is asked for again before it is mapped; that the first fault of an object that sets `whole` maps its region of 70 pages in a batch of 64 and one of 6, up to the object's end when it ends early, that a zap during the second batch's calls leaves the region unmapped and a region cut short during them has what remains of it mapped; and that a zap walk that waits for one space's lock, while another space it has zapped faults the object back in, zaps that space again.
* `tlb_hosttest.c` and `tlb64_hosttest.c` run `mm_model.h`, whose shootdown counts each request and keeps its range, on i686's and x86-64's `cpu.h`. Rows check that a page faulted in or mapped where none was costs no shootdown and one mapped over a present page costs one; that an unmap costs one shootdown for the call, of the span unmapped up to 33 pages and of every user translation past that, with no page present and no frame freed before it, and every frame freed after it; that an unmap of nothing costs none; and that an mprotect of eight pages and a fork, for the parent's pages it makes copy-on-write, cost one each.
* `kheap_hosttest.c` includes the kernel heap, `rach2/sys/mm/kheap.c`, over 260 MiB of host memory, with the frames a counter and the page tables a map of the mapped pages. Most rows give the heap 8192 pages with a reserve of 4096 and check that single pages stop at the reserve; that a scattered region still gives sixteen runs of 256 pages from the reserve and no more; that a large `kmalloc` takes a reserve run, and its free or a shortage of frames gives the run back; the guarded runs of kernel stacks, and a stack's run given back, which waits with its guard for the next guarded run of its length; and the cache's pages, taken from the other end. Other rows check `kheap_size_for`, an eighth of memory on x86-64 between a floor of 256 MiB and a cap of 512 GiB, and a heap given the whole region.
* `mm_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/mm.c` over a model of the paging layer that counts what it frees, and checks an address space's two counts: users, whose last put frees the pages, the directory and the regions, and holds, such as a `/proc/PID/mem` descriptor's, which keep only the record. Rows check that a holder takes a user's count only while a user remains; that a thread's end switches to the kernel's directory only as the last user; and, over a model of the file system's part, the program's file: shared by a fork's copy, released by the last end under the release's lock once the space is off the holder count's list, and not kept when the heap has no room. Its lock rows, which `--lock-only` runs alone, check a thread's holds of the regions' locks: a hold taken again only counts, one read hold alone may be let go, a write wanted by a reader is refused, a second address space has a record of its own, and files are reaped only once no hold is left.
* `vm_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/mm/vm.c`, the regions of an address space. Rows check `vm_insert` and its refusal of empty, unaligned and overlapping ranges; `vm_find` and `vm_overlaps`; the four cases of `vm_remove`; stack growth down to a limit, short of another region and within an address-space limit; an array grown past its first size and copied; the merge of neighbours; `vm_protect`'s splits and merges and its refusal of a gap and of write on a region marked never writable; `vm_find_free` and `vm_covered`; and regions that map a file, with the file page each piece starts at through splits and trims, a merge only where the pages continue, a reference per region, and a release left for `vm_file_reap`, which `vm_file_dead` reports; `--dead-only` runs that row alone. It also links `rach2/sys/mm/regions.c` and puts a change of the regions made through it, a mapping replaced as MAP_FIXED replaces it, against another thread that comes in while the pages go, over a model of the regions' lock: a fault at an address of the range, which must find a region there, and a reader of /proc/PID/status, which must find the size unchanged, each of which waits for the change and runs once it lets the lock go, and finds the gap, failing the row, when the change holds the big lock alone; `--regions-only` runs those two rows alone. A further row removes a mapped file's region through `regions_unmap` and checks that the region holds the file while the entries are cleared and lets it go for `vm_file_reap` only after; `--unmap-only` runs it alone.
* `wait_queue_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/wait_queue.c` with a stand-in process record, which reaches its static `enqueue` and `dequeue`. Rows check that both are idempotent; that `wq_wake_all` makes each of up to 64 blocked waiters ready, leaves a running one alone and empties the queue; that waiters keep the order they came in, so a wake makes the oldest ready first and a waiter that joins again goes behind the older ones and ahead of a later one; and that a wake is counted under each kind of change it is given, data, room or hangup, and summed by `wq_wakes`, while any wake wakes every waiter. One row runs `wq_wait_event` with a model `schedule` that clears the deadline as `signal_post` does, and checks that a wait whose condition is still false arms the deadline again before it blocks again; that `schedule` fails a fifth block and ends its wait, so a row whose wait loses the test that ends it fails instead of hanging. Another gives each queue a lock that is a word the test sets and clears, and checks that every call leaves its queue's lock free, that a poller on two queues is made ready by the first wake and asks for one interrupt, to the processor it ran on, that `wq_wake`, `wq_complete` and `wq_wake_proc` send their interrupts with no queue's lock held, and that `wq_wake_all_deferred` leaves them in its caller's set. Another runs `wq_wait_event_sigflag`, the halted vCPU's wait, which must end with `EINTR` on the signal-work flag set before it or by the wake that ends its sleep, never asking `signal_pending`, while a wait of another kind goes on past the flag. The last, which the `entries` argument runs alone, files records with a wake function, as Linux's wait entries, and checks that they stay on the queue through each wake, that every plain one is called and the exclusive ones in turn until one says it woke, and that `wq_wake_free` calls each with `WQ_FREE` and takes it off, after which `wq_entry_remove` does nothing.
* `sched_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/sched.c`, `rach2/sys/proc/wait_queue.c` and `rach2/sys/proc/rseq.c` over seven process records, with the interrupt flag, the floating-point save and restore and the stack switch replaced by stand-ins that check interrupts are off. Rows check plain rotation; a woken sleeper that runs before the next ready process, once; five spinning processes that each run within fourteen dispatches while two others are woken at every one; wake marks, the states selection passes over, ready-list removal and preemption deferred to the end of a system call; a thread's `sched_out`, called with interrupts off while the thread is still current, not when selection keeps a SCHED_FIFO thread over ordinary or equal ones, once, preempted, when a SCHED_RR thread gives way to an equal one, and once, not preempted, when a thread blocks; a processor's tick, which counts the global ticks it has not counted and charges a tick in ring 3 as user time, but charges the ticks due before a return to user mode or an idle wait's halt, which interrupts kept off in the kernel held back, as system time, or as idle when the halt follows the end of an earlier wait, unless the interrupt's average delay would bring them after that point; the load average's sample, which keeps its period's phase when made late and folds the samples of a gap of three periods at once; an idle machine, every processor online halted in an idle wait; unchanged selection work after 256 blocked records are added; repeated wakes and removals; two `sched_wake_blocked` calls on one blocked record, the first of which queues it at the tail, marks it woken, asks for an interrupt to the processor whose thread it outranks and sets that thread's `need_resched`, and the second of which asks for none and leaves it ahead of a record made ready after the first, while a running or a stopped record is left as it was; a `sched_wake_signal`, which clears the deadline and marks the record woken, and a `sched_wake_due`, which wakes and marks a sleeper only once its deadline has come, neither moving a ready record; affinity transfers and stealing from another processor's ready list; a remote affinity change that waits for its running target, a later request that permits the original processor and a target that exits while the caller waits, then the real rseq return on the moved thread, with its CPU fields and concurrency ID; and, on a modeled monotonic clock, a wait on the run queue of 2^32 + 10^6 microseconds counted whole in `run_delay` with the switch in `pcount`, none for a wait begun before the scheduler was on, a wait kept across a move to another processor's queue and counted when an idle processor steals the thread, and a suspend's hold, which adds the wait so far, counts none while it lasts and starts every queued thread's wait again; and the tick's slice: a thread a yield switched in 100 us before a tick keeps the processor, so the yielder waits the whole of its run, as KVM's steal_time selftest needs, a thread 1 ns short of 0.75 ms keeps it and one that has run 0.75 ms gives way, a woken thread takes the processor at the tick at once, and so does a real-time thread made ready with no wake's mark; within the slice an equal SCHED_RR peer waits for the slice, an ordinary thread a wake marked under the RR pair lets no tick rotate it, a raise of the queued peer asks the running one to switch, not itself, and the tick switches, and a fall of the running one below the queued one asks the running one itself, and the tick switches back; with two processors, a SCHED_FIFO thread of priority 50 woken where one of 99 runs goes to processor 0, which runs an ordinary thread, asks that thread to switch and takes the processor at its request, and goes there too when processor 1's thread is of priority 10 but pinned there; pinned to processor 1, the 50 queues behind the 99, an affinity that keeps processor 1 leaves it there, and processor 0's tick within an ordinary slice takes it from processor 1's queue, while an affinity move onto processor 0 asks its ordinary thread to switch; and with a third processor running priority 10 and a thread of priority 90 queued on processor 0, the 50 goes to the third, since a processor ranks by the highest real-time priority of its running and queued threads; each return of an idle wait that a loopback drain or ready work cancels, which takes a pending interrupt through its one-instruction window and returns with interrupts off and no halt, the window opened before the idle marker for a drain under the big lock or under a tried one, which it lets go, and for ready work found before the marker, and opened marked idle for ready work found after the marker and for a sleep that a wake declines while the tick stops; the model's interrupt flag lets a window open unmarked only where a row expects a return before the marker; an idle task kicked while awake after an interrupt ended its halt, whose wait opens its one-instruction interrupt window while still marked idle and does not halt, and one not kicked, which halts; and two busy processors whose ticks choose one idle processor with a stopped tick, which kick it once, the first claiming its idle task's `need_resched`.
* `leave_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86/leave.c`, the work of a return to user mode that i686's, x86-64's and the i386 entry's returns run, with the interrupt flag, the big lock, the delivery of signals and the rseq update the test's own, and posts a signal as another processor would in the middle of a handler frame's copy or the rseq area's, which the kernel makes with interrupts on. Rows check that a SIGKILL posted during a frame's copy, during the rseq copy, and during the rseq copy after a frame ends the thread before the return, a traced thread's too; that two signals pending at once, and a second posted during the first's frame, each get a frame before the return, the second above the first, as Linux; that a signal the handler blocks, posted during its own frame, stays pending for the sigreturn and sends no work round; that a tick's signal gets its frame; that a tracer's attachment delivers once and keeps WORK_SIGNAL and WORK_TRACE for the next return; and that every return ends with interrupts off, the lock given back and the call mark put back. `leave64_hosttest.c` runs the same rows with x86-64's `cpu.h`.
* `smp_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/smp.c` and runs as processor 0, with three other processors as records of `cpu_table` and an interprocessor interrupt of the test's own that counts what each processor is sent and answers at once. Rows check that a TLB shootdown interrupts a processor running a thread of the directory, marks it stale on an idle processor that kept it loaded, and leaves one in another directory alone; that the idle processor gives the directory up before it is freed; that a stop waits a second only for a processor that does not answer, and then sends an NMI to that processor alone and waits 10 ms more; that a processor stops by the interrupt's real `smp_service` or the NMI's real `smp_stop_nmi`, run as that processor, and runs `k_panic_hook`, the virtualization cleanup, before it is marked stopped; that `smp_call` runs its function on the caller's own processor at once with interrupts off, which come back on after it, and on another processor in that processor's `smp_service`, whose slot still names the function while it runs and is clear once the wait ends; that the wait for a processor that goes offline instead of answering ends with the function not run; that the boot counts no processor that did not come, nor one that comes late; and that the profiler's sample of a hold of the big lock walks only the interrupted thread's kernel stack, stopping at a saved frame pointer above it. A wait that spins 1000 turns of the model's `arch_relax` since the last interrupt sent fails the run and is ended, so that a wait that lost the test ending it fails instead of stalling the suite.
* `process_timer_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/process.c` with every export but the tick's made private, a model `signal_post` that counts each signal and wakes no one, and model run queues whose lock counts its takes and whose wake makes a blocked process ready. Over a blocked process ahead of a running one, each with a timer, rows check that the pass marks a due timer for a holder of the big lock, under the run queues' lock, without posting it or arming it again, and that a second pass marks it no second time; that the post sends `SIGALRM` to either and stops the timer, which keeps its interval and the tick it was due and posts nothing more until a `SIGALRM` is taken, or leaves it disarmed, and one not yet due is not marked; that a marked or stopped timer reports no due tick (`proc_alarm_due_tick`), so getitimer and alarm read no time left; that a take (`proc_alarm_taken`) runs a stopped timer again at the first tick of its interval's grid after then, an interval of one tick included, and a second take moves nothing, and that a `SIGALRM` posted and taken seven ticks late runs it at the next tick of its grid; that a setting made between the mark and the post posts the marked `SIGALRM`, and the post sends no second; that a thread's own return posts its `SIGVTALRM` and `SIGPROF` once and leaves the list; that a sleeper wakes at its deadline tick, marked woken by the model's wake (the kernel's `sched_wake_due` is `sched_hosttest.c`'s), and not before, while a blocked process with no deadline and a running process are left alone; that twenty sleepers due at once, more than one hold wakes, are all woken by one pass, which still reaches a timer linked behind them; and that timers and sleepers are served across 2^32 ticks, where the low half of the 64-bit count wraps; `proc_alarm_at` moves a tick of 0 to 1. Rows also check, through `proc_wake_due` and a model clock, step count and interrupt between ticks (`step_rows`), that a sleep to a time of real time waits while the clock's step count stays, wakes at the next tick after a setting of the clock, before its deadline, and that a sleep on time since boot stays asleep; and (`between_rows`) that a sleep with a time of the monotonic clock has the interrupt between ticks armed for it at every pass, more than two ticks from the time too, whatever its deadline tick, that an interrupt before the time arms it again, and that the interrupt at the time, or a tick whose clock has passed it, makes it ready before its deadline tick; and (`restart_rows`) that a sleeper the pass collected, which the model's arming for the record behind it moves to a later wait before the wake, keeps that wait's deadline, since the wake tests the record again (`timer_wake`), and is woken at its new time.
* `futex_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/futex.c` over user memory modelled as a byte array, with a write fault that stands for a sleep in which another thread runs or ticks pass. Rows check that `get_robust_list` reports the head of a thread that ends during the copy out, which AddressSanitizer judges by the freed record; that a thread's end releases a robust futex with `FUTEX_WAITERS` and `FUTEX_OWNER_DIED` and wakes the waiter, with the wake's interrupt sent after the bucket's lock, reading the word again when another processor changed it; what the end does with a futex it was taking or giving back; that the `CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID` word is cleared only when another thread shares the address space; that a wait's deadline counts the ticks a fault took; that an untimed wait keeps ERESTARTSYS and files no restart block, and that a timed wait files its restart block and `futex_wait_restart` resumes it to the first deadline, including when only one tick remains; and that a wait to a time of real time follows a setting of the clock in the wait or in the word's read, sleeping on after a step back and ending with `ETIMEDOUT` after a step forward past the time, and that its restart counts its tick from the clock as set; and that a wait whose tick comes before the clock reaches its time, as the clock read between ticks can be behind the tick count, counts its tick again and ends with `ETIMEDOUT` after a second wait.
* `ns_limits_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/ns.c` and `rach2/sys/fs/vfs_ns.c` over a mount table of the test's, whose unmount lets a record go as the kernel's does, and makes namespaces through `ns_fork`. Rows check 1000 mount namespaces at once with a mount each, every one seeing the boot's mounts and its own alone; a namespace made from the 500th seeing its mount; a mount hidden from one namespace; every mount and namespace id gone with the namespaces; `user.max_mnt_namespaces` as Linux derives it from memory, ENOSPC past it, and a user namespace's own limit counted per user; an id map of 340 extents written in descending order, read back sorted and mapped both ways; a 341st extent and an overlap refused with EINVAL; and parent ids across two extents of a map of five and of one of 340 refused with EPERM.
* `exit_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/exit.c` and `rach2/sys/proc/wait_queue.c` and runs `proc_exit_cleanup` with close and directory callbacks that wait on the real queue, and a scheduler stand-in that releases the contended lock only once the dying process is blocked on it. Six cases, with and without files, a directory and a parent, check the order of the end: the futexes; the address space, once, leaving the processor in the kernel's directory; the files; the directories; a `vfork` parent let go once; the children adopted; the timers aimed at the thread let go and the zombie published together (the stand-in for `ptimer_thread_exit`); and only then `SIGCHLD`, with the wait status kept and no waiter left.
* `elf_hosttest.c` includes the ELF loader, `rach2/sys/proc/elf.c`, with the page and mapping calls stubbed, and loads images built in a buffer through a read callback. Rows refuse with `ENOEXEC`, before a page is taken, a short header, a bad magic number, too many program headers, a table past the image, a segment larger in the file than in memory, and one outside the user window or wrapping past 2^32; a valid image whose page cannot be had answers `ENOMEM`, and a failed read `EIO`. Other rows load a position-independent image at a base and refuse a bad base or interpreter path, read an image as an interpreter, run a load inside another's read callback, check `elf_check`'s bounds on the program header table, and check that a segment's region with write or execute may also be read.
* `uimage_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/proc/uimage.c`, which builds a new image's initial stack, over a pool of host pages for frames and a table for the page directory, and reads each stack back through that table. Rows check stacks on one page and on many: argc, the pointer arrays, the strings, the auxiliary vector, the 16-byte alignment and the zeroed bytes below the stack pointer. Others check the budget, a quarter of the stack limit from 128 KiB to 6 MiB; a string of 128 KiB with its NUL and one a byte longer; an unreadable string and one that grew after it was measured; `argv[0]` dropped for a script; and a failed frame or mapping. After each row every frame is mapped or given back.
* `tls_hosttest.c` runs `tls_resolve_base` of `rach2/sys/arch/i686/tls.c`, which decodes the `user_desc` that `set_thread_area` takes. Rows accept the request musl's `__set_thread_area` makes, entry -1 with flags 0x51, and an explicit entry 6, and return its base; and refuse another entry, a segment that is not 32-bit, a code segment, a read-only one, one not present, and null arguments.
* `tls64_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/tls.c` with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` defined, over a model of the FS, GS and kernel GS base registers and the FS and GS selectors. Rows check that `ARCH_SET_FS`, `ARCH_SET_GS` and clone's `CLONE_SETTLS` refuse a base at or above `USER_ADDR_MAX` with `EPERM` and change neither the process record nor a register, and take 0 and the last user address below it; that `ARCH_SET_FS` and `ARCH_SET_GS` load the null selector into their own register before they write the base; that `ARCH_SET_GS` writes the kernel GS base register and not the kernel's own; that `ARCH_GET_FS` and `ARCH_GET_GS` read the registers, not the record; and that an address that cannot be written is `EFAULT`, another code `EINVAL` and a call with no process `ESRCH`.
* `ia32_signal_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/sigframe32.c` and `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/ptrace.c` with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` and `FPU_X86_64` defined, over a model of user memory below 4 GiB, the selectors, `lar` and the floating-point frame calls. Rows check the i386 `sigcontext_32`, `ucontext_ia32`, `rt_sigframe_ia32`, `sigframe_ia32` and `user_regs_struct32` and `struct user32`'s debug registers against Linux 6.12's sizes and offsets; the i386 siginfo's union at byte 12 for a kill, a queued value, `SIGCHLD`, a fault's address and a seccomp `SIGSYS`, and a queued one read back; the rt frame 4 bytes past a 16-byte boundary below its floating-point state, the handler's EIP, EAX, EDX and ECX, CS and SS, DF and TF cleared, the frame's words and the return through `rt_sigreturn` with the registers the handler left, R8 untouched and a kernel CS refused; the plain frame's own return code, the vDSO's when there is one, and its mask in `oldmask` and `extramask`; `SA_ONSTACK`'s frame and `uc_stack`; a frame that would reach 4 GiB refused; and an i386 tracer's `PTRACE_GETREGS` and `PTRACE_PEEKUSER`, `EIO` past `struct user32` or unaligned, and the register sets of a 32-bit and a 64-bit tracee.
* `fpuframe_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86/fpu.c` with the part that drives the unit left out, sets the save format by hand, and checks the floating-point images Linux's interfaces show, in i686's layouts. Rows check a signal frame's XSAVE state: its alignment and place below the stack pointer, the legacy part, Linux's software bytes and `FP_XSTATE_MAGIC2`, and the x87 part before it; a frame taken back whole, or with x87 and SSE alone when its magic words or sizes are wrong; frames refused for a bad header, a reserved MXCSR bit or a fault; the AVX-512 layout; the `NT_X86_XSTATE` image and one a tracer sets; and without XSAVE, the frames of the `fxsave` and `fnsave` formats.
* `fpuframe64_hosttest.c` defines `FPU_X86_64` and includes `fpuframe_hosttest.c`, so the same rows check x86-64's layouts: the signal frame has no x87 part before its image, the state without XSAVE is the `fxsave64` image, and the rows of the `fnsave` format and of the x87 part's control word are left out.
* `debugreg_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86/debugreg.c` with the privileged register instructions replaced by arrays of registers for two processors. Rows check that a processor clears a detached tracee's hardware enables on a switch and turns a stale hit into no trap, keeping a single step taken with it; that a user hit is `TRAP_HWBKPT` with its DR6; and that a kernel hit, a kernel single step and a hit at the system-call entry, with or without a DR6 status bit, are dropped: DR6 and the processor's enables are cleared, the thread's DR6 is kept, and the return to user mode loads the enables again, also after a switch or a migration, but not after a detach. debugreg.c refers to no signal function, so the link proves that nothing is posted.
* `pstore_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/pstore/ram_core.c`, the persistent RAM zones of ramoops, over zones in host memory. Rows encode blocks of 1 to 128 bytes with 2, 4, 16 and 32 parity bytes, check that each is a code word of Linux's Reed-Solomon code for ramoops (symbols of 8 bits, polynomial 0x11d, first root 1), and that the decoder mends up to half the parity's count of symbols, the word's last among them, and counts them. Others check a zone's header as Linux lays it out, a wrap, a write longer than the zone, the copy a second boot makes, oldest byte first, a zone emptied after the copy, a header out of range or of another signature emptied, and, with Linux's `ecc=1`, the count of blocks, ten damaged bytes in the data and the header mended across a reset, nine in one block counted as unrecoverable, and the notice Linux appends to the record, cut to the buffer.
* `ptrace_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/ptrace.c` with a scheduler that reports a stop and resumes the tracee. Rows check that a register-set request, either way, answers `ESRCH` without reading the registers when the tracee resumed during its sleeping iovec copy; that `PTRACE_LISTEN` takes a group stop and `PTRACE_INTERRUPT` releases a listening tracee; that listen and interrupt requests repeated during the stop on the way back to user mode stop the tracee before it returns, and `SIGKILL` ends that sequence; that `PTRACE_SETOPTIONS` and `PTRACE_SEIZE` refuse options past 32 bits; that a single step over a system call reports `TRAP_TRACE` with no system-call mark; and, with namespace stubs that translate an id differently for tracer and tracee, that a trace stop's `SIGCHLD` has the tracee's IDs as the tracer sees them, that a signal the tracer resumes with at a signal-delivery or system-call stop has the tracer's IDs as the tracee sees them, and that the `SIGCHLD` of a tracee's end is made for the tracer. Child-notification rows check CLONE_UNTRACED, a zero-signal child's wake without a signal, parent exec conversion, and generation wrap.
* `coredump_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/proc/coredump.c` and writes a core file into memory from a model address space whose array of regions the writer replaces with a longer one partway through, freeing the old, as another process sharing the space can with `mmap`. Rows check that the program headers describe each region once, as it was taken, with no read of the freed array; that a page the process has is written, an untouched anonymous page is a hole, and an untouched page of a mapped file is read from the file; the wait for the other threads to park, a thread's parking and `core_release`; and a count of program headers too large for the ELF header, given as `PN_XNUM` with the count in a section header.
* `corefile_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` for `fs_core_file`, which writes the file `core` in the working directory, with `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c`, a model mounted type, and a model of `core_notes` and `core_write`, since `core_notes` reads registers at 32-bit kernel addresses a 64-bit host does not have. While the dump waits for the other threads, a process that shares the descriptor table writes through the core file's descriptor and duplicates it: both answer `EBADF` and nothing reaches the file. With a limit that ends before the zero that gives the file its length, the call answers `EFBIG` and writes no zero; with one byte more, it writes it. The append rows of `corefile_hosttest.c` lose a source page after a write and between vector elements: neither call may release its file lock after moving bytes. A call that moved none may fault and resume, checking the new end against RLIMIT_FSIZE. A writev to a TCP stream whose second element fails with EPIPE returns the first's count with no SIGPIPE, and one whose first fails posts it.
* `syscall_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/syscall.c` and `rach2/sys/syscall_net.c` with the calls no row makes made private, user memory a checked byte array, and models of the socket, the interrupt mask, the scheduler and the timer tick. Rows check a connect that completes or is refused before or during its wait, blocking and not; a stream's sendmsg, which posts SIGPIPE only when its first element fails and not under MSG_NOSIGNAL, returns the first element's count when the second fails, and sends a vector of no bytes as a send of 0 bytes; a shutdown of a raw IPv4 or IPv6 socket, which takes the halves and is ENOTCONN with no peer, as Linux's inet_shutdown, and of a packet socket, which is EOPNOTSUPP and takes nothing; the clocks, read across 2^32 ticks, their resolutions and the ids refused; `clock_nanosleep` on each clock, relative and absolute; and `nanosleep`, `setitimer`, `getitimer` and `alarm`, with the interrupt mask at each step, the tick a sleep ends on and a real timer's nanosecond expiry, the remainder a signal leaves, the clamps of long times and the refusal of bad arguments; and an absolute `clock_nanosleep` on `CLOCK_REALTIME` across a setting of the clock in the sleep or between its reading of the clock and its block, which sleeps on after a step back and ends at the next tick after a step forward past the time, and which counts the clock's offset to the nanosecond, so that a step of half a second either way moves its tick by half a second, and an absolute sleep on `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` whose tick comes while the clock reads a microsecond short of the time sleeps a tick more. `sleep_between` checks that a sleep of a quarter tick, and one of a tick and a quarter whose deadline tick is two ahead, arm the interrupt between ticks for their time and end at it, before their deadline tick, that a signal pending as a sleep of a quarter tick blocks leaves a remainder of the quarter tick to its end, that one seen as the clock reaches the sleep's end, or as the tick count reaches its deadline tick where there is no interrupt between ticks, returns 0, writes no remainder and counts no sleep cut short, and that a sleep of 10,000,000,000 s keeps no time between ticks. Rows also check that a real-time timer whose `SIGALRM` the tick has marked (`alarm_due`), or whose post has stopped it, reads no time left with its interval in getitimer, and 0 from alarm.
* `ia32_ioctl_hosttest.c` includes the x86-64 kernel's compat ioctl path, `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/ia32_ioctl.c`, `ia32_ioctl_drm.c` and `ia32_ioctl_gpu.c`, whole, with the caller's memory an arena and the native `ioctl_fd` a model that records the number, the argument and the window it was handed. Rows make i386 calls with Linux 6.12's i386 layouts: ESRCH, EBADF for a closed or O_PATH descriptor and ENOTTY for a type no family takes; the families that pass, with the argument unchanged; FS_IOC32_* resized; HDIO_GETGEO, BLKGETSIZE and the read-ahead reads in 32 bits, with EFBIG; LOOP_SET_STATUS and LOOP_GET_STATUS through the 64-bit forms, with EOVERFLOW; EVIOCGBIT and the other bit sets cut to the compat length; the console's AUDIO_WRITE; KVM's dirty log; the ifreq requests in 32 bytes on a socket, SIOCGIFCONF's entries of 32 bytes from native ones of 40, with no buffer and with a negative length, and the routes of AF_INET and AF_INET6; and the DRM core's VERSION, GET_UNIQUE, GET_CLIENT, GET_STATS, SET_UNIQUE, WAIT_VBLANK, ADDFB2 and GETFB2, with what a failed call writes back.

### Descriptors, files and file systems

* `fd_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` and includes `rach2/sys/proc/process.c`, over a heap that can refuse the Nth allocation. Rows fail each allocation of the standard descriptions and of a new process and require nothing left allocated, published or counted as a fork; hold each of two processes that share a table to its own `RLIMIT_NOFILE`; copy a table with a reserved slot; in a shared table, find no descriptor in a reservation until `fd_install` publishes it, defer a close of a descriptor another sharer's call holds and claim its release at that call's end (`fd_close_claim`, `fd_holds_claim`), replace such a descriptor by `dup2` with a new entry while the holder's call keeps the old one through its number, release the old one at the end of the last call that keeps it and lead the number back to its slot once the new entry closes (`fd_dup_target`, `fd_gone_claim`), and let the table's lock go after every call; grow a table by chunks that leave every entry where it was; decide the last reference of a description in one step (`fd_ofd_unref_unless_last`); check the `ENOMEM`, `EMFILE` and `EBADF` of allocation and growth; check `fd_ofd_holders` and the order of the description and namespace locks while a node moves; refuse a remount read-only of a base while a description writes through a bind of it and of a bind only for its own descriptions, `EBUSY`, and refuse a writer, `EROFS`, while it runs and through the read-only base after (`fd_mount_readonly_begin`), over a `vfs_mount_base` that follows a bind; and take the big lock at a description's last end for inotify only while a watch is on its own file system (`ofd_end_big`), which a counting `bkl_enter` of the test's own shows. The table's spinlock is `compat/spinlock_host.c`'s, which stops the test on a lock taken twice.
* `lock_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/lock.c` whole, with a wait that runs at each sleep what the row's other processes do meanwhile. Rows check record locks (conflicts, `F_GETLK`, splits and joins, locks to the end of the file, and a close that drops only its descriptor table's locks on the file), open file description locks, `flock` locks and their conversion, two processes that share a table as one owner, `EDEADLK` for a cycle of waits, two threads of one description that wait together, and the locks and waits of a file that moves (`lock_repoint`).
* `open_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c`, `rach2/sys/block/loop.c` and `rach2/sys/mm/filemap.c` whole and links `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` and `rach2/sys/fs/nodelock.c`, over models of the KRFFS root, of a serialised mounted type and of a `file_locked` type that logs each lock and give; a waiting call of another process is parked by `compat/park_host.c`. Rows check an `O_TRUNC` open of a mounted file and its cleanup; a close, a `dup2` and the end of another thread's call while a release sleeps; the `ENOMEM` and `EMFILE` of `open`, `dup` and `dup2`; `unlink`, `rename`, `mkdir` and `rmdir` against an open being installed, a running program, a working directory or another process's `mkdir`; the release of a nameless file by each path a descriptor or a mapping ends on; the attribute and stat calls while a write moves the file; errors from a followed `/proc/PID/fd/N` stat and its temporary description reference; an open of `/proc/PID/fd/N` for a device opened through a device node of a mounted type while another thread closes the device's last descriptor, which must still close the device; the key of a file's locks; seeks of `/proc/PID/mem` and `/dev/bootlog`; `getdents64` past 4 GiB of a directory whose cursor is a byte, which passes on the `EIO` of a failed mount and the `ENOENT` of a removed directory from the type's `readdir` (`dir_past_end`), and `_llseek` of such a directory to its volume's largest file offset and one byte past it, `EINVAL`; the order of the node locks on the `file_locked` type in `mkdir`, `unlink`, `rmdir`, a rename within one directory, into a directory below, between unrelated directories and of a directory below itself; a mount against an `rmdir` of its target, placed while the `rmdir` looks the name up (`EBUSY`) and waiting on the target's lock (`ENOENT`); and a read of a disk file whose pages a shared mapping holds in memory when no temporary mapping is free for one, which returns the pages before it, or `ENOMEM` for the first, and never the file's stale bytes (`resident_unmapped`); a read of an ext2 file without the big lock while another thread, as the read's first page waits for the disk, maps the file shared and writable and stores into its second page, which returns the store and gives back the big lock it took, through the file system and through a model of the file page cache that honours its `cut` (`resident_race`, which `--race-only` runs alone); a read without the big lock whose end a sharer's seek put past 2^63-1, which is `EINVAL` and holds no big lock after; an open without the big lock that the name cache finishes, with descriptor 0 taken, which returns descriptor 1 and is not made again under the big lock; and `fs.protected_hardlinks` on a `link` to another user's file and `fs.protected_regular` and `fs.protected_fifos` on an `O_CREAT` open of an existing file in a sticky directory, at each level of each, with Linux's refusals and their controls.
* `pipe_ring_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/ipc/pipe_ring.c`, the byte ring of a pipe, over a ring of `PIPE_BUF_SIZE` bytes. Rows fill and empty the ring whole, clamp a put to the free space and a get to the bytes held, keep the order of a transfer across the end of the array, and check the read and write spans a caller copies through directly: none crosses the end of the array, and they agree with the byte calls.
* `pipe_copy_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/ipc/pipe.c`, `pipe_ring.c` and `rach2/sys/mm/usercopy.c` whole, over model user pages whose resolver starts another reader, writer or close before it returns a missing or copy-on-write page; a reader or writer waits for the pipe's lock, which the model gives it when the holder gives the lock back, and a close runs at once. Rows check that a read or a write holds the pipe across its resolve, as Linux holds `pipe->mutex`, so a read of a vector returns adjacent buffers while another reader waits and a buffer no append grows while a writer waits; competing reads and writes across the ring's end and the wrap of its counters, EOF, `EAGAIN`, `EINTR`, `EPIPE`, a read that faults on a later page of a buffer, which is EFAULT with the buffer kept, as Linux copies a buffer whole, a read of a vector whose first buffer faults and a read into NULL, which an empty pipe answers with `EAGAIN` and an ended one with 0 first, a write of less than a page that a fault or a close while its later page is resolved ends with no byte added, as Linux commits a page whole, the empty buffer a fault leaves, which wakes the readers, polls in, takes a later small write and goes once a read reaches it, as Linux's `pipe_write` leaves it, a tee or a splice from an ended empty pipe, which is `EPIPE` with SIGPIPE into a pipe with no reader and 0 into one with a reader, as Linux's `link_pipe` checks the output's readers first, and that a read wakes the writers for room, a write the readers for data and a close the other end for a hangup. No reader spins for the next write and no waiter for the lock's holder (`timer_cycles_agree`, `compat/spin_none.c`), so the rows run a splice or a tee between pipes as they would under the big lock, and do not tell the path without it, which differs in that spin alone, from the old one.
* `sysv_detach_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/ipc/sysv.c` with a model unmapper. Rows check an unaligned address, a search that finds no attachment, an unmap failure returned without searching later slots or changing metadata or reaping segments, and a successful detach that records its time and last operator and reaps a removed segment once.
* `unix_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/ipc/unix.c`, `pipe.c`, `pipe_ring.c`, `rach2/sys/proc/mutex.c` and `rach2/sys/mm/usercopy.c` whole, over models of a shared descriptor table, socket files and eight user pages; where a copy, the making or lookup of a socket file, or a last release sleeps, another thread's call runs by `compat/park_host.c`. Rows check that `accept`, `socketpair`, a receive of `SCM_RIGHTS` and `pipe2` reserve their descriptors while they copy out; that a `connect` which sleeps while another thread connects the socket fails with `EISCONN`; that a second `bind` or receive waits while the first sleeps, then fails with `EINVAL` or takes the next message; that a send whose copy sleeps through a `shutdown` fails with `EPIPE`; that a nameless KRFFS file whose descriptor is in flight, moved or not, is held, installed and freed once; and that a sendmsg or recvmsg vector whose lengths pass MAX_RW_COUNT together is cut there, its last element shortened, so that a datagram send fails with `EMSGSIZE` and a stream receive takes what is queued, not `EINVAL`. A whole-vector write keeps one datagram and copies a stream piece before publishing any of its elements, even when a sibling writes during that copy. The `vector-write` argument runs those two rows alone. A close begun just after the sender pins an SCM_RIGHTS source cannot mark the received descriptor closing; `rights-close` runs that row alone. `mutex-signal` runs only the new rows in which a contended interruptible mutex judges a real or ignored signal under the big lock, with the kernel's mutex code. The `timeo` rows move the model's clock and queue bytes while the call's own thread sleeps, and read the deadline each wait arms: a signal in a wait under SO_RCVTIMEO, or in the wait for the bind lock, is EINTR never restarted, and one in a wait with no limit is left to the dispatcher; a receive keeps the limit it began with when another is set while its copy sleeps; a named datagram send to a full queue keeps the SO_SNDTIMEO it had before its lookup of the path when a sibling takes the limit off while the lookup sleeps, and ends with EAGAIN at the limit; a wait after a copy that sleeps 250 ticks has the 150 its first wait left; a wait woken by a signal a sibling took sleeps on to its deadline and ends with EAGAIN; and a MSG_WAITALL receive whose bytes come 50 ticks apart ends at its 200-tick limit with four. `timeo` runs those rows alone. Its copy of `syscall_fs.c`'s `iov_import` must be kept line for line with the original.
* `evdev_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/evdev.c` whole, with the clocks read through the host's clock_gettime or fixed by a row, and reads a mouse's and a touchpad's event devices as Linux's evdev answers: EVIOCGBIT of the types, axes and buttons, EVIOCGPROP, the name and place, -ENOENT for EVIOCGUNIQ and -EINVAL for EVIOCGABS on a mouse and for a request evdev does not know; a motion read as its packet by two opens; the input core's filters, a motion of 0, codes the device lacks, a press while down and a release while up, and the SYN_REPORT of an empty packet dropped; a mouse's queue of 128 records, and an overflowed one read as SYN_DROPPED, the newest record and the packet after. EVIOCREVOKE rows check -EINVAL for an argument, -ENODEV for a revoked open's reads and ioctls, its poll's hang-up, the grab released with it, and the other open reading on. Clock rows check -EINVAL for an unknown clock, monotonic and boot-time stamps between the clock_gettime calls around them, the real time by default, one time for a whole packet on each open's clock, and a change of clock that empties a queue with records and queues a SYN_DROPPED for the next packet. Mask rows check all ones for a type with no mask and zeros past its codes, the codes and whole types a mask lets through, an emptied packet not read, a type that takes no mask, and -EFAULT. EVIOCGKEY reads the buttons down and takes their records out of the queue; the touchpad's rows read EVIOCGABS's limits and resolution, follow Linux's fuzz, drop ABS_MT_SLOT on a device with no slots and pass its other multitouch axes unfiltered, and set an axis with EVIOCSABS, its resolution 0 from a shorter structure and -EINVAL for ABS_MT_SLOT. An i386 reader on the x86-64 kernel, which a row makes `in_compat_syscall` say, reads records of 16 bytes with 32-bit seconds and microseconds, -EINVAL for 15 bytes, while an x86-64 reader goes on reading 24.
