Write rows check 0 bytes, -EINVAL for less than a record, a packet read back by every open, the writer's among them, the filters' drops, SYN_DROPPED among them, -EFAULT, the cap of 4096 bytes, the grab's holder reading alone, -ENODEV for a revoked writer and an i386 writer's 16-byte records; a keyboard's written key, LED and repeat handed to input.c, and a mouse's motion to mouse.c, unless the filters drop it or an open holds the grab. Rows give the console the keys of a power button and of a keypad, devices that are no keyboards, reported or written, keep them from it under a grab, and never give it a mouse's buttons. Reservations are invisible to opens and snapshots, exclude other registrations from their slot, publish at that slot and can be released unused. `evdev64_hosttest.c` runs the same rows with x86-64's `cpu.h`.
* `eventfd_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/io/eventfd.c` whole, with a copy to the caller that first runs a second read of the counter, as a thread sharing the descriptor can while the copy sleeps. Rows check that the first read returns the whole value and the second finds `EAGAIN`; that with `EFD_SEMAPHORE` each takes one; that a failed copy loses the value; the `EAGAIN` of a nonblocking read of zero and the `EINVAL` of a short read; and that a read wakes the queue for room alone and a write for data alone. The kernel's rows, over a model of the descriptor table, check `eventfd_get` and `eventfd_put` and their refusals of another object and of no descriptor; that `eventfd_signal` adds one, wakes the readers and tells each hook; that a hook added to a raised counter is told so; that a hook that takes the counter takes it and wakes the writers, as irqfd does; that a program's write tells the hooks as a signal does; that a hook taken off is told no more and can take the counter with it; and that the last close detaches and tells each hook, that a signal from a hangup racing the close reaches no detached hook, that a kernel holder keeps the counter after it, and that a hook added after it is hung up at once.
* `kvm_hosttest.c` checks the dirty ring's ABI and enable rules, mapping permissions, full-ring exits and refusal until reset, and the ring mapping's reference after close, and includes `rach2/sys/kvm/kvm.c` and `vcpu.c` whole and links `cpuid.c`, `msr.c`, `hyperv.c`, `pvclock.c`, `binstats.c`, `exitstat.c`, `pmu.c` and `dirty.c`, built for x86-64 with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` and with `RACH2_HOST_CPU` for a kick from another processor, over a model of the Lenovo's Haswell, a fake backend whose entry refuses or reports a script of exits, and fakes of the memory slots, interrupt routing and bus that answer their capabilities. Rows check the structures' sizes and offsets and the ioctl and capability numbers against Linux's; `/dev/kvm` registered as 10:232; QEMU 10's start-up to KVM_RUN in the order of its sources, through the required capabilities, the MSR lists with `E2BIG`, the microcode revision and IA32_DEBUGCTL on the index list, which names DEBUGCTL for both backends and the four last-branch MSRs for SVM alone, SVM's DEBUGCTL reading 0 and taking any write and its last-branch MSRs taking only the caller's 0, the revision read as the host's from the feature MSRs, and a new vCPU's at Linux's value for each backend, the supported CPUID by doubling and its masks, the emulated CPUID's three entries and its `E2BIG`, the vCPU and its shared and private mappings, the machine-check setup, the registers put and got and every MSR QEMU puts at a reset, to the backend's refusal; each exit kind through the run page, with a port and an MMIO read answered at the next KVM_RUN even under `immediate_exit`, a signal, a kick and a triple fault, the signal seen through the thread's signal-work flag and read under the big lock; KVM_RUN and its exits without the big lock, also when the system call's entry held it, which KVM_RUN gives back at the same depth, and interrupts off for each entry; `EINTR` from KVM_RUN and from a state ioctl whose wait for a vCPU that another process holds SIGKILL ends, with the entry's hold back at its depth; a guest's INIT, which keeps the APIC base whole, no `k_memset` of its reset covering it; the state ioctls' refusals that Linux makes; the kvmclock, counted from the raw clock, whose stub answers the slewed monotonic clock 7 ms apart and fails a reading of it; the Hyper-V reference TSC page, with the kvmclock off, written from a clock that KVM_SET_CLOCK moved between the run loop's `kvm_hv_prepare` and `kvm_clock_prepare`, by the second; an entry after `kvm_clock_prepare` under the master clock, when the host's clock stopped between them, sent back to the run loop, and the fast path ended, so that the next preparation writes the kvmclock without PVCLOCK_TSC_STABLE_BIT; the steal time, written from the thread's `run_delay` and marked preempted by the thread's `sched_out` across a switch at a slice's end inside KVM_RUN, but not at a halted vCPU's sleep at the run loop's top, which counts no preemption, nor where its page cannot be written without a fault nor in a region of memfd_secret, also one the guest moved the record into with the tables unchanged, before the next entry and after it, and a guest's KVM_VCPU_FLUSH_TLB in a preempted record taken with `preempted`, its padding kept, and turned into a flush of the guest's TLB at the entry; the vCPU attribute KVM_VCPU_TSC_OFFSET, read, set and matched across two vCPUs; CR0's CD and NW kept across a guest's INIT from each setting, as Linux's `kvm_vcpu_reset` keeps them; XSAVES offered on a model and a fake backend that have it, and kept out where the backend refuses it, leaf 0xD's sizes at a vCPU's XCR0, and IA32_XSS's rules; KVM_GET_XSAVE and KVM_SET_XSAVE in FXSAVE's form on a model without XSAVE, where KVM_CAP_XSAVE is 1 still and KVM_CAP_XCRS 0, and the get, from a vCPU whose image marks AVX alone and has marked bytes after the 512, gives x87 and SSE marked and the rest cleared; `kvm/exit_stats`'s rows for VMX's and SVM's exit codes, summed over two processors, with each exit's wait for the big lock through `kvm_bkl_enter`, its clear, the entry, run and exit counted only from marks in order, the preparation only for an exit the kernel handled, without a wait for the big lock inside it and as 0 when it ends on a processor whose TSC reads lower, the fast exits as `kvm_exitstat_fast` marks them, and an exit with no entry after it counted as one that ended KVM_RUN; a KVM_REQ_CLOCK_UPDATE or KVM_REQ_EVENT left after the run loop's look cancelling the entry, so that the loop takes it before the next, and a KVM_REQ_TLB_FLUSH left to the entry; what Linux's selftests ask for, as the entry for this test in `rach2/sys/kvm/Readme.md` lists them: the TSC's rate, the statistics files, KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY's results, the vCPUs' bounds, the CR4 bits before KVM_SET_CPUID2, the VM's capabilities and quirks, KVM_X86_XCOMP_GUEST_SUPP, the synchronized registers, the machine-check MSRs and each bit of HWCR from an Intel and an AMD guest, SYSCALL_MASK's bits 63:32 under SVM kept from the caller and refused from the guest, the MSR filter and the caller's MSR exits through KVM_RUN, and the XSAVE image `x86_64/state_test` moves from KVM_GET_XSAVE2 into a vCPU given no CPUID and one given an empty CPUID, with the MXCSR rules; `EIO` from another address space; and every allocation given back when the last mapping goes.
* `vfio_hosttest.c` links the files of `rach2/sys/vfio/` but `hw.c`, built for x86-64 with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64`, over a model of the Lenovo's bus in small (the host bridge, the HD 4400 with its OpRegion, an xHCI with MSI-X, a Lynx Point root port with a network card behind it, a root port without ACS with an endpoint behind it, and the LPC bridge's slot), and fakes of the IOMMU, pins, eventfds, vectors, `/sys` and `/dev` that record what VFIO asks of them. Rows check the groups as Linux's `pci_device_group` forms them, the PCH quirk among them, numbered by unit and then bus; `/dev/vfio/vfio` as 10:196 and a node for each group; `type`, `reserved_regions`, the `devices` and `iommu_group` links and `driver_override`; binding by `driver_override`, `new_id` and `drivers_probe`, refused for a device a kernel driver holds, and a group with such a device not viable unless it is a bridge; the group and container ioctls, `SET_IOMMU` refused without interrupt remapping unless the boot line allows it; `GET_INFO` with its IOVA ranges, the message window taken out and a relaxable RMRR kept, and its 8-byte aligned capabilities; `MAP_DMA` mapping each contiguous run of the pinned pages, each mapping kept past the end of the address space, and its refusals, among them a map of the same IOVA and the last group's detach made while its pin sleeps; the release of the files the pins held after the unmap, the detach, the container's close and a failed map; `UNMAP_DMA` refusing to split a mapping under v2, `UNMAP_ALL`, and a mapping whose memory the process unmapped; the regions' information, the OpRegion's type capability and `argsz`; the configuration space as the owner sees it, BAR sizing, the command word, MSI's virtual capability, ASLS and the reset bits; BAR mappings that hold the device and fault nothing with memory decoding off; the line masked at each interrupt and unmasked by ioctl or eventfd, MSI answered with one message, MSI-X entry by entry with the table's bytes kept from the owner; and the reset at the open, at `VFIO_DEVICE_RESET` with the message programmed again, and at the release after the last mapping, with every vector, eventfd and window given back.
* `kvm_vmx_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kvm/vmx.c` whole and links `kvm.c`, `vcpu.c`, `cpuid.c`, `msr.c`, `emulate.c`, `decode.c` and `hyperv.c`, built for x86-64 with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` and `RACH2_HOST_CPU`, over a model of the Lenovo's Haswell that stands in for `vmx_op`: its capability MSRs are the machine's, its VMX instructions fail as the SDM says, VMREAD and VMWRITE keep a VMCS of the fields and widths the SDM lists, and VMLAUNCH makes the entry's checks of the controls, the host state, the guest's segments, registers and events, and the MSR lists before it plays the next row of a script of exits. Rows check the probe's refusals, and the controls that load IA32_PERF_GLOBAL_CTRL left unused on Nehalem EP and Westmere EP and used on the Lenovo's Haswell; VMX on on a processor exactly while a VMCS is active there, off with CR4 back once its last VMCS is cleared and when VMXON or VMPTRLD fails, and the host's state restored and the FPU balanced after every run and every failure; the first entry a VMLAUNCH of a cleared VMCS and the next VMRESUMEs with no VMPTRLD, the VMCS left current and launched after each run; port, MMIO, CPUID, control and debug register, MSR and instruction exits, REP OUTSB and REP INSB cut at a page with RF set while they repeat and clear once they end, REP INSB whose RCX the caller lowers, raises or zeroes before the next KVM_RUN, and a REP OUTSB at offset 0 with a 64-bit address size moved whole, REP MOVSB, STOSB and INSB with ECX 0 at address size 4 in 64-bit mode under Intel's vendor and AMD's, a VMCALL with Hyper-V's hypercalls enabled among them; EPT faults and the memory type of each leaf; a table of the emulator's instructions with their operand on MMIO in 32-bit protected mode, each checked for the reads it asks, the writes it leaves, its registers and its flags, with the string instructions an element at a time, the stack and far-pointer forms, a segment register loaded from the device and SS's null selector loaded from it in 64-bit mode, MOVQ and MOVDQU, and instructions the decoder refuses, which end in the internal error with #UD queued and the exit's error code reported only when the interruption information is valid with one; a guest's WRMSR and the caller's KVM_SET_MSRS of SYSCALL_MASK with a bit of 63:32 kept without EFER.SCE and refused with it, and the entry's WRMSR of such a kept value caught and the processor's value left; #UD exits for SYSENTER, SYSCALL outside 64-bit mode, refused under Intel's vendor and run under AMD's, an RDPID the guest's CPUID lacks, UD2, and VMMCALL rewritten as VMCALL; the guest's own #DB given back with its bits in DR6 and DR7.GD cleared, the single-step trap pending under STI's shadow, INT1, #AC with its error code, and the processor's DR6 never written; real mode, PAE and long mode with a RIP above 4 GiB; the CR4 bits the guest owns, written without an exit and read back, and a state KVM_SET_SREGS gave read back whole before the next entry; hardware task switches by CALL, IRET, JMP and task gates for INT n, an exception with an error code, an external interrupt and an NMI, to 32-bit, 16-bit and virtual-8086 tasks, each checked against the TSSes, descriptors and registers Linux's `kvm_task_switch` leaves, and the faults of a gate's DPL, a TSS too small, a segment not present and a TSS in no slot; injection, the NMI and interrupt windows and an event the exit found under delivery; the host's NMI, machine check and interrupt; the host's FREEZE_WHILE_SMM in IA32_DEBUGCTL, which the model's exit clears, carried into the guest's DEBUGCTL beside its LBR, hidden from the guest's DEBUGCTL read back after the exit and written back to the host's, and a host DEBUGCTL of 0 not written; the caller's single steps, one over STI and the next out of its shadow, one over an RDMSR, a WRMSR and a Hyper-V hypercall the caller answered, breakpoints and BLOCKIRQ; a failed VMWRITE reported once; a move to another processor, which clears the VMCS on the one it left and takes that one out of VMX operation, writes the new one's host fields, drops the guest's translations there and launches the VMCS again, and a run back on the first, which clears it on the second; a state read between runs on another processor, which loads the VMCS there; a VMLAUNCH that fails on the guest's state, which leaves the VMCS unlaunched, so that the next entry is a VMLAUNCH again; two vCPUs on one processor in one VMXON, each resumed; VPIDs given and taken back; the VMCSs cleared as their vCPUs are freed, VMX off with the last; the XSAVES control on a model that has it, set only for a vCPU whose CPUID has XSAVE and XSAVES; the first entry of a new vCPU in 64-bit mode with RSP all ones, which the VMCS gets and the exit reads back; under a host DR7 that enables a breakpoint, DR7 at 0x400 while DR0 to DR3 take the caller's breakpoints, which the model's `write_dr` checks for every such write, and the host's DR0 and DR7 back after each run; the host's state and MSRs back, with the guest's FPU left to the switch, at a switch between two runs, a slice's end or a halted vCPU's sleep at the run loop's top, which marks no preemption, and saved and loaded again at the next entry; the fast path with the local APIC in the kernel, a WRMSR of the TSC deadline and the preemption timer's expiry entering again with the run loop not run and counted as fast, and a request ending it with the WRMSR stepped once; the host's XCR0 in between runs and at a halted vCPU's sleep while the guest's FPU is held; `kvm/exit_stats`'s eleven columns in order, with a run's entry, run and exit as the model's TSC steps them; a panic inside a run, which must clear every VMCS active on the processor and leave VMX off with CR4 back; and, over every row, each copy through the slot's memory and each dirty mark made under the slots' readers' lock, which the model counts, and every hold given back.
* `kvm_svm_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kvm/svm.c` whole over stubs of the run loop, the emulator, the local APIC and the processor, and links `decode.c`. Rows check that a nested page fault on MMIO, a #UD intercept and an instruction that could not be read at CPL 0 give the exit's code, EXITINFO1, EXITINFO2 and EXITINTINFO, and the error code only when EXITINTINFO is valid and has one: never for the error bit alone or the valid bit alone. Another checks that `svm_fast` takes a WRMSR of the TSC deadline, stepped by NextRIP's length, and leaves to `exit` another MSR's write, a read, one without NextRIP, one under an event's delivery and one without the local APIC in the kernel. Another row checks that the backend's `xsaves` follows `kcpu_xsaves_usable`. It runs no guest; the VMX test's rows of the same report cover `vmx.c` alone.
* `xsaves_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86/xsaves.c` over a modelled `cpuid` and MSR_AMD64_PATCH_LEVEL. Rows check `kcpu_xsaves_usable`: no XSAVES without leaf 0xD or its bit in subleaf 1; XSAVES on Intel with the microcode unread; none on a Zen 2 part that Linux 6.12's `erratum_1386_microcode` does not name, at any revision; on the two parts it names, XSAVES at the fixed revision and above, and none below it or at another stepping; and XSAVES on a family 0x17 model that is neither Zen 1 nor Zen 2 and on Zen 3.
* `kvm_vfio_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kvm/vfio.c` whole, the KVM VFIO bridge, built for x86-64 with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` and `RACH2_HOST_CPU`, over a model of the descriptor table, the VM's reference and the guest PAT's switch of memory types. Rows check `KVM_CREATE_DEVICE`'s ABI and its test flag, its refusals of an unknown type, no memory, no descriptor and a copy-back that faults, each with nothing left held, and one bridge to a VM; the attribute calls' errors, `EPERM`, `ENOTTY`, `EFAULT`, `ENXIO` and `EIO` for a caller of another address space; and the files a bridge holds: an add of a group or a device that takes a reference of its own and waits for an operation under way, `EBADF`, `EINVAL` for a file of another kind, `ENOENT` for a delete of a file it does not hold, `EEXIST` for an add of an alias of a file it has, a file kept past its descriptor's close until its delete, the noncoherent DMA policy on while a file is added and off when the last goes, and the bridge's last close, which drops the VM's reference and the policy.
* `kvm_pmu_hosttest.c` includes `pmu.c` over Intel and AMD register models: discovery, absent and ignored MSRs, CPUID bounds, register widths and permissions, RDPMC privilege, migration and INIT, masked filters and their allocation and copy failures, counter switching in each backend, the host's global control and FIXED_CTRL read again only after the host's PMU driver writes, the placement of guest counters by event constraints beside the host's counters, with fixed counter 0 after the general counters on Haswell and first elsewhere, the guest's LBR stack and IA32_DEBUGCTL, isolation between vCPUs, overflow status and deferred PMI delivery.
* `kvm_hyperv_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kvm/hyperv.c` whole over three vCPUs with the split controller, 64 KiB of guest memory, a fake APIC that records the vectors raised, one model of the host's clocks with a TSC of 2.4 GHz, and four fake eventfds. Rows check KVM_GET_SUPPORTED_HV_CPUID's ten leaves against Linux's, with and without the APIC in the kernel; the capabilities and KVM_ENABLE_CAP's answers; each MSR from the guest and from the caller, before and after the CPUID names Hyper-V and under KVM_CAP_HYPERV_ENFORCE_CPUID: the hypercall page on VMX and SVM, the VP index, the reference counter and the TSC page by Linux's formula and sequence, the frequencies, the crash and reset MSRs ending the run, the APIC MSRs, the VP assist page, the runtime, the invariant TSC, the synthetic debugger and SynIC's pages, SINTs and exits; a SINT raised through its route and its auto-EOI; the synthetic timers one-shot, periodic, auto-enabled and direct, a message held by a full slot and retried at EOM and at EOI; the hypercalls' #UD, statuses, 32-bit results, XMM input read only after `kvm_vcpu_fpu_sync` writes the registers, which a model holds apart from the image, back to the image, exits to the caller and their completion, HvSignalEvent over KVM_HYPERV_EVENTFD, the extended and debug hypercalls, and each one denied under the enforced CPUID; the IPI and TLB flush hypercalls with simple and sparse sets, fast and from memory; each entry point that KVM_RUN calls without the big lock, `kvm_hv_msr`, `kvm_hv_hypercall`, `kvm_hv_complete`, `kvm_hv_prepare` and `kvm_hv_stimers`, checked apart: each call begins and ends with the lock free, takes it at most once, and runs the work it covers at depth 1; and every allocation and eventfd reference given back.
* `kvm_gpamap_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kvm/gpamap.c` and `notify.c` over a model of a VM's slots and its maker's frames: a map of runs through two slots in order, held by a reference to each frame; each refusal with nothing held, a maker whose address space has ended among them, which leaves no range on its notice; a change inside the map's range on the notice but between its runs leaving the map; a change of the maker's memory over a run, its end, a slot's change and the VM's end each taking maps back through the owner's revoke while the frames are still held, the references kept and counted until the owner's free; every record and reference given back; and a map that finds the maker's regions' lock held to write, which waits for it with the VM held, holds nothing and answers `EAGAIN`, and called again maps, every row checking that the maker's tables are walked under that lock.
* `kvm_slots_hosttest.c` checks dirty-ring fault and software writes, independent vCPU rings, prefix reset, reprotection, invalid and deleted slots, offsets and counter wrap, and includes `rach2/sys/kvm/slots.c`, `tdp.c`, `dirty.c` and `notify.c` over a model of the caller's page tables, faults and changes: KVM_SET_USER_MEMORY_REGION's checks, all 32764 slot numbers in use at once, made in an order apart from their frames', each found by frame and by number, one moved into a hole and refused onto its neighbour, and half deleted before the rest, the guest page maps over a slot's old frames taken back at its move and its deletion, each of which waits for the copies through the slot, 4 KiB and 2 MiB leaves, none of them over memfd_secret's pages, the zero page's mark, the memory types, RAM's under the guest's PAT while a device that does not snoop is assigned, each change of the caller's memory dropping its leaves with a flush, the dirty log, a kernel's write that a move or a deletion of its slot waits for, its rest run from the model's wait for the readers' count, logged at the page it wrote in the slot it copied through, zaps in batches of 16 entries, with a fault between two batches of a move's zap finding the slot moved and a zap of every frame taking batches by the tables there, the fault's check before its leaf, where a page change finished, one under way and a notice's zap between its read of the counts and its leaf each make it map nothing and look again, and a copy-on-write break there, which the model counts under way across its notice, gives it the new frame, a flush that waits for a vCPU in the guest only until it takes the request, two replacements of the read side at once on host threads that pass one baton, the second coming while the first waits for a reader from before both and ending, as it does only when they wait one at a time, while new readers keep overlapping, `kvm_tdp_write_nofault` refusing a read-only slot, a page not yet stored into, a read-only, device or absent page, a write across a page or of nothing, an address of no slot, a caller's check made before the tables last changed and a thread of another address space, and writing a stored-into page of RAM with the slot's dirty bit set, and every table, allocation and hold of a lock given back.
* `kvm_decode_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kvm/decode.c` whole and decodes a table of encodings in 16, 32 and 64-bit mode, each row an instruction's bytes, its length and the decoded access written out as text: MOV of each size with ModRM, SIB, RIP-relative, moffs and immediate forms, MOVZX, MOVSX, MOVSXD and MOVNTI, MOVS and STOS with REP, the arithmetic and logic group, TEST, XCHG, INC, DEC, NOT, NEG, PUSH and POP of memory, and each further instruction of `decode.h`'s set, the string compares and scans, the shifts, multiplies, divides and bit tests, CMPXCHG and XADD, the far transfers, the segment and descriptor-table instructions, the SSE and MMX moves, SYSCALL, SYSENTER, SYSEXIT, RDPID, VMCALL and VMMCALL among them, under the operand-size, address-size, segment, LOCK and REX prefixes, a REX that a later prefix voids, and a 15-byte instruction. Each row decodes with bytes after it that the decoder must not take, then cut to its length and to one byte less. Refused rows are instructions outside the set, register forms, a LOCK the processor refuses, bytes that end early and a 16-byte instruction. `kvm_decode_fail` is checked against the layout of Linux's `emulation_failure`.
* `timerfd_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/io/timerfd.c` whole and calls `timerfd_tick` as the timer interrupt does. Rows check that a read takes the count before its copy, so an expiration during the copy is kept and a second read finds `EAGAIN`; that a failed copy loses the count and a short read is `EINVAL`; that a timer expires at the first tick at or after its time, measured from the clock's place within the tick: armed as tick 1000 begins at 1001, and armed just before 1001 at 1002 (`phase_rows`); that a timer armed while a tick is pending counts from that tick, so a timer of a tick armed half a tick into it expires at 1003 (`pending_rows`); that a tick before the clock reaches the time does not expire the timer (`lag_rows`); that a periodic timer counts from the time and interval it was given and stops at its expiry until it is read, 11 expirations for an absolute 10 s every second armed at 20 s once `timerfd_gettime` forwards it, and 4 read at the sixth tick for an interval of a tick and a half, after which it is due again at 7.5 ticks (`interval_rows`); that a read of an expired one-shot timer, nonblocking or blocking, returns 8 bytes holding 1 without a wait, and the next finds `EAGAIN` (`oneshot_rows`); that a timer and an interval of 200 days are reported and waited whole, and a time past the most seconds a setting keeps is taken as that most; that a step of the real-time clock forward past an absolute timer's time expires it at the next tick where no interrupt between ticks comes, a step back moves its tick at the step and keeps it waiting until the clock reaches its time, and a relative timer of `CLOCK_REALTIME` keeps its tick (`step_rows`); that a timerfd set with `TFD_TIMER_CANCEL_ON_SET` becomes readable at a step, its read fails with `ECANCELED` and the next finds nothing (`cancel_read_rows`); that a setting which leaves real time's offset where it was, refused or of nothing, cancels none, before a cancel or after a read reported one, while a step there and back cancels as the first step did (`cancel_refused_rows`); that a periodic one whose expiration nothing touched stays disarmed after that read and reports its interval, and one `timerfd_gettime` touched runs on, and that one stepped back from its expiry at 2 s to 1.5 s reports half a second to `timerfd_gettime`, which takes the expiration back, and expires again at 2 s after the canceled read (`cancel_periodic_rows`); that a canceled timerfd set to disarmed is not readable, a blocking read waits and a nonblocking one fails with `ECANCELED`, and a setting that arms a canceled timerfd arms it and fails with `ECANCELED` (`cancel_setting_rows`); that with a tick between the arming's reading of the tick count and of the clock the timer counts its time from the later reading and expires at the first tick at which the clock reaches it (`reading_rows`); that a timer has the interrupt between ticks armed at its end when set, an absolute one of `CLOCK_REALTIME` at its end less the clock's offset, which expires it at its time and arms it again when it comes early, and that one three ticks ahead is armed when set and again at each tick (`between_rows`); that a timer of a quarter tick armed with a tick pending is armed between ticks at three quarters past that tick and expired there while the tick is still uncounted (`between_pending_rows`); that a POSIX timer of a nanosecond is armed between ticks when set and once more at the expiration that sets its signal pending, not while the signal is pending, when the ticks count it in its setting's count and not in the signal, again once the signal is taken, with the intervals since added to its overrun, never for an ignored signal of an interval under a tick, and never for `SIGEV_NONE` (`ptimer_rows`); that a timer every 100 us whose signal stays pending from 0.1 ms counts its expiration at 0.2 ms in its setting's count, not in the signal, delivers an overrun of 4 when the signal is taken at 0.55 ms and is armed for 0.6 ms, that `timer_getoverrun`'s `overrun` stays 4 while its next signal is pending, that a query at 0.95 ms with that signal pending reports 50 us and counts the expirations at 0.8 and 0.9 ms in its setting's count, and that a first signal left pending across `timer_settime`, to which the new setting's first expiration adds one, is taken with an overrun of 1 while `overrun` is 0, the new setting's own (`deferred_rows`); that an expired one-shot `SIGEV_NONE` timer has no time left before the tick, that a periodic one reports its time moved past the clock, and that one queried on a clock that moves 200 ns on at each reading reports the time left from the reading it was moved on by (`none_rows`); that a timer of two ticks whose signal is ignored stays armed between ticks, that a second timer's expiration that waited for the first timer's pending signal of the same number has its signal posted when that signal is taken, with no overrun, and is armed between ticks again, and that a one-shot second timer's waiting signal is posted then too, though the timer is disarmed, neither timer reporting an overrun (`shared_rows`); that a new timer that takes a deleted timer's id adds nothing to that timer's pending signal, nor to a user's signal of `SI_TIMER` and that id, but waits, and is posted when the deleted timer's signal is taken, which forwards nothing, and that a periodic timer's signal left pending across `timer_settime` forwards nothing when taken (`key_rows`); that an expiration that waits for another timer's signal is kept across `timer_settime` and posted with the old setting, whose signal then forwards nothing, and across `timer_delete`, whose record is kept until it is posted (`waiting_rows`); that a timer of a tick whose notification waits from tick 1001 for a user's signal, or whose own signal is pending from then, and which expires again at 1002, then is disarmed or deleted, delivers an overrun of 0, as Linux, where counting 1002 in the notification gave 1 (`elapsed_rows`); that waiting notifications are posted in the order they began to wait, a later timer's before an earlier one on the list and a deleted timer's before a live one's (`order_rows`); that timer 1 of 0.1 ms and timer 2 of 0.2 ms, ahead of it on the list of armed timers, both overdue at one interrupt between ticks at 0.3 ms or at one tick, expire and wait in the order of their ends, so timer 1's is posted first (`delayed_rows`); that periodic timer 1 at 1 ms, armed before one-shot timer 2 at 2 ms, joins behind timer 2 when its first signal is taken, so timer 2's signal precedes timer 1's at their shared 2 ms deadline, at an interrupt between ticks and at a tick, and after an ignored first signal too (`equal_rearm_rows`); that `SIG_IGN` drops a live timer's waiting notification and frees a deleted timer's record, so that a later user's signal taken posts neither, and that a timer's `SIGCONT`, whose post discards the stops under `timer_lock`, drops a deleted `SIGTSTP` timer's notification without taking the lock again and frees its record as the post lets the lock go (`discard_rows`, with the stub's job control); that exec deletes a timer and a deleted timer's record, discards the timer's pending `SIGUSR1` and keeps a user's `SIGALRM`, after which no timer's signal follows (`exec_rows`); that a POSIX timer's expirations at the tick post no signal there but wait for a holder of the big lock, which posts one signal whose delivery counts the second expiration as its overrun, and that `timer_settime`, `timer_delete` and exec post or drop a waiting expiration before the setting or the timer goes (`fire_later_rows`); that a SIGCONT cancels a deferred one-shot SIGTSTP, with an unrelated deferred signal kept and the queue's tail repaired, and a timer-generated SIGCONT does so under the lock it already holds (`deferred_discard_rows`); that thread exit cancels its deferred clock and processor-time timer signals, keeps an unrelated one, publishes its zombie state before the timers' unlock, and lets a later expiration go to the process, while a thread without timers publishes death too (`thread_exit_rows`, with an observation at interrupt restore after the unlock); that a timerfd's expiration begins its blocked reader's wake record under the queue's lock and the run queues' lock taken after it; and that every interrupt mask is taken back. No row checks that the dequeue keeps interrupts off from taking the signal through `ptimer_rearm`, that a blocking wait takes the ignored signals it drops through the same dequeue (`signal_pending`), nor `timer_create`'s count of deleted timers against `PTIMER_MAX`: `sig_dequeue` and `signal_pending` are in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c`, which no host test includes, and `timer_create` here gets no memory from the model's `kzalloc`. The model's `kfree` does nothing, so the rows check that a deleted timer's record leaves `timers_gone` and not that it is freed: the frees by `ptimer_delete_all` at an exec (`ptimer_exec`) and at the end of the process, by a discard and by `ptimer_thread_exit` at a thread's end are checked by reading.
* `poll_select_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/io/poll_select.c`, which converts between `select`'s `fd_set` words and the poll core's `kernel_pollfd` entries. Rows turn read, write and except bits into events, fold one descriptor's three bits into one entry, treat `nfds` as a half-open bound, place descriptors 31 to 33 in their words, and refuse more descriptors than the array holds. Back the other way, each ready direction counts once, a hangup counts as readable and an error as readable and writable, only in a set the descriptor was selected for, and `pollfd_select_ready` agrees.
* `poll_deadline_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/io/poll.c` with its system calls made private, so that at `-O1` only the deadline conversions, `deadline_reached`, `deadline_passed` and `poll_wait` are compiled. The `enrollment` argument runs only the row that forces a room wake or a wake of no kind just after enrollment and before the second scan, with no input at either scan: the wake calls the record's function, which leaves the record on its queue and marks the poll woken, and the wait must not sleep without its record; later input completes it and every record is withdrawn. Rows check that `poll`, `select` and `ppoll` timeouts end at the first tick at or after their end, measured from where in the tick they began; that a zero timeout ends at once and a negative or null one has none; that the longest `ppoll` timeout ends 0x7FFFFFFF ticks ahead across the count's wrap; that a deadline is reached at its tick and not before; that a `poll` or `ppoll` whose tick has come before a clock held ahead of the count by a lost tick has reached its end moves to the tick at which it does; and that a `ppoll` of the clamped length ends at its tick alone.
* `epoll_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/io/epoll.c` and `rach2/sys/proc/wait_queue.c` whole, over model objects whose events and wakes each row sets and which count their polls. Rows check that an edge-triggered registration, in a set or in a set inside another, is reported again only for a wake of a kind it watches; that a one-shot registration reports once and again only after `EPOLL_CTL_MOD`; that a wait over a set of forty registrations polls only those on its ready list; that a wake of an object two sets watch with `EPOLLEXCLUSIVE` wakes one model sleeper and readies one set, and with no sleeper readies both, and that `EPOLLEXCLUSIVE` with `EPOLL_CTL_MOD`, a set or `EPOLLPRI` is `EINVAL`; that `EPOLL_CTL_ADD` nests five sets and returns `ELOOP` for a sixth level or a cycle; that a registration lasts and reports while its open file does, after its number is closed and across a fork, and goes at the file's last close; and that the last close of a set takes the registrations of it in other sets.
* `n_tty_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/tty/n_tty.c`, the line discipline, with echo and output written into a buffer. Rows cover ERASE, WERASE and KILL and their echo, of a whole UTF-8 character under IUTF8 rubbed out by its width, of one byte without it, and of a control character's `^X`; one line per read and `FIONREAD`; `VEOF` and its order against later lines; INTR, QUIT and SUSP, which flush all input; the input and output mappings; a partial line kept when canonical mode goes off; raw reads, which wait for `VMIN` bytes and never wait when it is 0; disabled control characters; a hangup read as EOF; the echo held given out at most a sink's room at a time, and kept whole, its oldest byte first even when the buffer is full, by a byte fed with its echo muted (`echo_muted`); a line capped at 4095 bytes and its newline (`N_TTY_LINE_MAX`), and read through a smaller buffer, which leaves the line open (`n_tty_line_open`) until the rest is read; `VEOL` and line ends across termios changes and reused ring slots; a new terminal's control flags; and initialization of storage filled with nonzero bytes, which must enable echo. Flow rows check IXON and IXANY as Linux 6.12's n_tty has them (`flow_control`): the defaults, VSTOP and VSTART consumed, a stop that holds the echo and the start that gives it out, a disabled character, ISTRIP before the test, VSTART winning when both are one byte, IXANY, and a signal character that restarts output; and (`flow_stops`) TCOOFF's stop that neither VSTART, IXANY nor a signal character ends, the look ahead and the byte fed after it, IXON turned off, and a signal character under NOFLSH that keeps the input and echoes in its turn.
* `serial_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86/serial.c` with the ring's counters set directly. Rows check that the look ahead gives only the bytes it has not given, with its cursor far behind the head and with the counters crossing the 32-bit wrap, and that a take past the cursor brings it up to the head.
* `tty_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/tty/tty.c`, `pty.c` and `rach2/sys/proc/wait_queue.c` whole and links `rach2/sys/tty/n_tty.c`, under a model scheduler that runs a producer only once the consumer has blocked and requires a wake before the consumer resumes. Rows check that a slave read, `TCFLSH` and `TCSETSF` wake a blocked master poller or writer, and a partial slave write or echo a blocked master reader; that the console's input pump runs with input interrupts masked; the kind of change each wake of a pair passes to its pollers; packet mode and vhangup discarding output and waking a packet reader for FLUSHWRITE; that a read which does not wait ends the console latency span; that slave output under OPOST takes two bytes of the master's ring for a newline, fitted again after a copy from the user during which echo took room; that while the console's keyboard is in a raw mode the tick's and a reader's drains discard COM1's bytes, a Ctrl+C among them, and none reaches the discipline until the keyboard translates again; and that the tick reads COM1 at each call while its receive interrupt is off, and while it is on, never while the line is low and every fifth of a second while the handler has left it high, the first time at the next tick even after a quiet spell of more than 2^31 ticks (`console_poll_backup`). Flow rows check that a VSTOP from the master stops the slave's output, reported to a packet master as TIOCPKT_STOP, and a slave writer waits until VSTART, reported as TIOCPKT_START (`flow_slave`); that the echo of input typed during a stop waits for VSTART (`flow_echo`); that TCOOFF holds against VSTART until TCOON, TCIOFF and TCION write their characters unless TCOOFF stopped output, and another action is EINVAL (`flow_tcflow`); that IXON with ^S and ^Q turned off and on gives a packet master TIOCPKT_NOSTOP and TIOCPKT_DOSTOP, and turned off restarts output (`flow_termios`); that a VSTOP behind a full slave acts at once and is fed later without acting again or reaching the reader (`flow_lookahead`); and that on the console a VSTOP stops output and sets Scroll Lock, a write waits until a VSTART from COM1 or fails with EAGAIN when nonblocking, a VSTOP behind a full console acts at once, and Hold stops and restarts output (`flow_console`). Further flow rows check that the echo held goes ahead of a slave write only as far as the master's ring has room and the write then waits rather than lose its bytes, that a master write goes on after the last slave closes, that a VSTART from COM1 drained with no reader keeps the keyboard echo held, a full buffer of it whole behind an ordinary byte that drain takes, that a nonblocking master write behind a full slave counts the flow characters it acts on and its VSTART gives out the echo held, that nonblocking master writes of data ending in a flow character, behind a full slave, drop no byte and count only the bytes the read queue gained (`lookahead_bounded`), that a blocking master takes the prefix through a flow character before another writer can fill its space, an interrupted write that took no byte returns EINTR, a nonblocking writer cannot enter while another sleeps (`lookahead_interrupted`), and a blocking writer waits for the write lock, released on a failed user copy too (`master_write_lock`), that a master vector keeps both elements together when its second buffer copy tries a competing write, and releases the lock on a fault in either buffer (`master_write_vector`), that TCIOFF and TCION wait for the master write lock even on a nonblocking descriptor and feed no byte when the wait is interrupted (`master_flow_lock`), that a background process takes SIGTTOU for a change of its terminal, goes ahead when it ignores the signal, a TOSTOP write among them, and gets EIO for an ignored SIGTTIN or an orphaned group, a parent whose leader exited while another of its threads runs keeping the group joined, that the console's TCIOFF writes its character under TCOOFF, and that a session leader's read of a slave does not make it a controlling terminal.
* `kd_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/kd.c` and `rach2/sys/evdev.c` whole and links `rach2/sys/keymap.c`, with the caller, the console's terminal and the input layer's repeat and LEDs modelled. Rows send Linux's KD* requests with Linux's structures as a stranger, a process of the console's session, the holder of `/fb` and root: KDGKBTYPE and KDGETMODE; the five keyboard modes, each set flushing the input and stopping repeat; meta; the lock flags and LEDs, which follow the locks until KDSETLED lights its own; table entries with Linux's ranges, entry 0, a table allocated on demand and freed, and table 0 never freed; a code point's entry taken and read in K_MEDIUMRAW and read as K_HOLE in K_XLATE; function strings up to 511 bytes; the accent table in code points and in 8 bits, up to 255 entries and left whole when its entries cannot be read; KDKBDREP; each request's -EPERM, -EINVAL and -EFAULT. Further rows check K_MEDIUMRAW's one- and three-byte key codes, K_RAW's set-1 bytes, the modes that translate, and the translated keys: a letter and its repeat, Enter as CR, Backspace as DEL, Shift, Ctrl+C, an arrow in both cursor modes, Caps Lock with its LED and a KDSETLED that keeps its own, Shift with Page Up and Page Down scrolling, Page Up's sequence, a Cyrillic letter in UTF-8 and dropped in K_XLATE, a Latin-1 letter, Alt as ESC and as K_METABIT's top bit, a parked reader with no session taking the keys, and the debug shell's input and interrupt; the state saved and restored for an owner of the screen; a DRM master's hold, which sends no key, keeps COM1's input open and a mode set meanwhile, and discards no input; and evdev's EVIOCGREP and EVIOCSREP on a keyboard and their -ENOSYS on a button. A stop before the first keymap use keeps Scroll Lock set through the first key event.
* `synthfs_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/fs/synthfs.c` and calls `synth_classify`, which sorts a path among the KRFFS root, `/dev`, `/proc` and `/sys`, over a model of one network interface, `eth0`, and no disks. Rows check names left to the root; devices and ptys; a process's files, links and threads, and `/proc/sys`; the PCI, USB, network and processor entries of `/sys`, a processor's `cache/indexM` with its key, NUMA `node0`, `clocksource0` and `/sys/class/dmi/id`, whose serial numbers and UUID are private; doubled and trailing slashes; `.` and `..`, which are refused; the bounds of a process id, a descriptor number and a path's depth; `ENOENT`, and `ENOTDIR` with the file a path goes on past; the rows of `/dev`'s directories, `input`, `dri` and `vfio`, and a device in each; the path written back for a directory; and VFIO's entries of `/sys` through the registry: a group below `/sys/kernel/iommu_groups`, a function's `iommu_group` link and `driver_override`, and `vfio-pci` below `/sys/bus/pci/drivers` with `drivers_probe` beside it; and the paths and attributes of `/sys/kernel/mm/transparent_hugepage`, with which of them root may write. A row adds 1500 attributes below one directory, past the registry's first 512 slots, and reaches each by name and by path, with a handle taken before the table grew still naming its entry.
* `devfs_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/devfs.c` whole and links `rach2/sys/block/partition.c`, `gpt.c` and the host spinlocks of `compat/spinlock_host.c`. Rows check that an exclusive create is `EEXIST` for an existing device and `ENOENT` for an absent one, and allocates nothing; which devices' transfers move the description's offset; that `/dev/grubcore` is the gap of an MBR disk (LBA 1 through 2047) or the BIOS boot partition of a GPT disk, with the first sector and size its ioctls report, and absent when the region is missing, overlaps the GPT entries or passes the disk's end; that the whole-disk node of a disk of 2^33 sectors covers them all; and that a node of the registry, `dri/card0` at 226:0, is found by path, by number and in the listing of `/dev/dri`, which it makes present, that a duplicate or misplaced name is refused, and that it is gone once unregistered and has its inode back when registered again. The table of `/dev`'s directories, `synthfs.c`'s, is stubbed with its two rows.
* `procfs_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/procfs.c` whole with its unused exports made private. Rows check a stat text whose allocation fails in whole or in part; `/proc/uptime`; an interface's counts past 2^32 in `/proc/net/dev` and `/sys/class/net/IF/statistics`; the CPU time of a process's stat and a thread's; a thread's and its leader's `schedstat`; task ids; process-node ownership for 0555 and 0500 directories and files across dumpable states and after the address space is gone; descriptor links; the counts a `/proc/PID/mem` descriptor takes of the address space; `/proc/PID/exe` on the KRFFS root and on another type; the paths of `/proc/PID/maps`; the possible-access and device flags of `smaps`, including write and execute restrictions and Linux's flag order; a read of a process's arguments while the process ends and is reaped; the text of a cache's size, type, ways and sharing processors, of `node0` and `node`, of the clock sources, of a DMI field, a missing one's `ENOENT` and `modalias`; a PCI function's `resource` file, a real one's BARs a line each in Linux's format and a reported one's 13 empty lines; its BAR files, including write-combining aliases only for prefetchable memory with PAT enabled, their page addresses, bounds and privileged opens, and distinct inodes for every PCI directory, bus link and file within and between functions; the parsing, range, floor and open errors of `oom_score_adj`; and the transparent-hugepage files: their text at Linux's defaults, with `defrag` never and `use_zero_page` 0 under 512 MiB, the native page-directory mapping size, the writes Linux's stores take and refuse, with `ERANGE` for digits past 64 bits whatever follows them, and through the attribute's write, which gives a store the first page of a write as Linux's kernfs does, 32 zeroes to `use_zero_page`, 40-byte words to `shrink_underused`, a write past a page, the 16 bytes `shmem_enabled` refuses and an empty write, which is 0; and a backlight's and a radio switch's numbers given to their stores whole with the count written, so that `k_strtoul` takes one newline and refuses a space or a second newline after the number, as Linux's kstrtoul; and rtc0's wakealarm as Linux's `wakealarm_store` takes it, with the clock read first: `+N`, `+=N` pushing the alarm set and refused with none, `EBUSY` for a new time over a set one, any time not after now clearing it, and junk, a space and a number past 64 bits refused. It also checks the pass-through port's removal and recreation through the real sysfs registry, its input and bus links, remove uevents, dead handles and replaced callbacks, a pass-through port made with no device below it that outlives its guest until it is removed, the replacement of all 24 protocol files on serio1, and cleanup of a rejected set of 25 files.
* `tmpfs_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/tmpfs.c` and `rach2/sys/mm/shmem.c` whole over a pool of host pages, each filled with 0xA5 when it is handed out. Rows check a file's bytes across holes and page boundaries, cuts and growth; the `mode=` and `size=` options, remount, `statfs` and `ENOSPC`; link counts, `rmdir` and hard links; rename's replacements and refusals; symbolic links, FIFOs and device nodes; listings; nodes held after their last name or their parent has gone; the pages mappings and truncations take and give back; the largest page a file may have; `fallocate`, under RLIMIT_FSIZE too; and a memfd's seals. The give-way rows (`give_way_per_leaf`) free a file with a page in each of four leaves: an unlink while a hold remains gives no way, the last put three, one between each two leaves, and a rename over such a file three, at each of which the moved file is found by its new name. A one-page file grown to the maximum size counts its clean resident page through the allocated leaf, with one scheduling point. Every frame must be back after the unmount.
* `filecache_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/filecache.c` whole, over a table of 64 host frames with a count of references each and a file whose identity, size and modification time the rows set, and runs a hook on the restore of the interrupt flag that follows a give of `fc_lock`, as another processor taking the lock the moment it is free. Rows check that a page stored reads back; that a page whose file's record a shrink frees between the insertion's two holds of the lock is not kept and the freed record is not read, which AddressSanitizer would stop; that a page of a file changed since its record was made is kept under the new record the insertion makes though the old one goes between the holds; that a readahead window, started through a `read_frames` that keeps the completion and a reference on each frame as a request does, puts its pages in the cache locked, its mark among them, which are neither read nor lent nor started again until the read's answer unlocks them; that a failed read drops its pages; that a change of the file while the read runs drops them and the answer, finding no page, lets the frames go; that a completion whose window the starter's put, or another completion of the window's last page, frees at its first wake wakes its other pages' own queues without reading the freed window, which AddressSanitizer would stop; that a run whose second insertion fails, since another reader stored that page after the first, is the one page, and each frame left over is freed once though it is let go twice; and that every frame comes back with the mount's pages.
* `pagecache_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/pagecache.c` whole with `PC_SLOTS` set to 4, over a model disk of 64 pages that counts reads and writes. Rows check that a fault reads its page once and a fault past the file is refused; that an eviction writes back only a dirty page; that a failed write keeps its page dirty and a shrink whose writebacks fail is reported; and that a writeback which sleeps while a reclaim, a miss, a sync or an eviction runs keeps its slot and bytes. Compiled with `PAGECACHE_SYNC_HOSTTEST`, as the fixture `pagecache` of `mk/host-fixtures.mk` compiles it, the test also runs `pc_sync_wait` and `sys_sync`, extracted from `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c`: a pin that only a blocked waiter's sleep lets go, a pin let go while the pass sleeps in a later write, and a failed write that a later pass writes, which the wait still answers.
* `pagecache_ring_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/mm/pagecache.c` whole at the `PC_SLOTS` that `rach2/sys/mm/pagecache.h` sets, and models a compile on a KRFFS disk root: a round touches the compile's 560 pages and then walks 2056 pages of node headers twice, as two lookups do, the ring of os-real's root once the kernel's build has added its nodes. After a round that fills the cache, three more must read nothing from the backing. Two control rows at 2048 slots read nothing for the staged ring, without the build's 1140 pages, and at least the whole ring each round with them. The sizes are constants in the test.
* `krffs_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c` whole and runs the KRFFS core over a 64 KiB image in memory, directly and behind a recorder that keeps every write in order. Rows check the vectors of `krffs_hash_vectors.h` and the inode numbers the root reports; each fault the mount check refuses; creation, writes, the refusal of a write to a freed node, holes, truncation, growth, relocation, directories, rename, orphan reaping and repair; the ends of the region; a directory whose identifier another has (`KRFFS_ETAKEN`); parent offsets, `..` and reconstructed paths when an earlier file, FIFO or socket has the directory's identifier; fields across a 4 KiB block; and the order of writes, where every state a stop can leave, and every refused write, must leave a chain a mount can walk.
* `krffsmount_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c` and `rach2/sys/fs/krffsmount.c`, the adapter for a KRFFS volume on a disk, whole and links `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` and `lock.c`, over separate arrays for the buffer cache, the device's cache and the medium. Rows check that a write and a truncation that move a file repoint every description while another file reuses the old place; a file an `O_PATH` description keeps across an unlink; the order of data and the clean flag, and the read-only mount a failed flush leaves; the `EIO`, `ENOSPC` and `EINVAL` of a failed creation; a FIFO whose owner crosses a 4 KiB block, and the `EIO` of a chown that would change bytes on both sides of it; the `EBUSY` of an `rmdir` of a working directory; the `ENOENT`, `EPERM`, `EISDIR` and `ENOTDIR` an unlink, a rename or an `rmdir` is refused with; an open file renamed to its own name, which keeps it; the group of a file and a directory made in a set-group-ID directory, and the bit the directory takes; and an `fsync` that writes only its file's header block beside a neighbour with nine dirty blocks, writes the backward link a shrink tagged with the file's open record after the file moved, writes that link by its offset after a truncate of the next file retags it and after the file is closed and opened again under a new record, and writes every dirty block on a mount a failure made read-only.
* `loop_attach_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c` and `rach2/sys/block/loop.c` whole and links `rach2/sys/block/chain.c`, with a namespace lock whose wait lets a writer move the file being attached and free its old extent. One row requires the attachment to take the moved node, and its reads and writes to reach the moved image while another file reuses the old extent. Seven failure rows (a path moved or removed, a failed open or sync, a writer in flight, a shared mapping) must return their error and release every lock and reference. A write from a caller without the big lock takes it once, for the file system alone, gives it back, and leaves no transfer counted in flight.
* `krffs_hash_hosttest.c` includes `krffs/stageimage.krffs.c`, the host's image stager, whole with its `main` renamed, and links the KRFFS library under `krffs/`. It checks the library's `krffs_calculate_djb_hash` and `krffs_mix_id`, which the stager uses, against the eight vectors of `krffs_hash_vectors.h` that `krffs_hosttest.c` checks the kernel against, so that a staged image gives a directory the identifier the kernel looks it up by. The vectors include bytes above 127, a name of 234 bytes and a mixed identifier of zero, which becomes 1.
* `readfile_hosttest.c` links `krffs/krffs_reader.c` and calls `krffs_read_regular`, the walk `readfile.krffs` uses to copy a file out of an image, over a region the test writes node by node: a root, a directory and a file of five bytes. Rows read the file by its path, and require a reason naming the fault for a missing name, a directory, a relative path, the root, a truncated region, a node size that is zero, too large or `UINT64_MAX`, a wrong magic, and a data size past its node or of `UINT64_MAX`.
* `krffs_fuse_hosttest.c` includes `krffs/krffs.c`, the FUSE mount tool, and `krffs/stageimage.krffs.c`, the image stager, whole with their `main`s renamed, and links the KRFFS library under `krffs/`; `fuse_main` only counts its calls. Over a 64 KiB image file in its fixture directory, rows require the mount to refuse each fault of the node ring before FUSE is called (a wrong size, magic, type, backward or closing link, data size or name, and a partial last header), and check the callbacks for holes, a file shrunk and grown within its node, `chown` with -1, the owner of a node made when FUSE reports no request, the free blocks of `statfs` on a full image, and `utimens` with `UTIME_OMIT` and `UTIME_NOW`. Other rows stage `/d/f` and `/f` with the stager, require the identifiers the kernel gives, and read each through the mount, and check the directories the mount makes: a file in one apart from a file of the same name at the root, the refusals of `rmdir`, `unlink`, `rename` and `mkdir` the kernel makes, a rename across directories and to the same name, and a file that keeps its directory when growth moves it. The test compiles the driver with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, as `krffs/Makefile` does.
* `ext2_hosttest.c` links the ext2 driver's files, `rach2/sys/fs/ext2.c` and the five `ext2_*.c` beside it, over `rach2/sys/fs/bcache.c`, on volumes the host's `mke2fs` makes from a tree the test stages, with `debugfs` adding device nodes and a file past 4 GiB; `tools/inspect/setidcheck.c` is built as a second reader. Rows read every file back at 4 KiB and 2 KiB blocks, and a sparse file through each pointer level at 1 KiB; mount read-only or refuse, by name, the features the driver lacks; fail by name a read or lookup through a damaged pointer or entry; check signed timestamp seconds and clamping at both limits through a remount, with 128-byte inodes and with 256-byte inodes on an `extra_isize` volume, which keep nanoseconds and times to 2446 (`--times-only` runs these alone); run fourteen write sequences judged by the driver, `e2fsck -fn`, `setidcheck` and `debugfs`, and stop thirteen of them after each write; replace files by symbolic links, by a rename over each and by an unlink and a symlink, on a `dirsync` mount and on one that writes back, and stop a rename of a link over a file after each write, with `e2fsck -fn` finding each entry's type its inode's (`--ftype-only` runs these alone); on a mount that writes back, stop the removal of an empty file, a fast symlink and a file with blocks, by an unlink, an unlink whose inode is taken again and a rename over it, after each write, with `e2fsck -fn` run to its end asking only for repairs of counts, bitmaps and unnamed inodes and `debugfs` finding each name left marked in use (`--removal-only` runs these alone); on a mount that writes back, stop a rename into a directory that grows, into its second block and into its first behind the single indirect, after each write, with `e2fsck -fn` asking only for repairs of counts, bitmaps and the file's link count and `debugfs` finding one of its names in use, and the same rename retried with the directory held after one and after two attempts whose reads of its table block fail, which must each fail, stopped after each write of the retry and judged the same way, make a fast symlink whose directory's times fail at a read after its name reached the device, which must keep the inode it names, and grow a directory into blocks a truncate freed while a second process reclaims the cache at each read, writing no freed block before the truncated file's table block (`--dir-commit-only` runs these alone); remove a file whose mapped pages failed to go back, unreported, held and freed at its last put and unheld and freed at its unlink, and require the new file that takes its inode number to `fsync` with no error, its sequence forgotten before the bit was freed; remount writable after a remount the heap could not give a batch; mount dirty volumes, repairing some, mounting ten faults read-only with the error bit on the disk and nothing more written, a create there `EROFS` and a remount writable, which checks again, `EROFS`, and mounting read-only unchecked, with the valid bit left clear, a volume whose check stops at a tree deeper than it walks or at a workspace the heap cannot give; a volume stopped at a fault keeps the clear bit of a named file's block until `e2fsck -p` repairs it, and one stopped for memory has the bit set by a remount writable once the memory is back (`--stopped-only` runs these alone); a live orphan whose inode bit is clear has it set, an orphan directory naming a file of one link stops the check, as does one whose `..` names a file or an empty directory named from the root, which an rmdir could free, and a file named only in an orphan directory has its inode and block bits set so that filling the volume takes neither (`--orphans-only` runs these alone); an entry naming an inode zeroed with its bit clear, which named a regular file, is cleared and the volume left clean, one naming a deleted inode whose bit is set is cleared with the inode left to `e2fsck -p`, and an entry whose type byte says a symbolic link for an empty file has the file's type set, each volume writable, while an entry of type 0 naming a live inode of mode 0600, of no kind, and the same inode on a volume without `filetype` each mount read-only with the fault named (`--entries-only` runs these alone); a dirty volume whose inode table has a free block mounts read-only, and on a clean one, which is not checked, a mkdir must refuse the block by name and leave it as it was with its bit set again, and under `errors=remount-ro` stops the mkdir with EROFS, its inode and directory count given back on the disk; mount a clean volume read-only, read it, `fsync` a file through its description, `statfs` and sync it and unmount it as the VFS does, and require every byte as it was; mount a dirty volume read-only, writing nothing, and remount it writable, which its check's fault refuses with `EROFS`, the error bit set; and run a sync as a second process at a remount read-only's write of the superblock, which must sleep on its buffer, resume on the read-only mount, and record no failure, so that a remount writable after it goes through; run a lookup that meets a malformed entry as a second process at the same write, which must sleep on `em->super` and leave the error bit, without the valid bit, on the disk after the remount and the unmount; and meet that entry on a writable mount under each error policy, `continue` from `s_errors`, which keeps the mount writable with the error bit on the disk, `remount-ro` from `s_errors`, from the mount's `errors=` and from a remount's after a first fault, which makes it read-only by name, `errors=continue` over `s_errors` 3, and a refused `errors=` value; under `errors=remount-ro`, a write behind a damaged pointer while another write waits in the batch, which must record no write failure, so that the flusher drains the waiting write and a remount writable checks the volume, and seven more faults Linux's `ext2_error` reports, each by its record with the error bit on the disk and the mount read-only: a truncate and an unlink past a block out of range, which free the rest, a fast link of 70 bytes, a move into a directory without `..`, and lookups of a linkless live inode, of a freed inode a name references and of an inode whose attribute block is out of range (`--dirty-only` and `--readonly-only` run these alone); and check the write batch with 16 and 512 cache buffers, and on a mount that writes back a batch kept across creates, a chmod and a rename whose writes share its blocks, a drain past a cache of 16 dirty buffers and two truncates interleaved at a disk read as two processes, which must hold `em->batch` and the groups' locks across no disk request and put the inodes on the disk before the bitmap. With the big lock reported free, as the file calls run on a type that sets `reads_unlocked`, a read and a listing beside a write waiting in the batch take no big lock and read the pending bytes from the batch's images, a hold that is not the last is dropped without it, and a release drains the batch without it; with the batch empty, reads, a listing and releases take it never and a last put takes it once. The namespace calls run under their directory's lock, as the VFS makes them. Its nested interleavings run each pair of thirteen operations, the second as another process at every disk request of the first, on a volume kept in memory, and judge whether the second ends or sleeps, and on which lock (`--pairs-only` runs them alone). A 32 KiB file on a volume of 1 KiB blocks, whose indirect block a service point holds busy part way through a page counts the pages before it, and not that one, in `cachestat` (`--cache-partial-only` runs it alone). On a mount that writes back, the rows of `ext2_fsync_rows.h` (`--fsync-only` runs them alone) check that an `fsync` beside a neighbour of 151 dirty blocks in another table block writes only the file's blocks, its bitmap, its descriptors and its table block, four requests on a device that takes 32 blocks at once, and leaves the neighbour dirty; that a stop right after it leaves a volume `e2fsck -fn` and the driver's check call clean, with the file whole and the neighbour's old bytes; that a stop after an `fsync` of an unchanged file is as clean beside a file of its table block that allocated new blocks, or a new directory there; that a file whose bitmaps outnumber its list, on 1 MiB groups, and the first `fsync` after a node with a list was freed write the whole device; that `fdatasync` writes no table block after a rewrite or a change of times alone and writes it after a growth; and that a second call made while an `fsync` or `sync` waits for the disk, an `fsync` of a neighbour in the table block or an `fdatasync` of the file, waits for those blocks before it returns or writes the table block, with a failure of the first reported by both; that a neighbour's append and chmod made while an `fsync` or `sync` waits for the disk leave a stop clean; and that one block that fails, with every other write going through, keeps the table block off the medium through an `fsync` that waited on it, a sync of the device and their retries, and that the block goes first once it heals. The rows of `ext2_order_rows.h` (`--order-only` runs them alone) check that a shrink writes the data of a file whose node was freed with its list and loaded again before its table block, and that a sync writes the data of a filled hole before the indirect block on the device that names it, and below a double indirect block the data, then the new indirect block, before it. The rows of `ext2_service_rows.h` (`--service-only` runs them alone) put another process at the service points of a free of a sparse and dense file, on a mount that writes back and on one with `dirsync`: files made, written, removed and synced in the same group, with every file left read whole and the volume clean by `e2fsck -fn`; a sync and `e2fsck -fn` of the device at each service point, which finds no block a pointer names with its bit clear; and a device that fails at one, inside the walk, where the round frees nothing, or at a round's end, where no other round walks, and the cut answers `-EIO`. The rows of `ext2_group_rows.h` (`--groups-only` runs them alone) run two processes in one group of a volume of sixteen 1 MiB groups, on a mount that writes back: two directories made at once and a truncation beside a new directory, the second process run inside the first's load of the group's block bitmap, which a pending write's image in the batch lets it read without the bitmap's buffer, must sleep on the group's lock, give two blocks to the two directories and lose no free; on a `dirsync` mount whose group 0 has no free inode, a creation in a directory there, run at a removal's write through of group 1's inode bitmap under that group's lock, must sleep on the lock and take an inode of group 1; after each, every group's bitmaps must have as many free bits as its descriptor counts, the descriptors must sum to the mount's free counts, and `e2fsck -fn` must find the volume clean. Without the group locks the first two fail, with one block given twice and one free lost, and the third by its sleep on the bitmap's buffer. The rows of `ext2_write_rows.h` (`--writes-only` runs them alone) run two processes on one file with the big lock reported free, as the calls run on a type that sets `writes_unlocked`, the second at the first's first disk request: two O_APPEND writes, a write of block thirty beside a truncation of forty blocks to two, and a write of the first block beside a fallocate of twenty, where the second must sleep on the node's lock and the file must read back as the lock orders the two; and an append beside an fsync of the file, which must sleep on no lock of the node's, after which a stop of the device leaves a volume `e2fsck -fn` and the driver's check find clean with the synced blocks whole. Every take of the big lock is given back, and `e2fsck -fn` finds each volume clean. With the node's lock made a no-op the first three fail, the second process sleeping on the buffer the first loads, and the second O_APPEND record lands on the first's bytes. The rows of `ext2_names_rows.h` (`--names-only` runs them alone) resume two independent directory listings after removed entries are reused for a longer name, seek into that name, and require the pending write to sync with the mount still writable, retry a lookup whose saved entry is removed and reused while it obtains the inode, and end a lookup of a name that stays while another name of its directory changes on every round, after a bounded number of changes; they also run the operations on names as callers without the big lock, the second process given the turn at each of the first's releases of it: forty creates beside forty unlinks in one directory, renames of a directory and a dozen files between two directories in both directions at once, each beside creates, renames over names another process removes and makes again in the slots they leave, and an rmdir beside a create in the directory it removes, once with each winning, the second process given the turn at each disk request too; every call must end, the names and the link counts of the files and of their directories must be what the calls that won left, `..` of each moved directory must name its new parent, the free inodes must come back once the last hold of a removed directory goes, and `e2fsck -fn` must find each volume clean. With the directories' locks taken out, the creates and unlinks, the renames over reused names and the rmdir beside a create fail. Independent e2fsck stdout contains the parsed repair questions; stderr remains in the adjacent `.err` file. `--capture-only` checks both real child streams and question parsing without making a volume. The cache rows count pending data before sync, count one page of a one-terabyte sparse file with no disk requests and bounded cache visits, and leave a cold map unfetched. The fence rows fence a node by a store of a field changed without its mark, an owner or an attribute block, on a mount under `errors=continue`: a fenced empty file whose attribute block is another file's must free nothing of it, its unlink `EIO` and, removed while open before the fence, its last close freeing neither the block nor its inode; a fenced file's open and a read through an earlier open, and a fenced directory's lookup, readdir and create, must be `EIO`; and an `fsync` of a file, and a `sync`, that come while a first `fsync`'s store of the file sleeps on its cold table block must end only after that store (`--fence-only` runs these rows alone). The rows of `ext2_batch_lifecycle.h` also check that an unlinked file a shared mapping's record holds, by a node hold and a handle, frees its blocks at its description's last close, whose put leaves only the record's two holds and lets the record go (`filemap_evict_unlinked`, which the test models), and not at an earlier put.
* `ext2_reserve_hosttest.c` includes the ext2 driver's files whole over a mount, an inode and a disk of 64 blocks that the test builds in memory. Rows write two blocks across each boundary between pointer levels and between indirect blocks: with one block too few the write must be `ENOSPC` before any byte is taken unless the caller is root, and with exactly enough each missing pointer block must count once. Others check that existing and pending pointer blocks are not counted again, and that a piece refused for its reservation leaves the earlier pending piece intact. Over a cache model that can call a block missing, a plan under its locks must read no block the cache lacks: a bitmap evicted after its fetch restarts the piece once with one image of each block, and one never kept restarts it eight times before the read under the lock. Stores of another inode and an allocation into blocks the batch holds must reach its images, a drain must reserve a buffer for each image before its first copy, a full cache must be written back only by a process past its share of writes, and a punch run inside another's bitmap fetch must free both files with the inode table dirtied first and the bitmap and descriptor after. A new inode whose parent's group is full must fetch another group's cold bitmap and descriptor block with the group's lock let go, and a write, a truncation, a change of attributes and a `fallocate` must sleep on a held file's lock before admission. Two rows force instruction interleavings: a cache-get hook stops a sparse growth between an inode encode and its store, where the node's word must show a store under way (`EXT2_STORING`) and its lock be held, so that another process's `fdatasync` waits for the store, and the `fdatasync` after it must put the grown size in a separate durable table image; an atomic-exchange hook attempts a device interrupt while the size sequence is odd, with interrupts initially on and off, and requires it deferred until the even sequence, when a big-lock reader can read the whole new size. These rows fail with the node's word cleared before its store and with the size writer's interrupt guard removed, respectively.
* `e2fsprogs_alignment_hosttest.c` includes `compat/e2fsprogs/alignment.h`, which compiles `vendor/e2fsprogs/e2fsprogs-1.47.4/lib/ext2fs/unix_io.c` (with `vendor/e2fsprogs/patches/buffer-alignment.patch` applied) over a sector device in memory that intercepts every data call and seek. Through `pread` and `pwrite`, with a buffer two bytes past a 512-byte boundary and an aligned one, rows check whole transfers of 4 KiB and 64 KiB, a short and an interior write that keep the rest of each sector, and the upstream rules for short counts, retries, zero fill and errors.
* `e2fsprogs_alignment64_hosttest.c` is the same test through `pread64` and `pwrite64`, which it selects by defining `ALIGNMENT_TEST_64`.
* `name_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/fs/name.c` over models of the VFS's holds and checks the capture of a name record by an open without the big lock (`vfs_name_capture_at`): kept with no change of names since the walk's count, and refused with `-EAGAIN`, every hold given back, while a change is under way, after one began and ended since the count, and across nested changes until the last ends; the capture under the big lock is not refused, and a type without `hold` and `put` gets no record.
* `fatname_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/fs/fat32name.c` and `fatcommon.c` and runs the FAT32 name rules alone: the conversion of a UTF-8 name to UTF-16, with 255 units the most and forbidden characters, a trailing dot or space, `.`, `..` and malformed UTF-8 refused; the 8.3 short name and its lower-case bits; the `~N` alias and its numbering up to six digits; the layout of the long entries; and the short name's checksum. Several rows record what mtools writes for the same name.
* `fatcheck_hosttest.c` compiles `tests/host/mkfatfixture.c` into its fixture directory and runs its `--check` over the volumes it builds. Each well-formed geometry, and a volume whose free clusters contain junk, must be accepted; each malformed volume must exit 1 with the finding named for its fault, among them loops, bad chains, cross-links, differing FAT mirrors, lost clusters, BPB faults, orphan long-name entries and the dirty flags. Four rows write long names of 20 and 21 entries and ones with a repeated start.
* `fat32_hosttest.c` links `rach2/sys/fs/fat32.c`, `fat32name.c` and `fatcommon.c` over `rach2/sys/fs/bcache.c` and the disk model of `compat/devmodel.c`, on volumes `mkfatfixture` builds. Rows read the well-formed volume back exactly and refuse each malformed one at mount or fail with `EIO` at the file it damages; run twelve write sequences on one volume that `mkfatfixture --check` walks after each; write long names of every kind; mount dirty volumes; stop each sequence after each of its writes, with short and with long names and on clusters of 4 KiB, judge every state the model can leave, and fail each barrier in turn; require a file on the medium after `fsync`, `sync` or an unmount; and count a write and an `fsync` beside a neighbour of 64 dirty blocks and judge a stop after that `fsync`. Dense interruption snapshots use the same independent `mkfatfixture.c` checker through its read-only in-memory entry, with fresh state and bounded complete output on every call. Valid and malformed volumes must match the prepared CLI's exact status and output, and repeated calls must leave the input bytes unchanged. Ordinary CLI and mtools checks remain.
* `exfatcheck_hosttest.c` compiles `tests/host/mkexfatfixture.c` into its fixture directory and runs its `--check` over the volumes it builds. The well-formed volume, its variants and a volume whose free clusters contain junk must be accepted; each of 25 malformed volumes, and 16 states a crash leaves under the dirty bit, must exit 1 with the finding named for its fault. Read rows copy files out with `--read`, a fragmented file and zeros past the valid length among them, and refuse bad extents.
