and that a second boot whose start finds the boot processor's bit withdrawn leaves the clock stopped while the bit stays so and starts it at the tick once the bit is back, with a time zone set before its page was built on the page. On an x86-64 Linux host the test also assembles both vDSOs' own code, `vdsocode.s` and `vdso32code.s`, which `host-inputs:` names, and runs it from memory below 4 GiB laid out as a program sees it, KVM's clock page, the data page and the image, with each system call replaced by `notl %eax`; the 32-bit code runs in the host kernel's 32-bit code segment, entered by far returns. With KVM's stable bit and a page whose system time is a second past the data page's base, each ABI's clock_gettime, gettimeofday and time, and the 32-bit clock_gettime64, read each clock's base and a second; through two pages that convert the host's TSC since a stamp 2^33 counts back, one shifting left and one right, the monotonic clock reads between what they convert the TSC to before and after the call; with the bit withdrawn each makes its system call; each ABI's gettimeofday gives the time zone `vclock_set_tz` put on the data page, with the time and for a null tv, with no system call, and with the bit withdrawn or in `VCLOCK_NONE` leaves a time and its zone to the system call but gives a zone alone itself; and over the data page as the kernel left it after the bit came back, with KVM's page 50 ns behind its base, each ABI's monotonic clock reads no earlier than the kernel's last reading without the bit. A host that runs no 32-bit code, found by a try in a child, fails the row that asks for it; macOS on Apple's processors and Windows run only the C reader's rows.
* `ntp_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/clock.c` and links `rach2/sys/ntp.c`, and drives them as the timer pass does: one raw tick a pass, `rtc_tick_advance` with the tick length read before it, and `rtc_seconds` at each second of real time. The vDSO's clock does not run, so every reading is the tick's; the monotonic clock is read with no floor, and real time as that clock plus the offset, which must move each tick by its length within 3.2%, and by a second at a leap. Rows check against Linux's arithmetic done by hand for the time constant 2: an offset of 500 ms that the PLL slews by 31.25 us a tick, so that the clock reads 2.031191406 s after 2000 raw ticks and 4.087701988 s after 4000 with 386238 us left, and one of -500 ms, each moving the monotonic clock with real time and leaving the offset, the boot time and the step count where they were with no timer cancelled; the frequency of 64000 units that an offset of 1 ms makes four seconds after the PLL began; a frequency of 100 ppm, read back as 6553600, that runs the monotonic clock 100 us a second fast against the raw clock, which a setting of the clock keeps, and its rate of 3518437208 units of 2^-45, which makes 2.5 ms of raw time 2500249 ns, and 500 ppm's, 2501249 ns, in `rtc_slewed` and between ticks (`rtc_tick_phase`); adjtime's 1.2 ms slewed at 500 ppm from the next second, read back as 700 and 200 us, then 0; `ADJ_SETOFFSET`'s shift, which steps real time and the boot time, forgets the PLL's offset, counts a step and tells the timers, a shift to a real time before the monotonic clock or before 1970, refused, which still tells the timers but counts no step, and a shift of nothing, which counts none; and a leap second of `STA_INS`, `TIME_INS` until midnight, read by adjtimex in the window before the tick as 23:59:59, TAI one more and `TIME_OOP`, then stepped back at the tick with a cancel and a step counted, `TIME_OOP` for the second, `TIME_WAIT` and `TIME_OK` once the bit is cleared, and one of `STA_DEL`, which steps 23:59:59 on to midnight in `TIME_WAIT` with TAI one less. `pll_coarse_rows` makes the fine clock cross second 104 while the last counted time is 103.999 and the PLL reference is 100: a 1 ms offset reads frequency 48000 while the reply reads 104, and a PLL enabled in that window starts its reference at 103. `fll_long_row` steps the clock 8,000,000,000 seconds ahead without advancing ticks, preserving the reference, and checks that a 400 ms offset at time constant 10 reads frequency 800000, which division by a capped 32-bit interval made 800001. It and rtc_hosttest compile their literal 1000 Hz fixtures with an explicit rate; `ntp250_hosttest.c` uses the production 250 Hz rate to check the four-millisecond unit, raw time, a frequency slew and the first PLL correction.
* `vga_text_hosttest.c` includes the text console driver `rach2/sys/display/vga_text.c`, with `fbcon.c`, `vga_font.c` and the console of `rach2/sys/kio.c`, over an array that stands for the adapter's memory, with its stores counted and its ports recorded. Rows count what each operation writes: a byte is one cell, placing the cursor four port writes, a scroll the new line's cells and the start address, and only the 180th scroll copies the window to the front of the memory. Other rows check `textcon_hold` and its release, nested and around interrupts and keyboard echo; the scrollback; `k_putc` and `k_puts`; the answers to a terminal's queries; UTF-8 drawn through code page 437 and the loaded font; the pending wrap in the last column; a stand-in in both fonts for every glyph of the coding agents' interfaces recorded in `tests/fixtures/agents/`; the width table's cells for letters, the agents' marks, CJK, emoji and combining marks, and a wide character drawn in two cells, wrapped before the last column and with a combining mark taking none; and the cursor and keypad modes that `kio_key_modes` gives, and SO and SI, which draw nothing.
* `vga_gfx_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/display/vga_gfx.c` and `vga_font.c` with the adapter's ports and aperture modelled, and runs the change to mode 13h and back with a keyboard interrupt pending at each port access in turn, with the caller's interrupts on and off. Rows check that each mode change keeps interrupts off across its port writes and restores the caller's flags, sets the maximum scan line, and saves and restores the font; that the console's font loads into each glyph's character and reads back, and is refused in a graphics mode; that on a framebuffer console mode 13h keeps its picture in memory of its own, touches no port, and hands the console the screen, the palette and each frame; and that the native mode there is the screen at its own size, pitch and depth, entered and left straight from and to mode 13h, refused off a framebuffer console and on a screen the console refuses.
* `fbdev_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/display/fbdev.c`, the operations of `/fb`, over a model display of mode 13h's picture and a native screen of 64 by 8 pixels, with user memory a checked byte array. Rows check each request's arguments and refusals, `ENOTTY`, `EFAULT` and `ENODEV`, and that a mode is changed under the console's lock; the offsets of a write at the native mode, which move, stop at the end, give `ENOSPC` there and `EFBIG` past it, and leave the description alone through pwrite's copy; mode 13h's whole frames, whose offset stays; a write cut short by a mode another thread left; and the owner's check across fork, by which a child that inherited the descriptor can neither write, ask nor give the display up, while another thread of the owner writes.
* `fbcon_hosttest.c` includes the framebuffer console `rach2/sys/display/fbcon.c` with the text driver and the console, over an array that `fbcon_window` hands it as the loader's framebuffer, and reads the pixels back against the font. Rows check the framebuffers it refuses, which leave the cells as they were; glyphs in their colors, the cursor, a Cyrillic letter reached through UTF-8 and a box corner; pixels of 32, 24, 16 and 8 bits, with and without a palette; the largest whole scale at which the screen has room for 80 columns and 24 rows, with the cells centered; scrolls that draw only the lines that moved, across the copy, and the scrollback; the panic screen's backgrounds; the 320x200 picture of mode 13h, scaled and centered with no cell over it; the native mode, the screen as the loader described it, given black with no cell or picture drawn over it and the cells drawn again after, and refused on an indexed screen and one of 8 bits; and the console's blank, the screen black with no cell drawn until the unblank draws them all again.
* `drm_core_hosttest.c` and its x86-64 wrapper accept `inherited` to run only the new inherited-lock rows after device setup: depths one and two dropped across both copies and an unlocked render or KMS handler, restored on success, bad copies, allocation failure and permission failure, and kept for locked rows and KMS without opt-in. With no argument these rows run with the rest. `drm_core_hosttest.c` includes the DRM core, `rach2/sys/drm/drm.c`, `drm_auth.c` and `drm_gem.c`, and `rach2/sys/proc/kcmp.c`, with i686's `cpu.h`, and `drm_core64_hosttest.c` runs its rows with x86-64's. A fake driver binds a device with a card and a render node, dumb buffers of the shmem helper mapped write-combining and an ioctl of its own, and another with a card alone and no display; the devfs registry is a table the rows open nodes from, and the shmem helper's frames are host pages. Rows check the nodes' names, numbers and modes and the bus names; VERSION, GET_UNIQUE, GET_CAP, SET_CLIENT_CAP, SET_VERSION, GET_CLIENT and GET_STATS; the dispatcher's refusals by type, number, flags and node, and its copies by direction; the master role between two openers, root through a passed descriptor and a close, with magic, authentication and the listener's view of each change; dumb buffers created, mapped, written through the mapping's frame, flinked, opened by name and freed, with the lowest free handles, names and offsets; events reserved, sent, polled, read whole, cancelled and left by a released file; unplug, after which ioctls, reads and mmap answer ENODEV, poll hangs up, a mapping's fault fails and the record goes with its last holder; and kcmp of a dup, a fork's copy and two opens of a node, its order, its refusals and its other kinds, KCMP_EPOLL_TFD with x86-64's registers alone, since an i686 register cannot carry the address of the test's slot, whose model reads a set only under a hold the lookup takes and gives back.
* `drm_kms_hosttest.c` drives kernel mode setting through its ioctl handlers with `drm_kms_model.c`, and `drm_kms64_hosttest.c` runs its rows with x86-64's structures. A fake driver has two CRTCs, the first with a primary plane of three formats and two modifiers, a cursor plane and a gamma table as GAMMA_LUT, the second with a primary plane; an overlay for either; an HDMI connector with the Lenovo panel's EDID and an unplugged VGA one; hooks that count a commit's steps; and Linux's checks of a plane clipped to its CRTC and of a CRTC that is on with its primary plane. Rows check GETRESOURCES, the planes each client cap lists, GETPLANE, the properties the atomic cap shows and their values, an enum's names, GETCONNECTOR's probe by the master and GETENCODER; blobs made, read and destroyed by their owner alone; ADDFB2 with and without modifiers and its refusals, ADDFB, GETFB and GETFB2 with handles for the master and root alone, RMFB, CLOSEFB, the lowest free ID and IN_FORMATS; atomic commits refused for a client without the cap, unknown and conflicting flags, an encoder, a property the object lacks, a value out of range, a source past the framebuffer, an ID of no framebuffer, a route the encoder cannot take, an event with no CRTC and a descriptor that is no sync file, OUT_FENCE_PTR written -1, a modeset refused without ALLOW_MODESET, tested and then committed with its event, a flip waited on an in-fence and a NONBLOCK flip; SETCRTC, SETPLANE, CURSOR and CURSOR2, PAGE_FLIP, DIRTYFB, DPMS and ACTIVE through OBJ_SETPROPERTY and SETPROPERTY, and SETGAMMA and GETGAMMA, each as a commit with Linux's refusals; and a file's release, which turns off the CRTC that showed its framebuffer and frees its blobs, with every allocation and buffer object reference given back.
* `drm_vblank_hosttest.c` drives vblanks, their events and commit fences with `drm_kms_model.c`, the real fences, sync files and sw_sync, and a model descriptor table, and `drm_vblank64_hosttest.c` runs its rows with x86-64's structures. A fake driver has two CRTCs whose vblank interrupt starts a timer, as vkms's does; each step of the test's clock by a frame of 60 Hz fires the vblank of each CRTC whose timer runs, and a wait that would sleep steps the clock until its condition holds. Rows check a modeset whose blocking commit returns at the vblank that sends its event, the count and time it carries, and the interrupt turned off at the first vblank five seconds after its last holder; WAIT_VBLANK relative, absolute, NEXTONMISS, by the high CRTC bits and SECONDARY, with its refusals and the driver's; CRTC_GET_SEQUENCE and CRTC_QUEUE_SEQUENCE; VBLANK, CRTC_SEQUENCE and FLIP_COMPLETE events in the order of their counts, a NONBLOCK flip and EBUSY for one behind it, and the events a CRTC turned off sends at once; a NONBLOCK flip on a sw_sync in-fence that signals five vblanks late, which programs the plane, signals its out-fence and sends its event only after it, a blocking flip on a late fence, and a blocking commit that waits for the deferred one ahead of it; under a driver's `commit_fence_timeout_ms` of 10 s, as i915's, a NONBLOCK flip on a fence that never signals, programmed by the worker once 10 s have passed with its callback taken back, and a blocking flip that waits 10 s and flips; debugfs's `force` words, the EDID override and its refusals, and the CRC source, entries, lines and overflow; a CRTC given a frame counter and a scanout position whose second reading of a frame lies a microsecond before the first, on which WAIT_VBLANK's time stays put at an interrupt of a frame already counted, and a vblank that begins between the counter's read and the time's, at an interrupt and as the CRTC comes on, is counted with its own time, while a driver's time before an earlier one is counted in `backwards`, which debugfs's `crtc-1/vblank_backwards`, read through `drm_debugfs.c` and `fs/debugfs.c`, gives as 0 and then 1 while `crtc-0`'s stays 0, until the device's unregistration takes the files and their holds on it; and an unplug that sends what is queued with -ENODEV on its fences, with every allocation and device hold given back.
* `drmcon_hosttest.c` includes the console's DRM client, `rach2/sys/drm/drm_client.c`, with `drm_auth.c` and the mode-setting files through `drm_kms_model.c`, and `drmcon64_hosttest.c` runs its rows with x86-64's structures. A fake device has one CRTC, an XRGB8888 primary plane and a connected virtual connector of three modes, and dumb buffers whose `vmap` is the test's memory; fbcon, `/fb`, the keyboard and the blank are records of the calls made to them. Rows check that a first open unblanks the console and hands the screen, `/fb` and the keyboard over, and that a master that sets another mode and exits without dropping the role, or drops it, gives them back on the console's own buffer at the loader's size, `/fb` under the console's lock and the keyboard last, the blank's countdown started again before `/fb` comes back; that a master that comes during the console's commit keeps the screen; that `video=` and a hotplug set the size named; that a buffer that cannot be mapped is forgotten until the next place, and on a device whose framebuffers are flushed is drawn through a shadow whose damaged rows a flush copies to the buffer's pages and commits, and not while a master holds the device; that on the firmware's device the CRTC is turned off and fbcon draws on the loader's framebuffer; and that a loader's framebuffer that goes with its device under a master, `drm_client_boot_screen_gone`, is forgotten once, is not attached again when the device leaves, and gives way to the console's own buffer on the next device at once, with every allocation and buffer reference given back.
* `bochs_hosttest.c` binds the std-vga driver, `rach2/sys/drm/bochs.c`, to a register model of BAR 2 and video memory, and links `rach2/sys/cmdline.c` for the boot line its probe reads. Beside the rows of its opening comment, the probe runs under lines of `nomodeset` and `bochs.modeset`, read as Linux's `drm_pci_register_driver_if_modeset` reads them: `nomodeset`, `nomodeset=1` and `nomodesetx` keep the driver off, as do `bochs.modeset=0` and `=0x0`; under `nomodeset`, `bochs.modeset=1`, `=0x10` and `="1"` bind it and `=-1` does not; a value that `kstrtoint` refuses, `x`, an empty one, `1x` and 2^32+1, and a bare word leave the parameter at -1; the last valid occurrence wins; and a word after a bare `--` counts for nothing. `bochs64_hosttest.c` runs the rows with x86-64's structures.
* `drm_edid_hosttest.c` probes a connector of a fake device with `drm_kms_model.c` and compares its modes, in order, its EDID blob and its size with the lists Linux 6.12 made: QEMU's EDID gives virtio-gpu's 26 modes and, behind a CRTC that refuses a frame over half of 16 MiB as Linux's VRAM helper does for bochs, std-vga's 15; the Lenovo panel's gives its one. Rows check that a base block with a bad checksum leaves no EDID and the DMT modes up to 1024x768, that a CEA extension with a bad checksum is kept, that an extension of another kind is dropped with the base block's count and checksum mended, that a header with two bytes swapped is mended, that each probe makes a new blob, and that a disconnected sink clears the EDID and the modes. `drm_edid64_hosttest.c` runs the rows with x86-64's structures.
* `drm_abi_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/drm/uapi.h` and checks the size and alignment of each of its 88 structures and unions, the offset and size of each of their 383 members, and each of its 89 ioctl numbers against Linux 6.12's on x86-64 and on i386. Linux's numbers are rows in the test, read from Linux's own headers with clang for both targets; the host's own ABI is compared with the column of its pointer width, and the i386 column with a layout made again by that ABI's rules from the class of each member's type, so a 64-bit member where Linux has a `long` fails although x86-64 agrees. A host whose 64-bit integer type is its word checks its own ABI alone. `udmabuf_create_list`'s alignment is not compared, since Linux's ends in a flexible array that C89 cannot declare.
* `ia32_fs_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/ia32_abi.h` and checks the size of each i386 structure the x86-64 kernel's compat file and time calls translate, and the offsets of the members they fill, 83 rows in all, against Linux 6.12's, read from Linux's headers and compat types with clang for i386: `stat`, `__old_kernel_stat`, `stat64`, `statfs`, `statfs64`, `flock`, `flock64`, the 32-bit and 64-bit timespec, `timeval`, `itimerspec`, `itimerval`, `utimbuf`, `sigevent`, `sel_arg_struct`, pselect6's sixth argument, the name's offset in `linux_dirent` and the 32-bit iovec. It also checks that a 64-bit argument split over two registers joins low word first with neither word sign-extended, and that the split members of `flock64` and `stat64` round-trip.
* `ia32_net_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/ia32_net.c` with `RACH2_HOST_X86_64` defined, over a model of user memory below 4 GiB and of the shared socket dispatcher. Rows check the i386 `msghdr`, `mmsghdr` and `cmsghdr`, the constants the shared socket code reads them by, the control message space of one and three descriptors and of a `ucred` at 4-byte alignment, the `timeval` of the old time-limit options, `__kernel_sock_timeval` of the new ones and recvmmsg's two timespecs against Linux 6.12; a msghdr read with its pointers and lengths zero-extended and a receive's results written at Linux's offsets, the name's length only with a name; a control message header of 12 bytes read and written; and socketcall reading only its sub-call's words, each zero-extended, with `EINVAL` and `EFAULT`.
* `ia32_ipc_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/ipc/ipc32.h`, `rach2/sys/arch/x86_64/ia32_abi.h` and `rach2/sys/proc/futex.h` and checks the size of each i386 record the x86-64 kernel's compat IPC, message queue, limit, usage and robust list calls read and write, and the offsets of the members they fill, 106 rows in all, against Linux 6.12's, laid out by i686-elf-gcc from Linux's headers: `ipc_perm`, `semid_ds`, `shmid_ds`, `msqid_ds` and `shminfo` without `IPC_64`, `ipc64_perm`, `semid64_ds`, `shmid64_ds`, `msqid64_ds`, `shminfo64` and `shm_info` with it, `msgbuf`, `ipc_kludge`, `mq_attr`, `rlimit`, `rusage`, `sysinfo`, `tms`, `robust_list_head` and the sizes of `old_utsname` and `oldold_utsname`.
* `virtio_gpu_hosttest.c` includes the virtio GPU driver, `rach2/sys/drm/virtio_gpu.c`, with the four mode-setting files and the fences of `rach2/sys/dmabuf/fence.c`, against a model of QEMU's virtio-gpu behind the virtio core's calls: its configuration, two queues that answer at the kick, at the next reap or never, and the 2D commands over resources whose backing is host pages. Rows check a bind with EDID, which reads the EDID and then the displays, fences each command with the next id and lists Linux's 26 modes of QEMU's EDID with no IN_FORMATS, and one without, whose preferred mode is the display's size; a dumb buffer's resource with adjacent pages as one entry; SETCRTC, which copies the whole buffer, sets the scanout and flushes, and DIRTYFB, which copies and flushes the clip alone from its offset; GETFB2 with no modifier and ADDFB2 with one refused; 200 scattered pages, whose entries go on past the command's page; a queue with room for two, which holds three commands until answers make room, in order; a plane update with the scheduler on, over a device that carries commands out only at a kick, as QEMU's virtio-gpu does without an ioeventfd, whose one kick carries the copy, the scanout and the flush, is made without the big lock on virtio-pci's transport and with it otherwise, and still comes when the copy fills the queue; SETCRTC of a buffer whose write fence has not signalled, which sends no copy, scanout or flush until it has, as Linux's drm_gem_plane_helper_prepare_fb makes virtio-gpu's planes wait; an error answer, which fails the fence with EIO; a device that never answers, whose waiter gives up at five seconds and whose late answer the reaper frees; the cursor's UPDATE_CURSOR with its hotspot and MOVE_CURSOR; a display change served at the next open with one hotplug; the driver's ioctls without 3D, where a 2D buffer of RESOURCE_CREATE is busy until its attach is answered and a dumb buffer is idle at once, as on Linux; a buffer's last reference, which unrefs its resource and frees its pages once the host answers; virtio-vga, which removes the firmware's framebuffer from each memory BAR; a device with no scanout; `nomodeset` and `virtio_gpu.modeset=0` on the boot line, which refuse the device with EINVAL, and `virtio-gpu.modeset=1`, which binds it under `nomodeset`, as Linux's `virtio_gpu_probe` decides; an unplug, which fails a command still out with ENODEV; and a reset over a transport whose backing is its own heap, as the shared region's is, which zaps an object no one exported before giving its frames back and leaves an exported one, whose frames an importer such as i915 may hold, its frames until it is freed; and blobs mapped from a transport's page list, whose revoke stops new faults of the blob (`map_ok`), keeps its map and queues the zap rather than running it in the change notice, which the commit worker then runs over the whole blob, holding a reference to it across the zap, in which the program's last reference may go, and freeing it at its own put; whose free with the zap still queued frees the map and takes the zap off the queue; whose device kill holds a blob across its zap while the program's last reference goes in it, frees the blob's map after the zap and the blob at its own put; and one whose UNREF cannot be had, whose free unplugs the device and whose kill passes over it at count 0, with no reference and no zap, gives its frames back at once and leaves the free to free it once. The run ends with nothing allocated and no page held. `virtio_gpu64_hosttest.c` runs the rows with x86-64's structures.
* `vgpushm_hosttest.c` includes the transport over one shared region, `rach2/sys/virtio/shmem.c`, and runs it through the virtio core against a model of the VM's device, which answers the driver's block from inside the transport thread's sleep. The region is 16 pages whose frames are made-up addresses in descending order. Rows check the layout and the table of frames; the handshake of epoch 1, with polling for want of eventfds; each queue in its page of the ring area with the device's bounds of 16 descriptors and 1 MiB; a command and its response; two adjacent pages merged into one descriptor, 17 segments refused with `-E2BIG` and 16 taken; the heap filled until `dma_alloc` and `backing_fill` give nothing and `-ENOMEM`, a gap of one page that two cannot use, and the heap empty again; a resource's backing as a run of the region's frames, each held once, at its offset, with no address for a ring frame or a foreign one; backing across isolated free pages of a fragmented heap, each held once and returned on release, a request above its free-page count refused without taking a page, and adoption failing part way through the third piece with every piece and reference returned; a doorbell counted and taken once, and a closed one that leaves the transport polling; an attach's doorbells with one eventfd given as `bell_out[1]` and `bell_in[0]`, or as `bell_out[0]` and `bell_in[1]`, refused with `-EINVAL` and nothing held once the record is given back, and four distinct ones taken and each given back; a ring of the queue's doorbell whose hook reaps nothing and whose interrupt thread's reap answers the waiter while the driver's interrupts are on, and none after their release; `VGS_IOC_INFO`'s report; a device in NEEDS_RESET that the driver drops; a setup the device refuses, which fails DRIVER_OK; the detach, GONE in a new epoch with every frame and the window given back; and a backing and a command's page held through a detach, whose record keeps its heap's tables, whose frames a region attached again over the same memory leaves out of its heap, and whose releases give them to that heap, the last freeing the old tables; and a backing with pages in the first and third leaves of its table, the second missing, released while the region detaches between leaves, which walks no page of the missing leaf, gives the first leaf's page to the attached heap and the far page to the detached record, and frees the record's tables with it. The memory object's stand-in keeps its pages in a directory of leaves, as `mm/shmem.c` does, and a hook in the test's `kirq_restore` runs the detach at the first point after it is set where interrupts are let in with `vshm_lock` free.
* `virtqueue_hosttest.c` runs the virtio core, `rach2/sys/virtio/virtqueue.c`, against a model device reached only through `struct virtio_ops`, the table a transport fills. The model gives memory a page at a time at device addresses that are not host addresses, as a transport over one shared region gives offsets, and reads the rings only through them, so a host pointer handed to the device fails the row. Rows check the status sequence of a bring-up, the features taken from those offered and wanted, a legacy device, refused features, a reset that ends after five reads and one that never ends, and the queue count; queue sizes cut to a power of two no larger than 128, the rings' layout in one page, and the refusals and memory of a queue's setup; a queue of 8 filled, refused when full, drained in the device's order and filled again with chains of one to three segments until both 16-bit indices pass 65535; a chain longer than the free descriptors in an indirect table, which comes back with its descriptors, and only the writable segments written, and one longer than both a table and the queue refused with `-E2BIG`; with `VIRTIO_F_EVENT_IDX`, a kick that notifies only for buffers past the device's `avail_event`, once for three the device has not taken, and a reap that points `used_event` at the end of what it takes, so that only the first return after it interrupts (`event_index`); a device that asks not to be notified, a returned head not in use, an index ahead by more than the queue, and a completion callback that adds to its own queue; and the configuration read again while its generation changes, the interrupt entries, and teardown, which frees every page after a reset and keeps them after one that did not end.
* `virtio_pci_shm_hosttest.c` runs the capability walk of `rach2/sys/virtio/virtio_pci.c` over a model of two functions' configuration space. The first is QEMU's virtio-vga-gl with `hostmem=4G`, whose shared memory 1 is the whole 64-bit BAR 4 at 14 TiB, followed by a second capability of id 1, which is passed over; the second has regions in an I/O BAR, past their BAR's end, wrapping and empty, each refused with a record, and one valid. No region is mapped.
* `panic_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/panic.c`, `klog.c`, the console and both text drivers, with `k_stop` and the reset jumping back to the row, and reads the cells a panic leaves on the text console at 80 by 25 and 80 by 24 and on framebuffers of 85 by 24, the Lenovo's, and 128 by 48: every cell of the character in her color with nothing written over it and nothing scrolled, the report's lines in order with the reason last, below the picture on the large screen and over its bottom-left corner on the others, for a page fault with its error code and both addresses and for a hardware fault with neither, the countdown to 0 over the default 30 seconds and over `panic=3`, `halted` for `panic=0` and an immediate reset for `panic=-1`, the 30 seconds `k_panic_setup` leaves with no `panic=` on the boot line, `panic_timeout` read once the other processors have stopped, so that a write while they stop sets the wait and a write after, from the crash record's save, changes neither the wait nor the report, a saved crash record, a reason too long for its row cut there, a process's name with an escape shown as `?` on the screen and absent from the port, a panic while the process was read leaving the process out, a fault that another processor notes, as `host_cpu`, leaving the owner's report its own, a real fault in the report's read of the process, a page of `compat/fault_host.c` that faults on every access, whose panic reports the first reason and fault again with no process and logs both reasons, a countdown of six digits on a row with room for fewer shown whole or as `rebooting` alone, blank to the end of its room, on the screen as first drawn, and on the port the record, the report's lines and the reset's line but none of the screen's text.
* `intelbl_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/display/intelbl.c`, the backlight of the Intel PCH's PWM, and drives its attach, get and set against a model of the processor's and the PCH's two PWM registers each. Rows check the Lenovo G50-70's PWM, a period of 937 at full duty, taken without a write, and a level written as the duty cycle with the period kept and clamped to 0 and to the period; the polarity bit, under which the duty cycle is the dark part; a PWM the firmware left off or with no period, refused untouched; and a PWM the processor still drives, whose duty cycle passes to the PCH's, cut to the period, before the PCH's override is set and the processor's PWM turned off, and which is left alone once the override is set.
* `hda_hosttest.c` includes the HD Audio driver `rach2/sys/audio/hda.c`, with `rach2/sys/audio/audio_proc.c` for its report, against a model codec and controller that answer the immediate command interface. Rows run the enumeration and stream setup at each of the twelve PCM rates and the fallback to the one rate a codec advertises; capabilities inherited and overridden and the formats two DACs share; packed connection lists, long and short, with ranges, and invalid lists and paths refused; a start at a lower level and at 0, after which the DAC's and the pin's output amplifiers must have that level's gain and mute bit again; the renderer's silence and sample phase after an empty or partly filled period, from 44100 Hz input at 32000 Hz output; the controllers and codecs taken as HDMI, with the ICH8, ICH9 and Gemini Lake controllers not among them; shutdown with a pending interrupt and a repeated shutdown without MMIO; a verb from the tick refused while the lock is held or a command is under way, touching no register, and a verb the codec never answers, which gives up after 1 ms of the model's wall clock, or 1000 polls when the clock stops, waiting with the caller's interrupts and reading with them off; the report under `/proc/audio`, over a codec with an ADC and a microphone jack whose sense needs a trigger, which prints the text, sends no verbs, gives the queue's fill, delay and position, each widget's capabilities, amplifiers, configuration, sense and connections, and the ADCs, outlives a stop, gives its whole length to a buffer too short for it, and renders both passes of a read from one snapshot, so a capture counter that wraps between them changes neither the length nor the text; and, over a model of the DMA's progress that moves the position and the wall clock together and keeps the sample of every frame played, the period at eleven rates in three descriptors, the ring of three periods in the caller's frames, the delay before and after a refill, a late service that fills both periods played, numbered blocks heard in order without a gap, a writer's stall of a second after which the blocks follow the last played, a second without service whose 14 missed revolutions are counted, after which none of the blocks queued before it plays, and a tick that fills nothing once the output stream's interrupt has come until more than a period and a half has passed since it, and then fills the period played; and, over the G50-70's codec, the input controls, with the two mics as sources, a gain and a boost read and set as percentages of their steps, a source selected and a level set while the input stream runs, and the gain muted at 0; and, over the G50-70's codec, the jacks polled from the tick every quarter second: the output moved to the headphone pin 22 and the speaker 23 turned off on a plug seen twice and back on an unplug, the primary mode that keeps the speaker, a plug that bounces at each poll and moves nothing, a read that times out, one behind it and one that finds the verb lock held, each unknown and none an unplug, no poll while the jacks are held, one tick after a gap of a quarter second that polls them, EAPD and the output pins off at a stop and on at the next start, and capture moved from the internal mic 26 to the jack mic 25 and back while it runs, with the left pin's input off, the mixer's choice kept while the jack is steady, and a plug while it is stopped recorded at the next start. The locks' row checks that the interrupt reads `INTSTS` under the stream lock and lets it go, that the delay counts what the resampler held at the last fill, unchanged when the resampler moves as a fill under way moves it, that the readers, called with interrupts off, take no mutex, that the interrupt's setup registers the handler with `ISR_UNLOCKED` on the line and on a message vector of a model that has messages, that each operation of `hda_driver` that changes the device, each of the fourteen, a start while the stream plays among them, takes the stream mutex once and gives it back, and that no verb is sent under the stream lock. With capture and playback running, the report's row checks that the tick's look reads the input stream under `cap_lock` and the output stream under the stream lock taken under it, that `/proc/audio`'s copy takes the stream lock once, under the stream mutex and `cap_lock`, and copies the report and the mode before it lets that lock go, and that capture's service, finding `cap_lock` held on another processor, waits for it and hands over the periods passed; the model's `khda` takes the services asked for under the stream lock and serves under the mutex. The rows count ticks at the 1000 Hz of the test's `host-cflags:` line, and `hda250_hosttest.c` runs them all at the shipped 250 Hz, where a tone of 100 ms sounds for 25 ticks and the jacks are polled every 63, as Linux's `msecs_to_jiffies` gives them at HZ=250, and the interrupt of a 23.3 ms period is late from the tenth tick after it, 40 ms, not the ninth; at 1000 Hz from the 36th, not the 35th.
* `dma_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/audio/dma.c` against a port-write recorder and a count register that a reading's flip-flop steps through a byte at a time. It checks the single-mask port and byte for every transfer channel; the count each channel reads from its own port, after a write that resets the flip-flop, with no borrow and with a borrow inside the first or the second reading; and no I/O for channel 4 or a channel above 7.
* `sb16_hosttest.c` includes the Sound Blaster 16 driver `rach2/sys/audio/sb16.c` and the test scale of `rach2/sys/audio/audio_debug.c` against port and DMA stubs, with a model of the half of the buffer the DMA reads. Rows check the DMA channel and mode a format takes and a buffer of both halves; the period, the DSP's block and the ring at each of seven rates and formats; a partial frame kept queued while silence goes out, and a short write that keeps the bytes of an incomplete frame; a setup that fails, which leaves playback stopped and the DMA channel masked and refuses samples; playback through a callback, which fills one half at a time, always the one the DMA is not reading, when interrupts find the DMA twice in one half as well as when they alternate, and leaves the ring alone; and the test scale at 44100 Hz, every frame of its five notes and three-period rests as the device reads them, its end, and its report of a device that stops taking frames. The stream rows check playback ownership against another stream and the console, capture refused, period grants from one byte through 16384 in each format and channel count, rates clamped to 5000 and 44100 Hz, the DSP commands for the grants, invalid and running setups refused, both halves primed before a notification, callbacks with interrupts masked, frame position and delay inside a period and across the DMA wrap, a repeated refill replacing its half, stop and restart, close, and retry after each failed DSP byte or DMA setup, with an interrupt at the last setup byte.
* `mixer_hosttest.c` includes `/dev/mixer`'s ioctls, `rach2/sys/audio/mixer.c`, over a model driver that keeps what it is given. It checks the masks of a driver with input controls and of one without, VOLUME and PCM on the hardware model, and PCM private to each caller through the dispatch driver, a level set in the driver by another path read as VOLUME, each source's gain as its level times RECLEV, IGAIN given to every source, the record source written as one source, two, none or a missing one, and the errors, OSS's version and SOUND_MIXER_INFO's count of writes.
* `resample_hosttest.c` includes the resampler, `rach2/sys/audio/resample.c`, and checks it with sines whose phase it knows at every output frame: in the band kept within 0.1 dB and what the conversion adds 80 dB below them, above the lower rate's Nyquist frequency 80 dB down, a constant kept exactly, a full scale step and sine against the filter computed in double, blocks of every size against one conversion, and equal rates copied. It prints the multiplies of a stereo frame at three conversions.
* `oplmusic_hosttest.c` runs the Doom port's OPL music player, `ports/doom/i_oplmusic.c`, with `midifile.c`, `mus2mid.c` and the `opl3.c` emulator, stubs for the zone allocator and the WAD calls that supply the instrument lump, and the port's `DG_TimeSpent` hook counting the emulator's calls. Rows check the loading of the GENMIDI lump, refused when missing, cut short, not cached or with a bad header, and released after; seven-bit channel parameters; a song's length in samples at its tempo; loops at a buffer's end, several in one buffer, and a song of zero length; and a MIDI file of two tracks, the tempo on the first and the notes on the second, played in runs of samples until it stops at its end.
* `fixtures/doom_libc.c`, built by preparation (`tests/cases/host/products.mk`) and run by the check `doom_libc`, links with `ports/doom/crt0.c` and the port's headers as a static freestanding Linux program, with no host C library. Both i686 and x86-64 binaries check long limits, `ssize_t` and `off_t` widths, exact-width format macros and `ll` output, decimal, octal and hexadecimal input, whitespace and failed conversions, empty and nonempty sorts, small `getcwd` buffers, and reads and writes through update streams. Preparation needs both cross compilers; [`tests/cases/host/host_doom.c`](../cases/host/host_doom.c) runs each program on a guest kernel of its ABI.
* `doom_platform_hosttest.c` includes the Doom port's platform layer, `ports/doom/doomgeneric_rach2.c`, with the engine's `i_system.c` and `m_argv.c`, and stands in for the devices, the free memory and the engine's loop. Eight runs start the game and quit through `I_Quit`, as the menu does. They check that `-iwad /demo/doom1.wad` is appended to the arguments, after a caller's own `-iwad`, which takes precedence; that the zone is sized from free memory or `-mb`, smaller when an allocation is refused, and stated in the report; and that a quit, or an exit from a callback, runs the engine's cleanup once with the framebuffer and mouse open, closes each once, prints one report and exits with status 0.
* `doom_format_hosttest.c` links the Doom port's printf formatter, `ports/doom/format.c`, and compares it with the host's `snprintf` over Doom's conversions, among them `%02i`, `%.3d`, `%.8s` and `%79s`, the flags, widths and precisions of each integer and string conversion, and the float conversions at 40 formats over 61 chosen values, their negatives, the infinities, 4,000 random encodings and 4,000 short decimals, where ties to even are met. Rows with glibc's own text, which the host's may not give, compare with fixed strings: `(nil)`, `(null)` cut by a precision, an unknown conversion, a long double and NaN's sign. It also checks the return C99 gives for a cut result, a `size` of 0 and `%n`.
* `doom_sha1_hosttest.c` links the Doom port's SHA-1, `ports/doom/sha1_fips.c`, and checks it against FIPS 180's test vectors: the empty message, `abc`, the messages of 448 and 896 bits, fed whole and in pieces from 1 to 1,000 bytes, and a million `a`s. Every length from 0 to 200 bytes must digest the same whole and a byte at a time, and the engine's helpers must add a word most significant byte first and a string with its NUL. Its lock rows count one take for a locked handler and none for an unlocked render handler or SYNCOBJ_WAIT, and retain the inherited-depth and KMS opt-in checks.

### The network

* `wlan_data_hosttest.c` links the station's data path, its key table, BlockAck reordering and rate selection with the real ciphers, against a scripted AP, a stub radio that may take keys into slots, a clock and a model work queue. It checks QoS transmit with sequence numbers per TID and access categories, CCMP and TKIP both ways in software and in a radio slot, replays per TID and through a radio's stripped PN, a pairwise key replaced with frames under way, whose old slot leaves the new key's counter alone, duplicates, reassembly and its refusals, Michael failures, a group rekey, BIP and protected management frames, ADDBA, reordering, BlockAckReq, the window and the timeout, A-MSDU and a forged one, and rate selection.
* `wlan_dev_hosttest.c` links the radio registry, world regulatory domain, station Ethernet conversion and the Ethernet and packet-socket paths. A stub lower radio and a model worker queue check stable interface indexes, carrier, EAPOL before authorization, IP after it, rejected state changes, LLC/SNAP and bridge-tunnel framing, malformed frames, removal, among it removal while another caller's request to bring the interface up or down sleeps in the worker's way down, the driver's start or the stop that a block during the start asks for (`compat/park_host.c`), an interface that stays down while the worker cannot be started, the radio's rfkill switch, whose block takes the interface down without the model worker running inside itself and refuses to bring it up, also during a sleeping start, and whose release leaves it down unless an up came before the worker applied it or the release came while the worker's stop slept, and channel restrictions.
* `wlan_join_hosttest.c` links the whole station layer, the registry, worker, scan, BSS table, MLME, softMAC station, data path, nl80211, rfkill and wsim, and acts as the medium of `/dev/wsim` and as an open access point. Through nl80211's requests a radio that an open of `/dev/wsim` registers scans channel 6, authenticates with Open System, associates with a control port that passes only EAPOL and is authorized; an IP datagram then goes out as a ToDS frame and one comes in from a FromDS frame. Idle on the AP's beacons, the station polls the AP with a null frame at 1 Mbit/s after 30 seconds, and again after seventeen intervals, 1740 ms, without a beacon, and once more when the AP answers that poll but sends no beacon; it counts the frames and the beacon losses. Taking the interface down deauthenticates, reports the disconnection and stops the radio, and refuses nl80211's requests with `ENETDOWN` until it is up again; an rfkill block leaves the network, stops the radio and takes the interface down from the worker, whose model refuses to run inside itself, and the interface does not come up until the release, when an up that comes before the worker has applied it starts the radio as the worker does. An association that requires management protection gives it to the data path.
* `netdev_hosttest.c` includes the driver table, `rach2/sys/network/driver/netdev.c`, with three model drivers whose verdicts each row sets. Rows check that every card becomes an interface, numbered from one and named from `eth0` in the table's order; that an absent card is passed over; that a card that will not start is stopped and its reason kept while the walk goes on; a machine with no supported card; a card refused by `rach2.nicfail`; calls with no interface bound; the link report; `IFF_RUNNING` following the link; and the carrier a live link keeps for two seconds of ticks across the tick count's wrap, a dead link read at every look, and a change the driver reports read at the next.
* `rtl8139_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/driver/rtl8139.c` over a model of its registers that supplies one complete frame and an overflow status for each interrupt; PCI discovery and DMA are not modelled. Through 1024 interrupts of alternating receive-ring and FIFO overflows, every overflow must be counted, every frame delivered intact and released, and only the first overflow logged. A later receive error must still warn, and a transmit must start under the saved interrupt flag and restore it.
* `rtl8168_hosttest.c` includes the RTL8168GU driver, `rach2/sys/network/driver/rtl8168.c`, over a model chip that answers every register the driver touches and records every write, and compares that record with the order and values of the reference driver. Rows cover the bring-up; a different 8168 and an all-ones window refused, and an absent card reported absent; every handshake that never completes; transmit, with padding and a full ring; receive, with error frames, fragments and short frames dropped; ring wrap; the card's messages and interrupt storms; link changes; and the card's own counters.
* `e1000_hosttest.c` includes the Intel 82540EM driver, `rach2/sys/network/driver/e1000.c`, over a model card that moves descriptors as the manual says and records every write in order. Rows check the bring-up order and values; the station address from the register pair, or else the EEPROM; refusals of a reset or EEPROM that never answers, an all-zero or group address, an unmappable window and an interrupt line past 15; an absent card costing no register access; each of the four receive checks failing alone; ring wrap; transmit padding, an over-length frame and a bounded stall; the stop sequence, of a started card and of one whose bring-up failed; and the link's speed and duplex.
* `packet_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/ethernet/ethernet.c` and `rach2/sys/network/ip/ip.c` with the layers around them replaced by recorders, each frame at the end of its own allocation so that a read past it is a sanitizer report. Receive rows drop a short Ethernet header, a version other than 4, bad header and total lengths, a bad checksum, a fragment and a datagram for another host, and pass each protocol to its parser, the raw layer and the packet sockets. Send rows run `ip_tx`: the MTU on every path, a link that is down, a datagram held on an ARP miss, 60000 bytes at an MTU of 576 held whole on an ARP miss and sent as all 109 fragments when the mapping comes, a driver's refusal, the 64-slot loopback ring overfilled and drained in order, the datagrams a tick finds on it counted as left for the timer, and broadcasts from two cards.
* `nd6hold_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/ip6/nd6.c` and links `frag6.c`, with the Ethernet layer and ICMPv6 replaced by recorders. 6000 bytes sent to a neighbour not yet known leave only a solicitation; the answer sends all five fragments, which make up the payload, once; and a neighbour that never answers gets the held datagram one address unreachable.
* `arp_hosttest.c` includes the address cache, `rach2/sys/network/arp/arp.c`, with the Ethernet layer replaced by a recorder, and feeds it packets one at a time. Rows check that no address a neighbour cannot have is cached; that a request for this machine is answered with its hardware address; that a learned mapping releases the datagrams held for it and a refused one does not; that a seventeenth neighbour replaces the entry longest unused, across a counter wrap too; permanent mappings; malformed layouts; a second interface; and the network lock's balance.
* `netconf_hosttest.c` runs `rach2/sys/network/netconf.c` and the command-line capture of `rach2/sys/cmdline.c`, with ARP and TCP replaced by counters. `netconf_parse_cidr` and `netconf_gateway_ok` refuse octal-looking, out-of-range and trailing text, a subnet's own network and broadcast addresses, loopback, multicast, and a gateway off the subnet. Rows on a /32, a /31 and a /30 check, through those and the setters and `netconf_add_address`, that every address of a /32 or /31 is a host with no broadcast address, a /31's other address its gateway and a /32 with none, and that a /30 refuses its network and broadcast addresses. `netconf_apply` keeps the defaults for an empty line, gives an address without a gateway no gateway, refuses duplicates, and takes `rach2.ip=none`; `rach2.ifaddr=` configures interfaces by MAC and skips bad tokens. `netconf_set_address`, `netconf_set_netmask` and `netconf_set_gateway` change an interface live: a classful mask until the real one comes, the ARP cache forgotten, TCP told under the network lock, the second interface kept apart from the first.
* `netconsole_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/netconsole.c` with the Ethernet layer replaced by a recorder. Rows check that nothing is sent before the console is enabled or while the link is down, and that what was held goes out after; the frame's fields and checksum; a frame for each line and for each full payload; overflow counted rather than written past the ring, and a ring that fills holding whole lines; a link just up sent nothing until it has been up for the settle time, unless the kernel panics; a full ring drained in the fewest frames; a card that refuses frames; a driver that prints from inside the transmit, whose message is held rather than sent from within it; a target told of its lost records by Linux's dropped line in the next record's datagram, or by a gap in the extended `seq`; the records setup logs before it attaches its targets, which keep the runs of drops counted before setup for them; and `netpoll.carrier_timeout=` read once by `kstrtouint` with base 0 at any length, the last valid occurrence counting, with a refusal logged for each value it refuses and each bare key in the line's order, and read no more when netconsole starts.
* `rcvwnd_hosttest.c` runs `rcvwnd_update` from `rach2/sys/network/tcp/rcvwnd.c`, the receiver's choice of when to announce a larger window. Rows check that a drained ring announces its window; that growth below one segment or half the ring, whichever is smaller, stays quiet (RFC 1122, section 4.2.3.3), at ring sizes where each half of the rule decides; that a window is not announced twice; that a ring of 65536 bytes or more is clamped to 65535 unless the window is scaled; and that a zero ring, an overfull one and a zero segment size neither wrap nor divide by zero.
* `tcp_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/tcp/tcp.c` and links `rcvwnd.c`, with IP replaced by a recorder of every segment `ip_tx` would send, the socket layer by a recorder of callbacks, and the clock by a variable. Rows cover the control-block pool (forty blocks, one freed from the middle), a closed port, the handshake, duplicate, overlapping and out-of-order data around a hole, zero windows, a FIN and a half close, the argument checks of `tcp_send`, the peer's MSS, path MTU reports and `tcp_in_flight`, window scaling, selective acknowledgements, ephemeral ports, a driver's transient and permanent refusals, retransmission timeouts and slow start, a timeout kept on time when each call of `tcp_tick` follows four ticks, fast retransmit and recovery, the zero-window probe and its back-off and give-up, to a silent peer, to one that answers and under `TCP_USER_TIMEOUT`, its first probe kept on time with a call every fourth tick, a window closed over data in flight, probed by the retransmission timer with the oldest data again, counted in TCPRetransSegs, kept past 924.6 s by a peer that answers, given up 240 s after a peer goes silent and under `TCP_USER_TIMEOUT`, the first probe over data in flight given up by `TCP_USER_TIMEOUT` counted from the oldest data's send, a segment outside the receive window dropped and answered at most once in 500 ms, a SYN, an acknowledgement of data never sent and one too old refused, the old acknowledgement's bound with the peer's largest window binding, after a stream that went round the sequence space and on a passive connection, judged only below snd_una, so that one 0x7fffffff ahead is dropped unanswered, keepalive probes answered by no refused segment and by no old acknowledgement, data with one moving only the idle clock, a resend of a whole segment and a FIN sent again in CLOSE_WAIT acknowledged and counted in DelayedACKLost, the first probe over data in flight counted from the right send after seventeen sends, past the control block's sixteen runs, and after a send the heap refused a run for, which waits until the heap gives one, data taken before with a new FIN moving the idle clock, a byte sent into this side's closed receive window dropped, round-trip samples, and a SYN sent again with growing waits until the open fails.
* `tcp_reset_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/tcp/tcp.c` with recorders like those of `tcp_hosttest.c` and sends it the segments a host off the path can forge. An established connection takes a reset only at the next expected sequence number, the number before it after the peer's FIN, or the right edge of the rightmost SACK block it sends, acknowledges one elsewhere in the window at most once in 500 ms, and drops one outside it (RFC 5961, section 3.2), and challenges a SYN; a reset taken in CLOSE_WAIT records EPIPE; an opening connection takes only a reset whose ACK acknowledges its SYN; in SYN_RCVD a reset in the window, with data or without, draws at most one challenge in 500 ms, and one at the next number frees a passive block and records ECONNRESET on an active one. After the peer's FIN, data past it ends the connection with a reset at its acknowledgement number, counted in TCPAbortOnData, in CLOSE_WAIT with EPIPE and in LAST_ACK by freeing the block, and after the program's close new data frees the block with a reset in the same way, in FIN_WAIT_1, in FIN_WAIT_2 and in ESTABLISHED while the FIN waits behind data a closed window holds. A close with data unread frees the block with a reset at `snd_nxt`, counted in TCPAbortOnClose, from ESTABLISHED and after `SHUT_WR`; `SHUT_RD` alone leaves ESTABLISHED taking data, and with `SHUT_WR` data past the FIN in FIN_WAIT_2 ends the connection with ECONNRESET and a reset while the socket keeps its block; a shutdown in SYN_SENT frees the block, sends nothing, records ECONNRESET and gives back an ephemeral port but not a bound one, and a later shutdown of the socket with no block takes the flags and fails with ENOTCONN, and keeps them when it connects again. A shutdown in SYN_RCVD after a simultaneous open takes the flags and sends no FIN, then or at the handshake's end, and a send there waits and then fails; one in TIME_WAIT with the socket attached, or after the peer's reset, takes the flags and fails with ENOTCONN; `SHUT_WR` leaves a listener listening, and `SHUT_RD` stops it, freeing its block and handshakes, emptying its queue, and leaving it free to listen again. A queued connection nobody accepted is ended with an RST and ACK at `snd_nxt` from ESTABLISHED and CLOSE_WAIT, counted in EstabResets, and freed with nothing sent once the peer has reset it. In SYN_RCVD a resent SYN draws the SYN-ACK at most once in 500 ms, an ACK of another number a reset, a segment past the window an acknowledgement, held back in the same tick only without data, and a SYN at another number a reset that frees the passive block; the ACK that ends a handshake brings its data and FIN; and after a simultaneous open a SYN outside the window draws a challenge, the peer's SYN-ACK ends the handshake, and so does an ACK of the SYN's own number, with no reset and the SYN left in the send ring until an ACK of it, which the retransmission timer meanwhile sends again as the SYN-ACK at the initial sequence number with a SYN's options. A handshake completed after its listener closed is answered with a reset and never accepted. An initial sequence number is the clock, 2500 a tick, plus SipHash-2-4 of the addresses and ports under a key drawn once after `tcp_init` (RFC 6528).
* `socket_wake_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/socket/socket.c` over protocol layers no row calls, and records each wake and the kinds of change it names. A UDP datagram and TCP data wake a socket for data alone, and data a full receive ring takes none of wakes nothing; freed send-ring space wakes it for room alone; a FIN, a connection that completes or ends, and a connection queued on a listener wake it for every kind. An edge-triggered epoll registration for one kind is thus not told of the other. A stream shut for receiving whose connection was then reset reads ECONNRESET once and then 0; a send on a socket a shutdown in SYN_SENT left with no block fails with ECONNRESET once and then EPIPE; a stream with no block reads ENOTCONN and polls POLLHUP and POLLOUT, and once shut for receiving reads a recorded error once and then 0 and adds POLLIN and POLLRDHUP; a UDP socket shut for receiving gives the datagram queued, then ends a blocking receive with 0 while one that does not wait gets EAGAIN, and polls POLLIN and POLLRDHUP, and POLLHUP shut both ways; and a closing listener resets the connection nobody accepted before releasing it.
* `raw_hosttest.c` includes `rach2/sys/network/socket/raw.c` and registers a socket for ICMP. A datagram of another protocol is not queued and wakes nothing; an ICMP datagram is queued whole, keeps its source address, and wakes the socket for data alone, so that an edge-triggered registration for output, which a raw socket always has, is not told of it. Two ICMP datagram sockets hold identifiers of their own; an echo reply goes to the socket of its identifier alone, without the IP header, an echo request with that identifier to neither, and an identifier is free again once its socket ends. An echo socket reports port 0 until a connect fixes its identifier, then that identifier, which its requests carry and its replies reach; a bind gives the identifier asked for, which the requests then carry, refuses one another socket or a kernel ping holds with EADDRINUSE, and refuses a second bind with EINVAL; an echo reply with a bad ICMP checksum is dropped for the echo socket and taken by a raw one.
* `netif_hosttest.c` includes the socket calls of `rach2/sys/syscall_net.c` and `rach2/sys/network/socket/packet.c` over a model of two interfaces, with user memory a small arena addressed by offset. Rows check `SO_BINDTODEVICE`; the local addresses of packet and TCP sockets; a frame that wakes a bound packet socket for data alone; accept refused on datagram, non-listening and raw sockets before any wait; the creation flags of `socket` and `accept4`; an accept the heap has no room for, which returns `ENOMEM` and keeps the connection queued; a `recvfrom` with an address and no length pointer, which returns `EFAULT`; and another thread's close of the descriptor accept reserves while it copies the peer's address.
* `dhcpmsg_hosttest.c` runs the message half of the DHCP client, `utils/dhcpmsg.c` with `utils/ipcheck.c`, on messages copied to heap blocks of their exact length and built with the test's own checksum code. Rows check the discover and request of `dhcp_build` (padding to 300 BOOTP octets, lengths, checksums, a zero UDP checksum sent as all ones); the refusal by `dhcp_frame_ok` of short, malformed and fragmented frames; `dhcp_parse` on a good reply, on replies to another transaction or hardware address, and on truncated or hostile options; `dhcp_lease_usable`; `dhcp_sane_lease`; and the renewal and rebinding times of `dhcp_boundaries`.
* `dhcpc_hosttest.c` includes the DHCP client, `utils/dhcpc.c`, and links `dhcpmsg.c` and `ipcheck.c`; its files go to a private directory under `/tmp`, its ioctls and standard descriptors to models, and its waits to a modelled clock. Rows check failed address removals, at expiry too; a first lease applied when cleanup fails, when its claim cannot be recorded, and when the interface already had the address; the classful mask of a lease without one; overlapping resolver writers; unreadable and absent claims; a reply wait that lasts to its deadline; a rebinding request sent again at half of what is left; standard descriptors that cannot be redirected; and the claim of an adopted address.
* `netcfg_hosttest.c` runs the `/etc/network/interfaces` reader, `utils/netcfg.c` with `utils/ipcheck.c`, over files written in its fixture directory. Rows read DHCP and static stanzas, several interfaces, one named twice, the selection with and without `-a`, and inet6 stanzas stepped over; refuse an unknown method, a value that is not an address, an overlong line, too many interfaces and a missing file; check names against their field; and check the refusals of `netcfg_check_static`: a missing address or netmask, a netmask that is not a run of ones, the subnet's own addresses, which a /31 and a /32 do not have, and a gateway off the subnet, on this machine, multicast, loopback or on network zero.
* `netcfg_io_hosttest.c` includes `utils/netcfg.c` with `fopen`, `fgets`, `ferror` and `fseek` replaced by a stream model over two complete stanzas. After an ordinary two-pass read, a read error after the first stanza in either pass and a failed seek between the passes must each make `netcfg_read` refuse the whole file.
* `netup_hosttest.c` includes `utils/netup.c` and links `netcfg.c` and `ipcheck.c`, with `fopen`, `ferror`, `kill`, `socket`, `fork` and `waitpid` modelled, so no client is started. `dhcp_holder` must take an absent claim or a dead holder as free and an unreadable, unread, empty or live claim as busy, and `apply_static` must open no socket while the claim is unreadable. `run_dhcp` must report a client that reached the background as success, and one that exited or was killed first by its status or its signal.
* `resolv_hosttest.c` includes the stub resolver, `utils/resolv.c`, with its socket calls and clock replaced by a model of one connected UDP socket that discards datagrams from any other peer. `dns_query` must send on one IPv4 socket connected to the nameserver's DNS port, take the nameserver's answer, and close the socket once, so that an answer or NXDOMAIN from a foreign address or port cannot decide the name; a failed connect fails the query without sending. The kernel's UDP delivery is left to the guest probes.
* `ncport_hosttest.c` includes `utils/nc.c` and calls its `parse_port` directly. Ports 1, 80 and 65535 are taken; 0, 65536, 65616 (which a cut to 16 bits would make 80), a trailing character, an empty operand, a sign and a leading space are refused, and a twenty-digit operand, run last, is refused without overflow.
* `ncwrite_hosttest.c` runs the `main` of `utils/nc.c` with `read`, `write`, `socket`, `connect` and `close` modelled: 1024 bytes of input and 1024 of reply, through writes of at most seven bytes. Both outputs must receive every byte, also when one write is interrupted with `EINTR`; a write that returns zero or fails with `EIO` on either output must stop the transfer with status 1.
* `sshpeer_hosttest.c` includes the readiness and deliberate-flood helper with modeled sockets, reads and polling. Every refused identification requires a completed 50-millisecond wait before another connection; an interrupted receive or wait opens no extra socket, an immediate identification needs no delay, and connection or wait failures remain fatal. The flood still waits for its held sockets and retains its indefinite ownership wait without readiness pacing. No row judges elapsed time.
* `httpd_hosttest.c` runs the `main` of the status server, `utils/httpd.c`, over modelled calls: `accept` gives one connection, `recv` one request, `send` keeps the page, and `getsockname` answers with the address and port a row chooses. The page must name the client and the address and port the connection reached, or 0.0.0.0 and the bound port when `getsockname` fails. Port operands of 0, 65536, `80x`, an empty one and twenty digits are refused with the usage line and status 2.
* `wifi_hosttest.c` includes `utils/wifi.c` and runs its `main` against a fake wpa_supplicant, a child that binds the control socket in the test's directory, answers the requests as wpa_supplicant does from a table of networks, sends a monitor the events of a scan, a connection, a refused passphrase and an enterprise server's certificate, and writes each request it takes to a file. Rows check the scan's list, one line per network at its strongest access point, the strongest first; a personal network joined, written with bgscan and saved, and the other networks enabled again; a refused passphrase, whose network is not kept; an enterprise network probed with `ca_cert="probe://"` and pinned by its hash on yes, with no password set before the answer; one refused on no and removed; a pinned network whose server presents another certificate, refused and kept, and the reason `wifi status` finds in the log; `wifi list` with a pin, `wifi forget`, the usage and the decoding of a name.

### Boot, disk tools, accounts and the shell

* `bootctl_hosttest.c` runs `utils/bootslot.c`, the library `bootctl` drives, over FAT32 images that `mkfatfixture.c`, written apart from it, builds in the test's fixture directory, through a write callback that counts each sector of a run as one write and can drop a sector while reporting success, stop after a chosen write, or tear a sector. Rows check that a well-formed image opens and a kernel installs with the fallback saved and a one-boot trial armed; the commit, the cancel and the copy into the fallback; each malformed volume refused with its own diagnostic; kernel validation; the menu slot written whole and read back; volumes of 4 KiB clusters; unknown variables kept by the environment writers; and a dropped, cut or torn write, which fails the install with the fallback still saved and the trial not armed.
* `bootslot_hosttest.c` includes `utils/bootslot.c` over a sector model that begins after FAT discovery. Rows check that `bootslot_validate_kernel` takes an x86-64 kernel with the a.out kludge and refuses one without, and refuses a multiboot2 header that is absent, has an unknown tag or one past its end, has an address tag but no entry tag, or loads or enters outside the file, the load or the first 8192 bytes; and the disk policy an image declares, of which only read-write is taken. Other rows check a chain's cluster count past 4 GiB, that kernel slots smaller than `BOOTSLOT_SLOT_BYTES` are refused with the command that moves the disk, an install refused before any write when the fallback is unverified, and a fallback copy whose payload write is dropped, whose read fails, or whose final header write is dropped or torn.
* `bootverb_hosttest.c` runs `bootverb_parse` and `bootverb_known` of `utils/bootverb.c`, the command line of `bootctl`. Rows check each of the nine verbs, `status`, `commit`, `cancel`, `kernel`, `config`, `grub`, `trial`, `env` and `fallback`, with the operands it takes and the operand it returns, `env` with a key alone or a key and a value; and refuse a missing or surplus operand, the old spellings `--commit` and `--where` and a bare kernel path, an empty word, a prefix, a suffix and capitals. Other rows check that a refusal clears the returned operand, that both operand outputs may be NULL, and that every verb the parser takes is one `bootverb_known` knows.
* `grubslot_hosttest.c` runs `utils/grubslot.c`, which lays a boot loader window out as an entry sector and two tails of the GRUB core and installs a new core into the tail not in use. Rows check the layout of the post-MBR gap, LBA 1 through 2047, with tails of 1023 sectors at LBA 2 and 1025, and of a BIOS boot partition at LBA 34 through 2047, with tails of 1006 at 35 and 1041; windows refused over sector 0, too small or past 32 bits; which tail an entry sector points to; the blocklist patch, where a core of 1007 sectors fits the partition's tail and one of 1008 does not; installs that leave the old tail and sector 0 alone; and core images refused, with nothing written.
* `grubembed_hosttest.c` includes `tools/image/grubembed.c` and calls `bios_boot_partition`, its finder of the BIOS boot partition the GRUB core is written over, on GUID tables the test builds with their checksums in a temporary file. Rows take a partition over sectors 34 to 2047, as this project's disks have it, and refuse one that starts at sector 2, over its own entry array, one that starts before the first usable sector, and one that ends past the last.
* `gptdisk_hosttest.c` includes `utils/gptdisk.c` with its `write` counted, over an image file of 2048 sectors whose GUID table the test builds byte by byte, made 4096 sectors long as an image written to a larger disk is. Rows check the table read from its primary, rewritten with its backup at the disk's end and the protective MBR's boot code kept, read from that backup alone and grown into the space; a partition under the backup refused; and the disk GUID's text form both ways. A rewrite stopped after each of its writes, from either copy, must leave a copy that reads, and a primary of 136-byte entries, which the kernel refuses, leaves the table to be read from its backup.
* `diskctl_hosttest.c` includes `utils/diskctl.c`, with `utils/gptdisk.c`, and runs `diskctl esp` on an image file whose MBR entry 1 has type 0x0C, with the kernel's table reread answered by the test. While the reread is refused with `EBUSY` the command writes type 0xEF and fails; run again with the type already on the disk it must have the table read again and succeed, as must a third run; and an entry of another type is refused, unread and unchanged. Its `data` rows use an empty GUID table to require that a start below the first usable sector, or one whose rounding overflows, leaves both copies unchanged and never asks for a reread; the free-run lookup enforces the same lower bound.
* `growroot_hosttest.c` includes `utils/growroot.c`, with `utils/gptdisk.c`, and answers its device calls from memory and its `/proc` files from temporary streams. Rows check that a valid MBR is written once from the sector the writer checked, with only the root's length and ending CHS bytes changed, and that a failed or short read, a changed disk or partition field, or a partition in the space to grow into stops it before any write. Three rows check that `gpt_limit` bounds a GUID table's root by the entry after it, or by the backup array when there is none, and stops at an entry that overlaps the growth. A run with `--measure` forks a child for each tool and checks the eight measurement lines against counters the children change.
* `sectorcopy_hosttest.c` runs `utils/sectorcopy.c`, the copy library of `reflash`, against a model stream and device. Rows check `sectorcopy_parse_u64` on decimal numbers up to the largest 64-bit value, refusing one past it, a sign, hexadecimal, white space and trailing text; `sectorcopy_bounds` at the ends of 64 bits and for an offset or length inside a sector; copies from a stream that hands out a byte at a time, ends early, has more than it declared, or fails, including on the read that shows it ended, each written to the device in whole sectors; an image of 2 MiB and 32 KiB written in exactly three writes of 1 MiB, 1 MiB and 32 KiB; and the progress lines, one at each step and one at the end.
* `sha256_hosttest.c` runs `utils/sha256.c`, the digest `reflash --verify` prints. Rows compare its digests of the empty message, the two vectors of FIPS 180-4, messages of 55, 56 and 64 bytes, where the padding adds one block or two, and a million `a` characters fed a thousand bytes at a time and again one byte at a time, with digests written in the test.
* `mkhash_hosttest.c` links musl's SHA-512 crypt, `vendor/musl/musl-1.2.5/src/crypt/crypt_sha512.c`, as the `krffs/mkhash.krffs.c` tool does, and checks `__crypt_sha512` against the two vectors of the SHA-crypt specification, one with `rounds=10000`, and one of the project's own, and that a setting that is not SHA-512 gives no hash.
* `pwauth_hosttest.c` runs the credential library `utils/pwauth.c` with musl's SHA-512 crypt, supplying `crypt`, `getspnam`, `getrandom` and an `fsync` that fails on a chosen call, and interposing `snprintf` and `getpid` to read the signal mask where the library blocks signals. Rows check the password comparison against the SHA-crypt vectors, with a locked, empty or missing entry denied; the salt mapping; the replacement of a file, before and after its rename; the lock protocol through the `_at` forms; and, mostly in child processes, what a writer does with its lock under `fork`, `exec`, unexpected signals, `SIGPIPE`, a full descriptor table and `exit`, and how the password prompt leaves the terminal when started in the background or continued during its teardown. Retry intervals are counted by an interposed `sleep`; a contending child waits for its parent to release the real lock before it retries. Pipe notifications at slave-terminal setup, the refusal report and teardown newline order the signal checks without sleeps.
* `pwauthfault_hosttest.c` includes `utils/pwauth.c` with system-call stand-ins that fail on demand. Rows check the parse of the process id in a lock file, which refuses an embedded NUL, text past the first buffer, ten digits, zero, leading space and split digits and completes short reads; that a failure to turn echo off, at the start or on resume, stops the password read before it reads; that a terminal mode error stops it while `ENOTTY` reads with no echo change; and that a lock or temporary file that cannot be removed is reported by name and returned as a failure, with the target unchanged.
* `pwauthatexit_hosttest.c` runs `utils/pwauth.c` with an `atexit` that takes the first registration and refuses the second; it is a program of its own because the stand-in replaces `atexit` for the whole process. It checks that `cred_txn_begin_at` then refuses the transaction and leaves no lock file, that it tried both registrations, and that the one function registered does not change a `SIGPIPE` disposition it never saved.
* `accounttools_hosttest.c` includes `utils/login.c`, `su.c` and `sudo.c` with the identity calls and descriptors modelled, so the host's identity does not change. Rows check `sudo`'s policy file, which takes a line that is exactly a user id and refuses one past a long comment or line, a suffix, a sign, a NUL and a missing file; `login`'s read of a name, which ends at end of file, discards a line too long for the buffer, and refuses an empty read or an error; and that `login` closes every descriptor from 3 to 1023, and `su` and `sudo` do so before they change identity.
* `brightness_hosttest.c` runs the brightness keys' action, `krffs/acpi/brightness.script`, with the host's `/bin/sh` over a backlight class of its own that `BACKLIGHT_CLASS` names. Rows check the Lenovo's range of 937 stepped down by 93 to 46, a twentieth, and back up to 937; a range of 15, whose step and floor are 1; a level below the twentieth that a step down leaves and a step up raises; the direction taken from the name the script is run by, as acpid runs `/etc/acpi/brightness/up`; a direction refused with status 2 and nothing changed; and a level that is no number, which fails the run with status 1 while the other backlight steps.
* `airplane_hosttest.c` runs the airplane key's action, `krffs/acpi/wifi.script`, with the host's `/bin/sh` over a switch class of its own that `RFKILL_CLASS` names, and a stand-in for BusyBox's `rfkill` first on `PATH`, which logs its arguments and sets the Wi-Fi switches' soft blocks. Rows check the Lenovo's two Wi-Fi switches beside a Bluetooth one, blocked by a press and unblocked by the next with Bluetooth left alone; switches that disagree, all blocked; a switch hard-blocked with its soft block clear, which counts as on; and a class with no Wi-Fi switch, and an empty one, on which `rfkill` is not run.
* `vconsole_hosttest.c` links `utils/vconsole.c` and checks defaults, quoted assignments, duplicate assignments, empty settings, malformed and oversized input, embedded NULs, numeric limits, command arguments and a US reload after failure of each tool. Its streams are closed on every path and its fixture directory is removed on success or named as evidence on failure.
* `initpolicy_hosttest.c` includes `utils/init.c` with its `exec` modelled and the reboot call replaced by an exit status. Rows check the SSH policy file, which takes the exact line `root-password-login yes` with trailing white space and refuses any other text; the command lines of OpenSSH's `sshd` and of Dropbear with and without root's password; that a service whose descriptors cannot be redirected does not run, and the network setup leaves init's descriptors alone; that, with a real `fork` and signal delivery, a shutdown signal at the fork of the network worker or the SSH server, or while `ifup` runs, ends the child and leaves init's mask and handlers as they were; and that the console's login starts with `LOGIN_TIMEOUT=0`.
* `file_read_hosttest.c` includes `utils/cp.c`, `cat.c` and `ls.c` with in-memory I/O.
