Rows check normal EOF and `EIO`, before and after partial input, the status and path in the diagnostic, descriptor releases, short writes, standard input and the default directory. A successful print changes errno so directory EOF must be distinguished from errors left by earlier calls.
* `rm_hosttest.c` includes `utils/rm.c` and runs its command entry point on host files and directories, with an `unlink` that answers `EACCES` for one path a row chooses. Rows check that a denied symbolic link, as an operand and inside a directory removed with `-r`, stays a link and leaves its target's bytes alone, and that the command removes a link, a file and a tree with a dangling link in it, requires `-r` for a directory, and fails on a missing operand.
* `ish_hosttest.c` includes the ISH shell, `ish/ish.c`, with `ish_shell_utilities.c` and `ish_cstring_utilities.c`, and the cases of the guest probe `tests/musl/ishmusl.c`, with wrappers that make waits fail and reads come short or fail. Rows check whole input lines, read boundaries, end of file and long lines; redirection when `open` returns a standard descriptor; `HOME` and `PATH` matched only by their exact names; an interrupted wait retried, and a failed one, or one for another child, decoding no status; reads a byte at a time and read errors; a path joined up to its limit and refused past it; and a search path entry too long to join, skipped with status 127.
* `fixdir_hosttest.c` includes `compat/fixdir_host.c`, the fixture directory of the host tests, and checks the `$TMPDIR` values it takes and the ones that send the directory to `/tmp`: a semicolon, a space, a redirection, an ampersand, a wildcard, a quote, a dollar sign, a relative path and one of 101 characters. Child processes check that each makes its directory where those rules say, that `fix_format` ends the process with status 1 when its text does not fit, and that the directory is kept, with its files, after an exit without `fix_passed`, and removed otherwise, unless LeakSanitizer finds a leak, a row run on Linux.

The serial transport fixture also keeps one real responder command running as an owned service while a second command and its file transfers complete. It requires cancellation to finish the original service owner with no surviving process group. Scripted frames check interleaved output, normal service completion, refusal of a second service, preserved measurement strings and unconfirmed cleanup after console loss.

The native observer fixture checks that a joined owner sampler records a real terminal counter sample covering its cleanup reading, and that its trace imports. A terminal sampler that is stopped or cancelled, or whose owner exits before the terminal reading, writes an incomplete footer that cannot supply comparison evidence; the lost owner is a POSIX row, since it forks the owner.

The scenario fixture also copies ranges to and from a sparse file at the USB far partition's actual position past two terabytes. Independent wide writes seed extraction, and independent wide reads judge insertion and neighboring bytes, so two narrowed calls cannot conceal the wrong location. Only the short payload extents are allocated.

`etude_remote_hosttest --routine-only` exercises the ordinary route wrapper through the existing C responder. The same compiled fixture supplies its private SSH stand-in and staged native child. It checks file staging, both output streams, a returned proof, failure results, handshake loss, a nonzero transport exit and control-input abandonment, and requires all children reaped. It uses no real remote endpoint. The Windows pipe and Job adaptation retains its platform acceptance check.

`etude_platform_hosttest --routine-entry-only` calls the native routine input, phase, preparation, sequence and result-projection implementations. Its command substitutes preserve all fourteen projection and refusal modes, focused push filters, declared clean preparation, allocation before production, counted vectors and the Windows/WSL request identity. It creates only private fixture files. The ordinary platform fixture runs these rows too, in the `etude_platform` check on the collection host; the GNU helper does not build this binary.

`etude_platform_hosttest --routine-listener-only` runs the existing listener regression through its native entry. The ordinary platform fixture runs it too, including boot identity, accepted jobs and changed-listener readback refusals.

`etude_scenario_hosttest --net-bulk-only` checks the existing network-case raw disk path with a full patterned 4 MiB file. It requires the input to bypass serial copying, the complete returned bytes and digest, and client stop/read/restart order. Short or changed returns and unconfirmed client exit publish no result. It boots no machine.

The virtual channel rows of `dsp_hosttest.c` also cover two readers receiving the same input, a slow reader and a reset leaving the other alone, per-process PCM applied before summing with ownership kept across an inherited open, the console joining and leaving a DSP player, the format, channel and rate queries on a duplex open whose capture rate differs from playback's, which keep both directions running with their data, and capture of the mix on a playback-only device through loopback. A row calls the mix's `transfer` with interrupts on and its `period` with them off, as HD Audio's `khda` does under its stream lock, and checks that each gives back the spinlocks it takes, waits for nothing, calls no operation of the driver, and that `period` wakes the writer.

`fence_hosttest.c` includes the fence, dma-buf, sync-file and software timeline code whole over a descriptor and scheduler model. It checks context ranges, callback removal, an interrupt, raised by a callback's model device and taken when the signal unmasks, that frees two points of a chain while another fence's callbacks run (`--interrupt-only` runs it alone), waits and their timer restoration, merged producer errors and callback teardown, reservation snapshots, buffer size and inode identity, sync-file merge and info, implicit poll wakeups, CPU access flags, deadline hints and timeline close. The guest `udmabufprobe` checks the real memfd and mapping path.

`syncobj_hosttest.c` and `syncobj64_hosttest.c` include the fence, sync-file, software timeline and syncobj code whole over models of descriptors, eventfds, handle tables and a scheduler whose sleep runs the producer step a row arms. They check binary waits for any, all and a submission, Linux's refusals, a syncobj shared between two opens through its file, out-of-order timeline points and the point each query reaches, waits at points, transfers, eventfds, a failed producer's error read through every consumer's sync file while the syncobj waits succeed, the calls refused by drivers without the features, allocation failures that change nothing, and a chain of 200,000 points freed without recursion.

`debugfs_hosttest.c` includes debugfs and the fence, sync-file and software timeline code whole over the same kind of descriptor model. It checks the root's mode 0700, each node's mode, links and inode, lookups with `.` and `..`, listings in order, directory paths and statfs's magic; the registration errors, an open the file refuses, and a directory taken out while its nodes are held; and a timeline opened through `sync/sw_sync`, its fences made and signalled through the file, and `sync/info` read before and after, in Linux's text.

`vgpu3d_hosttest.c` and `vgpu3d64_hosttest.c` run only their new virgl rows over the virtio GPU model: capability descriptions and payloads, a second read answered from the cache, per-file contexts, 3D resource fields, byte transfers in both directions, submissions queued without sleeps, foreign in-fences, sync-file and syncobj output fences, out-of-order completions and device reset with commands outstanding. They use real fence and chain code and require all model allocations and backing pages released.

`vgpublob_hosttest.c` and `vgpublob64_hosttest.c` run the blob rows of `rach2/sys/drm/virtio_gpu_blob.c` over the 3D model, which they teach CREATE_BLOB, MAP_BLOB, UNMAP_BLOB, ASSIGN_UUID and SET_SCANOUT_BLOB, with a host-visible region at 14 TiB whose first megabyte is host memory. Rows check the parameters of a device with blobs, UUIDs, context initialization and capability sets 1, 2, 4 and 6; blobs of host memory, whose commands go before their CREATE_BLOB, which MAP_BLOB places first fit, which map whole with the host's cache type, whose pages and kernel window reach their piece alone, and which unmap before their UNREF; a full region and a refused map, which leak nothing; blobs of guest memory and of both, with their entries, contexts, UUIDs, transfers and refusals; devices without context initialization, UUIDs, the region or virgl; CONTEXT_INIT with each capability set, its refusals, which change nothing, and its name; rings, each its own fence context, whose answers retire only their own ring's fences, whose polled ring alone sends an event, and whose dependencies on another ring are waited for; PRIME export and self-import of a blob; a blob as the scanout; and the copies a submission counts in `virtio_gpu_copied_bytes`: the boxes of TRANSFER3D, COPY_TRANSFER3D and RESOURCE_INLINE_WRITE at their resources' formats, a block-compressed one among them, found in an unsorted buffer list with repeats, on a virgl and a virgl2 context, none on a Venus context, and none, with the submission still taken, when the allocation of the count's table alone is refused. Each row ends with nothing allocated and no window mapped.

`futex_pi_hosttest.c` and `futex_pi64_hosttest.c` exercise PI futex ownership, trylock and unlock errors, atomic retries, donation through blocked owners, cycle detection, ordered handoff, exit and condition-to-mutex requeue. Their model uses the real futex implementation with both kernel structure layouts. `sched_pi_hosttest.c` uses the real scheduler to check FIFO precedence and yield, equal-priority RR rotation, inherited priority above base RT priority, and selection after deboost.

`timercal_hosttest --idle` runs only the stopped-tick rows: a timer stopped with no event, a stale pending tick, a long one-shot event, a whole-tick deadline, phase-preserving restart and the TSC deadline and its clear. `sched_hosttest --idle-only` runs only idle accounting: a remote projection with steal time, catch-up once on wake, the first busy tick and the phased load sample. Both sets also run in their model's ordinary invocation.

`process_timer_hosttest --event-owner` runs only the event ownership row: a pass on another CPU rearms no future wake, its owner rearms it, and either may expire it once due. The idle rows of `timercal_hosttest` also force a smooth clock ahead of an uncountable coarse tick, keep an unchanged programmed event, and leave periodic mode on first idle with TSC-deadline support.

`kvmguest_hosttest` models the guest's KVM records: independent feature setup, per-CPU EOI word registration and local and remote steal-time reads.

`itco_hosttest`'s elapsed rows check irregular service spans, zero elapsed time, the open deadline, and the next service across a tick-count wrap. The ordinary run ends with them, and `--elapsed-only` runs them alone. `timercal_hosttest --idle` also checks the physical/guest 4 ms scheduler phase and an independent 1 ms event.

`process_timer_hosttest --fine-alarm` runs its fine-alarm rows alone: expiry and posting without a coarse tick, nanosecond readback, phase after late signal consumption and units preserved by exec. The ordinary run includes these rows.

`timercal_hosttest --hz250` runs only its physical/guest common-phase row at the production rate, with a fine event and a stopped idle span. Its other event rows express positions in logical tick units and counts at the model's actual device rate.

`kvm_irq_hosttest --fine-timer` runs only the half-millisecond halted-vCPU deadline and hardware-bound rows, including a synthetic timer with no APIC timer armed. `kvm_svm_hosttest --fine-timer` runs only the software-event arming row. Their ordinary runs include these new rows.

`i915_base_hosttest --fine-forcewake` runs only its new two-millisecond forcewake release row, with the monotonic clock independent of HZ and the deadline published after the register lock. The shared i915 clock fixture uses explicit milliseconds for these driver rows.

`pci_affinity_hosttest.c` runs the real PCI message allocator over the configuration-port model on four processors: MSI and MSI-X share allocation counts, affinity changes retain the vector, invalid masks preserve both masks, rebalancing leaves an offline processor, remapped migration retains its live handle and a whole x2APIC destination, and shutdown and disable update the records. `procfs_hosttest --irq-only` runs only its affinity rows, including comma-separated words, processor 63, zero's default, bad input, user-copy failure, EOF and the read-only effective mask. `vtd_hosttest --affinity-only` runs the real PCI and remapper paths on the Lenovo chip model: the cached old destination is invalidated, the live MSI handle survives, MSI-X moves, an offline destination falls back and shutdown aims at the remaining processor. These rows also run in each test's ordinary invocation.
